--- QUESTION 0001 ---
According to the functional requirements of a Safety Management System (SMS) described in the ISM Code, what are the three criteria that a company must establish and maintain procedures for regarding document and data control?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Valid documents are available at all relevant locations.
2. Changes to documents are reviewed and approved by authorized personnel.
3. Obsolete documents are properly removed.

--- QUESTION 0002 ---
What specific manual may be used to describe and implement a company's Safety Management System (SMS)?
--- ANSWER ---
The Safety Management Manual.

--- QUESTION 0003 ---
Under the "Resources and Personnel" requirements of the ISM Code, what three things must a company ensure regarding a ship's Master?
--- ANSWER ---
The company must ensure the Master is:

1. Properly qualified.
2. Fully conversant with the company SMS.
3. Given necessary support to safely perform duties.

--- QUESTION 0004 ---
What two requirements must a company fulfill regarding the manning of each ship under the "Resources and Personnel" section of the ISM Code?
--- ANSWER ---
The company must ensure that each ship is:

1. Manned with qualified, certificated, and medically fit seafarers.
2. Appropriately manned in order to encompass all aspects of safe operations.

--- QUESTION 0005 ---
What must a company establish to prepare for emergency actions under the "Emergency Preparedness" requirements of the ISM Code?
--- ANSWER ---
Programs for drills and exercises.

--- QUESTION 0006 ---
Under the ISM Code's guidelines for "Shipboard Operations," what types of documents should a company establish concerning the safety of personnel, the ship, and the protection of the environment?
--- ANSWER ---
Procedures, plans, and instructions, including checklists as appropriate.

--- QUESTION 0007 ---
When a non-conformity is reported during inspection and maintenance activities, what details should be included in the report if known?
--- ANSWER ---
The possible cause of the non-conformity.

--- QUESTION 0008 ---
What specific type of equipment and technical systems must a company identify under the "Inspection and Maintenance" guidelines of the ISM Code?
--- ANSWER ---
Equipment and technical systems the sudden operational failure of which may result in hazardous situations.

--- QUESTION 0009 ---
What specific actions must occur before a transfer of command between an outgoing and incoming Master is legally complete?
--- ANSWER ---

1. All Flag State required logbook entries have been signed by the outgoing and incoming Master.
2. Associated Regulatory and Company documentation has been signed by the outgoing and incoming Master.
3. The incoming Master must sign the Official Log Book (OLB).
4. The outgoing Master must sign off the Official Log Book (OLB).

--- QUESTION 0010 ---
What five items would you expect to find inside the Master's safe?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Controlled Drugs
2. Cash
3. Original Master keys
4. Ship Security Plan
5. Extra Loadline Certificates (if the vessel carries multiple Loadline Certificates)

--- QUESTION 0011 ---
Under Section 5.1 of the ISM Code, what are the five key areas regarding the Master's responsibility that a company should clearly define and document?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Implementing the safety and environmental-protection policy of the company.
2. Motivating the crew in the observation of that policy.
3. Issuing appropriate orders and instructions in a clear and simple manner.
4. Verifying that specified requirements are observed.
5. Periodically reviewing the safety management system and reporting its deficiencies to the shore-based management.

--- QUESTION 0012 ---
Under which specific piece of UK maritime legislation is a Master required to ensure the ship is in a seaworthy condition and render assistance in the event of a collision?
--- ANSWER ---
Merchant Shipping Act 1995 (MS 1995).

--- QUESTION 0013 ---
What are known as the "4 pillars" of the Maritime Industry?
--- ANSWER ---

1. SOLAS
2. MARPOL
3. STCW
4. MLC

--- QUESTION 0014 ---
Which maritime convention dictates rules concerning the Seafarer Employment Agreement (SEA), fresh water (FW) standards, and accommodation inspections?
--- ANSWER ---
Maritime Labour Convention (MLC).

--- QUESTION 0015 ---
Where does the concept of "Master's Discretion" originate, and what does it state regarding the Master's authority?
--- ANSWER ---
It originates from SOLAS Chapter V, Regulation 34-1. It states that the owner, charterer, operating company, or any other person shall not prevent or restrict the Master from taking or executing any decision which, in the Master's professional judgment, is necessary for safety of life at sea and protection of the marine environment.

--- QUESTION 0016 ---
Where does the mandate for a Master's "Overriding Authority" originate, and what must the company explicitly establish within the SMS regarding it?
--- ANSWER ---
It originates from ISM Code Part V (Section 5.2). The company must establish in the SMS that the Master has the overriding authority and responsibility to make decisions with respect to safety and pollution prevention, and to request the company's assistance as necessary.

--- QUESTION 0017 ---
Within a typical shipboard delegation of responsibility, which crew member is specifically responsible for the 2nd Officer's folder, charts, publications, and voyage planning?
--- ANSWER ---
The 2nd Officer.

--- QUESTION 0018 ---
Which crew member is primarily tasked with maintaining the minutes of safety meetings, tracking non-conformities/actions, and handling accident reporting?
--- ANSWER ---
The Safety Officer.

--- QUESTION 0019 ---
What information or documentation should be displayed or available on the notice boards near the gangway or ship's office as evidence of a safe shipboard culture?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Name & Contact for the DPA (Designated Person Ashore)
2. Health and Safety & Environmental Protection Policy
3. DMLC Part 2 / Complaints Procedure
4. Safety Officer / Safety Minutes
5. Risk assessments and PTW (Permit to Work) Procedures
6. IMO Signage and MARPOL Placards
7. Muster List contents

--- QUESTION 0020 ---
When arriving from outside the European Union (EU) with uncleared goods, what six specific forms must be completed to report to Customs?
--- ANSWER ---

1. IMO General Declaration
2. Ship's Stores Declaration
3. IMO Crew's Effects Declaration
4. Crew's List
5. Passenger List
6. Cargo Declaration

--- QUESTION 0021 ---
What are the two strict deadlines for making a vessel report to Customs after inbound arrival?
--- ANSWER ---
The report must be made not later than 3 hours after arrival at a berth, OR 24 hours after arrival within port limits if the vessel is not given a berth.

--- QUESTION 0022 ---
Within what timeframe relative to the Estimated Time of Arrival (ETA) must a Maritime Declaration of Health be reported?
--- ANSWER ---
It must be reported not more than 12 hours and not less than 4 hours before the vessel's ETA.

--- QUESTION 0023 ---
According to the instructions, what specific temperature threshold defines an illness "involving a temperature in excess of" a certain limit as grounds for suspecting an infectious disease in the absence of a surgeon?
--- ANSWER ---
A temperature in excess of 38°C (associated with diarrhea, rash, glandular swelling, or jaundice persisting more than 48 hours).

--- QUESTION 0024 ---
What details should be recorded in Box 13 of the IMO General Declaration if the cargo is a single bulk commodity?
--- ANSWER ---
A description of the cargo, where it can be expressed in a short phrase.

--- QUESTION 0025 ---
If a separate cargo declaration or manifest is submitted, what specific types of items must be detailed in Box 13 of the IMO General Declaration?
--- ANSWER ---
Details of unmanifested items, such as small packages, unaccompanied baggage in charge of the master, livestock, and birds.

--- QUESTION 0026 ---
Who is authorized to sign the IMO General Declaration form?
--- ANSWER ---
The form must be signed either by the master or a person authorized and acting on the master's authority.

--- QUESTION 0027 ---
What is required if a vessel is carrying firearms as part of its ship's stores?
--- ANSWER ---
The vessel should have a valid license or permit authorizing their carriage/possession from the appropriate country of origin.

--- QUESTION 0028 ---
How should the IMO Ship's Stores Declaration be organized if stores are kept in multiple areas of the vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
A separate declaration should be provided for each actual location within the vessel that is used to store ship's stores.

--- QUESTION 0029 ---
Who is responsible for checking the ship's stores and completing the Ship's Stores Declaration form, and who must ultimately sign it?
--- ANSWER ---
The person responsible for checking the ship's stores should complete the form, and the master must sign it.

--- QUESTION 0030 ---
Under what circumstance is an individual crew member required to complete the IMO Crew's Effects Declaration?
--- ANSWER ---
Each member of the crew is only required to complete the form in respect of any effects that are in excess of their traveller's allowance or subject to prohibitions or restrictions.

--- QUESTION 0031 ---
What must happen before excess goods declared on an IMO Crew's Effects Declaration can be legally landed in the UK?
--- ANSWER ---
Duty must be paid and any licensing requirements must be fulfilled regarding restricted goods.

--- QUESTION 0032 ---
What is the primary purpose of the IMO Crew List form?
--- ANSWER ---
It is used to provide information relating to the number and composition of the crew on arrival or departure.

--- QUESTION 0033 ---
The IMO Passenger List form is specifically mandatory for vessels that are certified to carry at least how many passengers?
--- ANSWER ---
Vessels that are certified to carry 12 passengers or more.

--- QUESTION 0034 ---
What commercial or administrative document normally consists of or satisfies the cargo declaration?
--- ANSWER ---
The manifest.

--- QUESTION 0035 ---
What specific details must the manifest or commercial documents give for each consignment in a Cargo Declaration?
--- ANSWER ---

1. The maritime transport document reference (e.g., bill of lading number)
2. The container identification/vehicle registration number
3. The number, kind, marks, and numbers of the packages
4. The description and gross weight/volume of the goods
5. The port or place where the goods were loaded onto the vessel
6. The original port or place of shipment for goods on a through maritime transport document

--- QUESTION 0036 ---
What specific model form produced by the International Maritime Organization (IMO) will be accepted for a cargo declaration if there is no manifest available?
--- ANSWER ---
The model form IMO FAL 2.

--- QUESTION 0037 ---
What exact text must the master or authorized person sign on the Cargo Declaration to certify the accuracy of the cargo details?
--- ANSWER ---
"I declare to the best of my knowledge, that these cargo details are correct for the cargo carried in [name of vessel] which are contained in this/these document(s) consisting of [number] page(s)."

--- QUESTION 0038 ---
As a Senior Officer, who does the Chief Mate assist in implementing the Company, Safety, and Environmental Policy?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master

--- QUESTION 0039 ---
What system must the Chief Mate ensure is understood and underpins all working activity on board?
--- ANSWER ---
The Safety Management System (SMS)

--- QUESTION 0040 ---
To ensure the vessel is at all times in what condition must a systematic approach to work be taken?
--- ANSWER ---
A seaworthy condition

--- QUESTION 0041 ---
What are the four specific examples of systems of work or plans that a Chief Mate must follow as listed in the text?
--- ANSWER ---
Risk assessment, load & lashing plans, ballast plan, and Fresh Water Management Plan.

--- QUESTION 0042 ---
What criteria must the Chief Mate calculate, and what information must they prepare regarding the ship's condition for departure and arrival?
--- ANSWER ---
They must calculate ship stability criteria and prepare departure and arrival draught information.

--- QUESTION 0043 ---
The Chief Mate must ensure the vessel meets the certification requirements of which state?
--- ANSWER ---
The flag state

--- QUESTION 0044 ---
According to what two guidelines or requirements must a Chief Mate establish a planned maintenance system?
--- ANSWER ---
Manufacturers' requirements and International Conventions.

--- QUESTION 0045 ---
What must the Chief Mate establish for the crew to ensure continuous proficiency and readiness?
--- ANSWER ---
Regular training

--- QUESTION 0046 ---
What are the main functional roles and areas of responsibility assigned to the Chief Mate on board as listed in the text?
--- ANSWER ---
Cargo Load/Discharge, Safety Officer, Vessel Stability Officer, Security Officer, Crew Management, Purchasing, Planned Maintenance for Deck, Environmental Officer, Compliance, and Safe Navigational Watch.

--- QUESTION 0047 ---
What four sources or documents outline where a Chief Mate would find their specific onboard responsibilities?
--- ANSWER ---
Master's Standing Orders, Safety Management System, UK Legislation, and International Conventions.

--- QUESTION 0048 ---
What is a temporary allowance granted in writing by an Administrator that permits a ship to proceed without being in full compliance with a specific Convention requirement?
--- ANSWER ---
A Dispensation

--- QUESTION 0049 ---
What type of release from statutory convention requirements is permanent, issued on a case-by-case basis, and linked directly to an associated statutory certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
An Exemption

--- QUESTION 0050 ---
For how long is an Exemption Certificate valid?
--- ANSWER ---
It is valid for the duration of the validity of its associated statutory certificate.

--- QUESTION 0051 ---
What is meant by "ambulatory referencing" in domestic maritime legislation?
--- ANSWER ---
It is a reference to an international instrument (such as an IMO Convention) which is interpreted as a reference to that instrument as modified from time to time, meaning future changes take effect in domestic law without needing to pass through parliament.

--- QUESTION 0052 ---
What UK Marine Guidance Note (MGN) outlines the Harmonised System of Survey and Certification (HSSC)?
--- ANSWER ---
MSN 1751

--- QUESTION 0053 ---
What are the primary aims and benefits of the Harmonised System of Surveys and Certification (HSSC)?
--- ANSWER ---
It aims to simplify the survey and certification process by standardizing validity periods and survey intervals for the nine main convention certificates, thereby reducing paperwork, surveyor numbers, and overall costs.

--- QUESTION 0054 ---
Under the Harmonised System of Surveys and Certification (HSSC), how many distinct types of surveys are there?
--- ANSWER ---
Seven (7) types of surveys

--- QUESTION 0055 ---
Which type of survey involves a complete examination of all items relating to a certificate before a ship is put into service?
--- ANSWER ---
Initial Survey

--- QUESTION 0056 ---
What distinguishes a Renewal Survey from a Periodical Survey?
--- ANSWER ---
While both require a complete examination of all items relating to a certificate, a Renewal Survey leads to the issuance of a brand-new certificate.

--- QUESTION 0057 ---
Which type of survey is defined as a general inspection of items relating to a certificate to ensure they have been maintained and remain fit for service?
--- ANSWER ---
Annual Survey

--- QUESTION 0058 ---
What type of survey is conducted when important repairs or renewals are made to a vessel outside of the regular schedule?
--- ANSWER ---
Additional Survey

--- QUESTION 0059 ---
What is a key difference between a Survey and a Vessel Inspection regarding their scope and regularity?
--- ANSWER ---
A Survey has a fixed scope covering specific items to issue statutory certification and is scheduled, whereas a Vessel Inspection is usually unscheduled, has a variable scope, and looks at the overall safety or pollution prevention standards of the vessel.

--- QUESTION 0060 ---
Under the Alternative Compliance Safety System (ACSS), which regulatory audits and certifications remain fully under the direct issuance of the MCA rather than being delegated to a Recognized Organization (RO)?
--- ANSWER ---
ISM, ISPS, and MLC audits and certificates

--- QUESTION 0061 ---
What specific certificate does the MCA issue to indicate that a vessel is officially enrolled on the Alternative Compliance Safety System (ACSS) scheme?
--- ANSWER ---
Certificate of Inspection (MSF 1115)

--- QUESTION 0062 ---
To be eligible for the Alternative Compliance Safety System (ACSS), a UK-registered vessel must not have been detained within how many months?
--- ANSWER ---
Within the previous 36 months

--- QUESTION 0063 ---
What is the maximum number of deficiencies a vessel can have in any single Port State Control (PSC) inspection over the last 12 months to qualify for the ACSS?
--- ANSWER ---
No more than 5 deficiencies

--- QUESTION 0064 ---
What are the six UK-authorized Recognized Organizations (ROs) listed for the ACSS eligibility criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
ABS, BV, DNV, LR, NK, and RINA

--- QUESTION 0065 ---
Under the Enhanced Authorisation Safety System (EASS), which specific certificate is the only exception that the Recognized Organization (RO) cannot issue?
--- ANSWER ---
The ISM Document of Compliance (DOC)

--- QUESTION 0066 ---
How often does the flexible window reset for a "Standard" risk ship under the Enhanced Authorisation Safety System (EASS)?
--- ANSWER ---
Every 3 years (resets after every Flag State Survey)

--- QUESTION 0067 ---
What is the standard frequency and window reset period for a vessel classified as "High" risk under the EASS framework?
--- ANSWER ---
6 months (with surveys within 6 weeks following an MCA request)

--- QUESTION 0068 ---
According to MGN 546, what is the minimum frequency for bottom inspections of cargo ships within any five-year period?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum of two inspections at intervals not exceeding 36 months.

--- QUESTION 0069 ---
What is the standard maximum age limit for a ship to normally be eligible for alternative In-Water Surveys (IWS) instead of out-of-water bottom inspections?
--- ANSWER ---
Normally 15 years of age

--- QUESTION 0070 ---
How often must passenger ships undergo a bottom inspection, and how many must be out of the water in a five-year period?
--- ANSWER ---
Passenger ships require a bottom inspection every year; at least two of these inspections in any five-year period must be conducted out of the water.

--- QUESTION 0071 ---
What framework is established under MGN 672 to allow qualifying vessels to dry-dock once every 7.5 years (90 months) instead of twice in 5 years?
--- ANSWER ---
Extended Dry-Docking (EDD)

--- QUESTION 0072 ---
What is the maximum age a ship can reach from its date of build to remain eligible for the Extended Dry-Docking (EDD) scheme?
--- ANSWER ---
Up to 15 years of age (and it must be implemented before the ship reaches 10 years of age).

--- QUESTION 0073 ---
Name four types of cargo or specialized vessels that are eligible for consideration under the Extended Dry-Docking (EDD) framework.
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: Dedicated container carriers, LNG Tankers, LPG carriers, Ro-Ro cargo ships, Refrigerated cargo ships, Dedicated wood chip carriers, Dedicated cement carriers, Livestock carriers, Deck cargo ships, or General cargo ships of double-side skin construction.

--- QUESTION 0074 ---
Which types of vessels or propulsion characteristics are explicitly excluded from being considered for Extended Dry-Docking (EDD)?
--- ANSWER ---
Vessels under the Enhanced Survey Programme (ESP), feeder/short-sea vessels, vessels 10+ years old at implementation, vessels with propulsion thrusters, ice notation, high-speed craft, propeller connections with a keyed taper, and all passenger ships.

--- QUESTION 0075 ---
What are Class Renewal or Special Surveys, and at what intervals are they typically conducted?
--- ANSWER ---
They are extensive examinations (typically out of water) carried out at five-year intervals to verify the structural integrity, main and essential auxiliary machinery, and systems of a vessel.

--- QUESTION 0076 ---
What international regulation enforces additional safety and enhanced survey measures for bulk carriers and tankers?
--- ANSWER ---
SOLAS Chapter XII (enacted in the UK via the Merchant Shipping Regulations 2022 / SI 2022/1218).

--- QUESTION 0077 ---
For Enhanced Survey Programme (ESP) ships, at what age is an approved survey programme mandatory on board upon commencing a special or intermediate survey?
--- ANSWER ---
After 10 years of age

--- QUESTION 0078 ---
Recent MSC.525(106) amendments to the ESP code updated the term used to describe poor survey conditions. What is the new phrase used instead of "Poor"?
--- ANSWER ---
"Less than Good"

--- QUESTION 0079 ---
If a bulk carrier carries cargo with a density between 1250–1780 kg/m³, how must the density of that cargo be declared and verified?
--- ANSWER ---
The shipper must declare the density, and it must be verified by an accredited testing organization.

--- QUESTION 0080 ---
What are the three core responsibilities of a ship's Owner and Master regarding surveys and certification?
--- ANSWER ---

Maintain the ship and equipment to ensure it can safely proceed to sea.

Inform the Certifying Authority of structural, machinery, or equipment changes.

Report accidents or defects that affect the ship's safety or LSA equipment.

--- QUESTION 0081 ---
What is the difference between a Statutory Certificate and a Certificate of Class?
--- ANSWER ---
A Statutory Certificate is issued by or on behalf of the Flag State to prove compliance with international conventions (SOLAS, MARPOL, etc.). A Certificate of Class is issued by a classification society as evidence that the vessel is built and maintained to the society's specific rules.

--- QUESTION 0082 ---
What does a "Condition of Class" mean when issued by a classification society?
--- ANSWER ---
It refers to mandatory requirements that specific measures, repairs, or surveys must be carried out within a specified time limit for the vessel to retain its classification.

--- QUESTION 0083 ---
What are the four sequential stages outlined for the Ship Survey/Audit Process?
--- ANSWER ---
Plan, Prepare, Conduct, and Review

--- QUESTION 0084 ---
According to SOLAS Regulation V/23.2.4, what marking requirement must be met for pilot ladders used for pilot transfer?
--- ANSWER ---
All pilot ladders used for pilot transfer must be clearly identified with tags or other permanent markings to enable identification for the purposes of survey, inspection, and record keeping.

--- QUESTION 0085 ---
What specific information regarding an identified pilot ladder must be recorded on board the ship?
--- ANSWER ---
A record must be kept on board as to the date the identified ladder is placed into service and any repairs effected.

--- QUESTION 0086 ---
Under SOLAS Regulations V/26 and V/28.1, what types of records must be maintained by all ships engaged on international voyages if they are not kept in the ship's logbook?
--- ANSWER ---
A record of navigational activities and incidents, including drills and pre-departure tests, maintained in another form approved by the Administration.

--- QUESTION 0087 ---
For ships with ro-ro spaces, when must all securing of cargoes, cargo units, and cargo transport units be completed in accordance with the Cargo Securing Manual?
--- ANSWER ---
All cargo securing must be completed before the ship leaves the berth.

--- QUESTION 0088 ---
Which types of cargoes are excluded from the requirement to be loaded, stowed, and secured in accordance with an approved Cargo Securing Manual?
--- ANSWER ---
Solid and liquid bulk cargoes.

--- QUESTION 0089 ---
Under SOLAS Regulation VI/5-1, when must Material Safety Data Sheets (MSDS) be provided to ships carrying oil or oil fuel in bulk?
--- ANSWER ---
Prior to the loading of such oil as cargo in bulk or the bunkering of oil fuel.

--- QUESTION 0090 ---
What must the Administration or a recognized organization verify before issuing a Safety Management Certificate (SMC) to a ship?
--- ANSWER ---
They must verify that the company and its shipboard management operate in accordance with the approved safety management system.

--- QUESTION 0091 ---
Where must a copy of the Document of Compliance (DOC), issued under the ISM Code to a company, be kept?
--- ANSWER ---
A copy of the Document of Compliance must be kept on board the ship.

--- QUESTION 0092 ---
What is the primary function of the Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR) required under SOLAS Regulation XI-1/5?
--- ANSWER ---
It provides an onboard record of the history of the ship with respect to the information recorded therein.

--- QUESTION 0093 ---
How many security levels must a Ship Security Plan make provisions for under the ISPS Code?
--- ANSWER ---
The plan must make provisions for three security levels.

--- QUESTION 0094 ---
According to the ISPS Code, what is the minimum requirement for logging communications related to the direct security of the ship?
--- ANSWER ---
Records must be kept of specific threats to the ship or to port facilities the ship is or has been in.

--- QUESTION 0095 ---
Which three MARPOL Annexes allow an Administration to grant an exemption certificate from survey and certification requirements to an unmanned non-self-propelled (UNSP) barge?
--- ANSWER ---
MARPOL Annex I, Annex IV, and Annex VI.

--- QUESTION 0096 ---
An International Oil Pollution Prevention (IOPP) Certificate is mandatory for which categories of ships engaged in international voyages?
--- ANSWER ---
Any oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage (GT) and above, and any other ship of 400 gross tonnage (GT) and above.

--- QUESTION 0097 ---
What form supplements the International Oil Pollution Prevention Certificate for oil tankers?
--- ANSWER ---
A Record of Construction and Equipment for Oil Tankers (Form B).

--- QUESTION 0098 ---
What separate record books must be carried by an oil tanker of 150 gross tonnage and above under MARPOL Annex I?
--- ANSWER ---
An Oil Record Book Part I (Machinery space operations) and an Oil Record Book Part II (Cargo/ballast operations).

--- QUESTION 0099 ---
Under MARPOL Annex IV, what condition must be met for a ship to discharge untreated sewage from a holding tank into the sea?
--- ANSWER ---
The ship must be outside passenger ship special areas (if a passenger ship) or in any area (if non-passenger), and the sewage must be discharged at a moderate rate approved by the Administration based on standards developed by the Organization.

--- QUESTION 0100 ---
What is the minimum gross tonnage threshold for a ship to be required to carry a Garbage Management Plan under MARPOL Annex V?
--- ANSWER ---
100 gross tonnage and above (or any ship certified to carry 15 persons or more).

--- QUESTION 0101 ---
Which document must be carried on board alongside an Exhaust Gas Cleaning (EGC) system unit fitted as an alternative compliance method for SOx emissions?
--- ANSWER ---
A SOx Emission Compliance Certificate together with the EGC System Technical Manual.

--- QUESTION 0102 ---
What is the minimum gross tonnage required for a ship to maintain a ship-specific Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan (SEEMP)?
--- ANSWER ---
400 gross tonnage and above.

--- QUESTION 0103 ---
For ships of 5,000 gross tonnage and above, what specific methodology must be included in the SEEMP prior to the first reporting period?
--- ANSWER ---
A description of the methodology that will be used to collect data required by regulation 27.1 of MARPOL Annex VI and the processes used to report the data to the ship's Administration.

--- QUESTION 0104 ---
What three specific operational parameters must be calculated and planned within the SEEMP on or before 1 January 2023 for ships of 5,000 gross tonnage and above?
--- ANSWER ---
The methodology to calculate the attained annual CII, the required annual operational CII, an implementation plan with regard to the required annual operational CII, and a procedure for self-evaluation and improvement.

--- QUESTION 0105 ---
What corrective action framework is triggered if a ship is rated as D for three consecutive years or rated as E under MARPOL Annex VI carbon intensity regulations?
--- ANSWER ---
The SEEMP must be reviewed to include a plan of corrective actions to achieve the required annual operational CII.

--- QUESTION 0106 ---
Within how many months from the beginning of the calendar year must the Statement of Compliance related to fuel oil consumption reporting and operational carbon intensity rating be issued?
--- ANSWER ---
No later than 5 months from the beginning of the calendar year.

--- QUESTION 0107 ---
Which vessels with rechargeable systems containing ozone-depleting substances are required to maintain an ozone-depleting substances record book?
--- ANSWER ---
Each ship subject to MARPOL Annex VI, regulation 6.1.

--- QUESTION 0108 ---
What operational transitions must be logged in a logbook or electronic record book for marine diesel engines certified to Tier II and Tier III standards?
--- ANSWER ---
The tier and on/off status of the engines, together with the date, time, and position of the ship, at entry into and exit from a NOx Tier III emission control area (or when status changes within such an area).

--- QUESTION 0109 ---
What operational records must be completed prior to entering an emission control area for a ship using separate fuel oils to comply with sulfur limits?
--- ANSWER ---
The volume of low-sulfur fuel oils in each tank, as well as the date, time, and position of the ship when the fuel oil changeover operation is completed.

--- QUESTION 0110 ---
How long must a bunker delivery note and its representative fuel sample be kept on board?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be kept on board in accordance with the requirements of MARPOL Annex VI, regulations 18.6 and 18.8.1.

--- QUESTION 0111 ---
What two primary data files are required to document the calculation processes for verification of a ship's energy design and index under MARPOL Annex VI regulations 22 and 23?
--- ANSWER ---
The EEDI Technical File and the EEXI Technical File.

--- QUESTION 0112 ---
Who is responsible for preparing the Technical File that must accompany a marine diesel engine throughout its life on board?
--- ANSWER ---
The applicant for engine certification, which must then be approved by the Administration.

--- QUESTION 0113 ---
Where must adjustments or modifications made to an engine after its pre-certification be fully recorded if the Engine Parameter Check method is used?
--- ANSWER ---
In the engine's record book or electronic record book of engine parameters.

--- QUESTION 0114 ---
In what format must certificates for masters, officers, or ratings be kept on board the ship on which the holder is serving?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be kept available in their original form.

--- QUESTION 0115 ---
What is the minimum gross tonnage required for a ship to carry an International Anti-fouling System Certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
400 GT and above, when engaged in international voyages (excluding fixed/floating platforms, FSUs, and FPSOs).

--- QUESTION 0116 ---
What document must be signed and carried by ships of 24 meters or more in length but less than 400 GT regarding their hull coatings?
--- ANSWER ---
A Declaration on Anti-fouling System, accompanied by appropriate documentation like a receipt or contractor invoice.

--- QUESTION 0117 ---
How long must ballast water record book entries be maintained on board the ship before being transferred to the Company's control?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be maintained on board for a minimum period of two years after the last entry, and thereafter under the Company's control for a minimum of three years.

--- QUESTION 0118 ---
What is the gross tonnage threshold above which a ship must carry a certificate of insurance or financial security for civil liability regarding bunker oil pollution damage?
--- ANSWER ---
Greater than 1,000 gross tonnage.

--- QUESTION 0119 ---
What is the minimum gross tonnage threshold for a ship to carry a certificate of insurance or financial security in respect of liability for the removal of wrecks?
--- ANSWER ---
300 gross tonnage and above.

--- QUESTION 0120 ---
What layout requirement is mandated on the navigation bridge of all passenger ships for emergency management?
--- ANSWER ---
A decision support system for masters.


--- QUESTION 0121 ---
When was the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC) adopted, and when did it enter into force?
--- ANSWER ---
It was adopted in February 2006 and came into force in August 2013.

--- QUESTION 0122 ---
How many titles is the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC) divided into?
--- ANSWER ---
5 titles.

--- QUESTION 0123 ---
What are the descriptions of Title 1 and Title 2 of the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC)?
--- ANSWER ---
Title 1 covers the minimum requirements for seafarers to work on a ship, and Title 2 covers the conditions of employment.

--- QUESTION 0124 ---
What are the descriptions of Title 3, Title 4, and Title 5 of the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC)?
--- ANSWER ---
Title 3 covers accommodation, recreational facilities, food, and catering; Title 4 covers health protection, medical care, welfare, and social security protection; and Title 5 covers compliance and enforcement.

--- QUESTION 0125 ---
Under the MLC, how do Part A Standards and Part B Guidelines differ in terms of enforceability?
--- ANSWER ---
Part A Standards are mandatory, whereas Part B Guidelines contain non-mandatory recommendations.

--- QUESTION 0126 ---
What criteria dictate which ships must carry a Maritime Labour Certificate issued by the flag State?
--- ANSWER ---
Any ship over 500 GT operating in international waters or between ports of different countries.

--- QUESTION 0127 ---
What is the maximum validity period of a Maritime Labour Certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
5 years.

--- QUESTION 0128 ---
Within what timeframe must the intermediate inspection for a Maritime Labour Certificate be completed?
--- ANSWER ---
Between the second and third anniversary dates of the certificate.

--- QUESTION 0129 ---
What mandatory document must be attached to the Maritime Labour Certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
The Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance (DMLC).

--- QUESTION 0130 ---
What is the difference between Part I and Part II of the Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance (DMLC)?
--- ANSWER ---
Part I lists how the Flag State's regulations comply with the Convention, while Part II is drawn up by the shipowner to outline the measures taken on board to comply with those Part I requirements.

--- QUESTION 0131 ---
Whose date must match the DMLC date displayed on the Maritime Labour Certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
The date of Part II of the DMLC.

--- QUESTION 0132 ---
How does the MLC define a "shipowner" if the legal owner has transferred operational responsibility?
--- ANSWER ---
A shipowner means the owner or any other organization or person (such as the manager, agent, or bareboat charterer) who has assumed operational responsibility and agreed to take over the duties and responsibilities imposed on shipowners by the Convention.

--- QUESTION 0133 ---
What two specific types of financial security or insurance certifications are associated with the MLC?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Financial Security Insurance
2. Repatriation Insurance

--- QUESTION 0134 ---
According to the 2020 Regulations, what happens to a Seafarer's Employment Agreement (SEA) if the seafarer is held captive due to piracy or armed robbery?
--- ANSWER ---
The SEA cannot expire or be terminated; it remains in effect until the seafarer has been repatriated or has died.

--- QUESTION 0135 ---
What are the requirements regarding seafarer wages during a period of captivity due to piracy or armed robbery?
--- ANSWER ---
Wages must continue to be paid in accordance with the Seafarer's Employment Agreement (SEA).

--- QUESTION 0136 ---
Name at least five core particulars that must be contained within a Seafarer's Employment Agreement (SEA).
--- ANSWER ---
Any five of the following: seafarer's full name/DOB/birthplace; shipowner's name and address; place and date the agreement is entered into; capacity of employment; amount or formula for wages; amount or formula for paid annual leave; termination conditions and notice periods; health and social security protection benefits; entitlement to repatriation; or reference to a collective bargaining agreement.

--- QUESTION 0137 ---
What are the night work restrictions for young persons under the age of 18 under the MLC?
--- ANSWER ---
They may not work at night (unless part of recognized training), which is defined as a period of at least nine hours, starting no later than midnight and ending no earlier than 5 am.

--- QUESTION 0138 ---
What is the minimum consecutive rest period required for a young person in any 24-hour period under the MLC?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum of 12 consecutive hours.

--- QUESTION 0139 ---
What is the minimum weekly rest requirement for a young person under the MLC?
--- ANSWER ---
At least 2 days of rest in every week.

--- QUESTION 0140 ---
What rest break is a young person entitled to if their daily working time exceeds four and a half hours?
--- ANSWER ---
A rest break of at least 30 minutes, which should be consecutive if possible.

--- QUESTION 0141 ---
Under UK law, what is the maximum continuous duration of service permitted on board a ship for a seafarer?
--- ANSWER ---
A maximum of 11 months.

--- QUESTION 0142 ---
How is the minimum annual paid leave entitlement calculated per month of employment under the MLC?
--- ANSWER ---
On the basis of a minimum of 2.5 days per month of employment.

--- QUESTION 0143 ---
According to the December 2024 MLC amendments, what recreational communication facilities must be available to seafarers at a reasonable cost?
--- ANSWER ---
Telephone and internet access.

--- QUESTION 0144 ---
What are Flag States required to do regarding seafarer deaths under the December 2024 MLC amendments?
--- ANSWER ---
They must investigate and record deaths, and report them annually to the International Labour Organization (ILO).

--- QUESTION 0145 ---
What requirement for personal protective equipment (PPE) was introduced in the December 2024 MLC amendments?
--- ANSWER ---
PPE must be appropriately sized for the seafarers.

--- QUESTION 0146 ---
What is the maximum validity period of a medical certificate for a young person?
--- ANSWER ---
1 year.

--- QUESTION 0147 ---
What specific protections must be detailed in an Onboard Complaints Procedure document?
--- ANSWER ---
The right of the seafarer to be accompanied or represented during the procedure, and safeguards against the possibility of victimizing seafarers for filing complaints.

--- QUESTION 0148 ---
Which ships are mandatory required to have a qualified medical doctor on board under MLC rules?
--- ANSWER ---
Ships engaged on international voyages ordinarily exceeding 3 days and carrying more than 100 people.

--- QUESTION 0149 ---
What is a Collective Bargaining Agreement (CBA) and how does it relate to the SEA?
--- ANSWER ---
It is an agreement between an employer and a trade union, and it must be attached to the Seafarer's Employment Agreement (SEA) if applicable.

--- QUESTION 0150 ---
What are the ISM implications for a Safety Management Certificate (SMC) and audit requirements if a ship has been laid up in hot mode for less than 3 months?
--- ANSWER ---
The SMC remains valid as in operation, and an audit is not required.

--- QUESTION 0151 ---
What are the ISM implications for a Safety Management Certificate (SMC) and audit requirements if a ship has been laid up in hot mode for between 3 and 12 months?
--- ANSWER ---
The SMC must be revalidated, and an additional audit with the scope of an initial audit is required.

--- QUESTION 0152 ---
What are the ISM implications for a Safety Management Certificate (SMC) and audit requirements if a ship has been laid up in hot mode for more than 12 months?
--- ANSWER ---
The SMC becomes invalid, and an interim audit is required.

--- QUESTION 0153 ---
What are the ISM implications for a Safety Management Certificate (SMC) and audit requirements if a ship has been laid up in cold mode for less than 3 months?
--- ANSWER ---
The SMC must be revalidated, and an additional audit with the scope of an initial audit is required.

--- QUESTION 0154 ---
What are the ISM implications for a Safety Management Certificate (SMC) and audit requirements if a ship has been laid up in cold mode for more than 3 months?
--- ANSWER ---
The SMC becomes invalid, and an interim audit is required.

--- QUESTION 0155 ---
What are the four phases of the MCA auditing process outlined in MSIS 37?
--- ANSWER ---
Plan, Prepare, Conduct, and Review.

--- QUESTION 0156 ---
What are the three categories of ISM audit findings?
--- ANSWER ---
Observation, Non-conformity, and Major Non-conformity.

--- QUESTION 0157 ---
How is an "Observation" defined during an ISM safety management audit?
--- ANSWER ---
A statement of fact made during an audit and substantiated by objective evidence, for which the company or ship is not liable to provide evidence of corrective action.

--- QUESTION 0158 ---
What is an ISM "Non-conformity" and what is its standard close-out timeframe?
--- ANSWER ---
An observed situation where objective evidence indicates the non-fulfilment of a specified requirement of the ISM Code; it should normally be closed out within three months of the audit.

--- QUESTION 0159 ---
What constitutes a "Major Non-conformity" under the ISM Code?
--- ANSWER ---
An identifiable deviation that poses a serious threat to the safety of personnel, the ship, or the environment that requires immediate corrective action, or the lack of effective and systematic implementation of an ISM Code requirement.

--- QUESTION 0160 ---
What action must be taken during a ship audit when a major non-conformity is found to permit the vessel to sail?
--- ANSWER ---
The major non-conformity must be downgraded to a standard non-conformity.

--- QUESTION 0161 ---
What follow-up requirement must be satisfied within 3 months of downgrading a major non-conformity?
--- ANSWER ---
An additional audit must be carried out, timed to verify the effectiveness of the corrective action taken.

--- QUESTION 0162 ---
According to MSN 1868 Amendment 1, who is responsible for the overall safety of the ship and for ensuring that watchkeeping arrangements are adequate for maintaining safe navigational watches at all times?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master

--- QUESTION 0163 ---
According to UK requirements, during what periods or conditions does the UK not consider it safe for the officer in charge of the navigational watch to act as the sole look-out?
--- ANSWER ---
During periods of darkness or restricted visibility

--- QUESTION 0164 ---
Which administration or agency is responsible for issuing a Safe Manning Document (SMD) for UK vessels?
--- ANSWER ---
The Maritime and Coastguard Agency (MCA)

--- QUESTION 0165 ---
According to the Merchant Shipping Regulations, what is the minimum gross tonnage (GT) threshold above which all United Kingdom seagoing vessels must hold a Safe Manning Document?
--- ANSWER ---
500 GT or more

--- QUESTION 0166 ---
Whose responsibility is it to ensure that a ship is safely and adequately manned?
--- ANSWER ---
The owner or operator

--- QUESTION 0167 ---
Under the provisions of which international convention and regulation is the Minimum Safe Manning Document issued?
--- ANSWER ---
Regulation V/14 of the International Convention for the Safety of Life at Sea, 1974 (SOLAS), as amended

--- QUESTION 0168 ---
When assessing tasks to determine the minimum safe manning level, what four specific attributes of a task must be evaluated?
--- ANSWER ---
Duration, Frequency, Competence, and Importance

--- QUESTION 0169 ---
When evaluating a task to determine safe manning, how is "Duration" measured?
--- ANSWER ---
As a measurement of total man-hours versus the actual duration taken for task completion

--- QUESTION 0170 ---
Every seafarer must be provided with a minimum total number of hours of rest in any 24-hour period. What is this minimum limit?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 10 hours

--- QUESTION 0171 ---
The required 10 hours of rest in any 24-hour period may be divided into no more than two separate periods. What is the minimum length required for one of these periods?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 6 hours

--- QUESTION 0172 ---
What is the maximum allowed interval between consecutive periods of rest for a seafarer?
--- ANSWER ---
It shall not exceed 14 hours

--- QUESTION 0173 ---
What is the minimum mandatory number of hours of rest a seafarer must receive in any 7-day period?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 77 hours

--- QUESTION 0174 ---
According to the Masters Guide to UK Flag, what are three exceptions where the standard hours of work and rest regulations may be overridden?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Emergencies and situations likely to become emergencies unless action is taken.
2. Essential work on board which cannot be delayed for safety or environmental protection reasons.
3. Factors beyond the control of the Master or the operator other than commercial needs.

--- QUESTION 0175 ---
How long must the records of the consultation process for a vessel's safe manning proposal be retained by the owner or operator?
--- ANSWER ---
5 years

--- QUESTION 0176 ---
Once endorsed, how long must records of seafarers' daily hours of rest be retained?
--- ANSWER ---
For at least one year

--- QUESTION 0177 ---
If an exceptional circumstance (such as injury or death) requires a seafarer to be removed from a vessel and a replacement cannot join in time, what document must the Master request from the MCA to sail legally in non-compliance with the Safe Manning Document?
--- ANSWER ---
An Exemption Certificate

--- QUESTION 0178 ---
Even if a vessel is granted an Exemption Certificate to sail in non-compliance with its Safe Manning Document, what regulations must the Master ensure the remaining crew continue to follow?
--- ANSWER ---
The Hours of Work Regulations

--- QUESTION 0179 ---
Which UK legislative act contains sections 58 and 59, which provide the framework for statutory rules governing the Code of Conduct, Safety Management Systems, and disciplinary procedures for the Merchant Navy?
--- ANSWER ---
Merchant Shipping Act 1995

--- QUESTION 0180 ---
What are the four levels of formal and informal disciplinary procedures recognized onboard under the Code of Conduct?
--- ANSWER ---
Informal Warning, Formal Warning, Written Warning, and Final Written Warning

--- QUESTION 0181 ---
What is the maximum disciplinary penalty a Petty Officer can administer onboard a ship?
--- ANSWER ---
An Informal Warning

--- QUESTION 0182 ---
Which specific officer rank holds the authority to issue a "Dismissal from ship" penalty as a disciplinary measure?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master

--- QUESTION 0183 ---
What are the three possible courses of action available to a manager after holding an informal meeting to hear a seafarer's explanation regarding a breach of conduct?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Determine there is no case to answer and take no further action.
2. Issue an Informal Warning.
3. Initiate formal disciplinary action.

--- QUESTION 0184 ---
What maximum disciplinary sanction may be applied if an act of gross misconduct has been established to the satisfaction of the Master?
--- ANSWER ---
Dismissal from the ship (either immediately or at the end of the voyage) and dismissal from employment

--- QUESTION 0185 ---
When a seafarer is given a disciplinary warning higher than an informal warning, what additional information must they be advised of?
--- ANSWER ---
The likely consequences of further breaches of discipline

--- QUESTION 0186 ---
During a formal disciplinary action scenario, what document or entries must the seafarer be provided a copy of following the decision?
--- ANSWER ---
A copy of any entries made in the ship's Official Log Book

--- QUESTION 0187 ---
Under the provisions of the Merchant Shipping Act, what are the possible consequences for a crew member who refuses a direct order to assist in an emergency situation?
--- ANSWER ---
It is treated as a serious breach of discipline and may result in criminal prosecution.

--- QUESTION 0188 ---
According to the Merchant Shipping (Official Log Book) Regulations 1981, which types of United Kingdom vessels are exempt from the requirement to carry and keep an Official Log Book (OLB)?
--- ANSWER ---
Fishing vessels, ships less than 25 GT, and pleasure vessels.

--- QUESTION 0189 ---
What are the two actions a Master can take in an additional entry to correct an incorrect entry found in the Official Log Book (OLB)?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master can either amend or cancel the incorrect entry.

--- QUESTION 0190 ---
Apart from the Master, who is authorized to amend an Official Log Book entry after the book has been sent to the Registry of Shipping?
--- ANSWER ---
A superintendent or Proper Officer.

--- QUESTION 0191 ---
When does an Official Log Book (OLB) officially expire?
--- ANSWER ---
It expires 6 months after its first entry, or when all persons have been signed off from the Crew Agreement, whichever comes first.

--- QUESTION 0192 ---
To which entities or individuals must an expired Official Log Book be returned for storage?
--- ANSWER ---
The Registry of Shipping and Seamen, an appropriate Superintendent, or a Proper Officer.

--- QUESTION 0193 ---
Under Section 44 of the Merchant Shipping Act 1995, what is the minimum number of seamen who must complain to the Master about bad quality, unfitness, or a deficiency in quantity of provisions or water to mandate an investigation?
--- ANSWER ---
Three or more seamen.

--- QUESTION 0194 ---
What does the acronym HACCP stand for?
--- ANSWER ---
Hazard Analysis and Critical Control Points.

--- QUESTION 0195 ---
What is considered the most important aspect of the HACCP system regarding how it controls food safety hazards compared to an inspection system?
--- ANSWER ---
It is a preventative system rather than an inspection system.

--- QUESTION 0196 ---
Within the HACCP system, what do the components "HA" and "CCP" systematically represent?
--- ANSWER ---
"HA" represents Hazard Analysis (identifying the where and how of hazards), and "CCP" represents Critical Control Points (providing the control of the process and proof of that control).

--- QUESTION 0197 ---
According to MSN 1845, how frequently should fresh water tanks be thoroughly pumped out and, where necessary, hosed prior to refilling?
--- ANSWER ---
6 monthly.

--- QUESTION 0198 ---
According to MSN 1845 guidelines for 12-monthly maintenance, what concentration of free chlorine should fresh water tanks be chlorinated to when refilled with clean freshwater?
--- ANSWER ---
0.2 ppm (parts per million).

--- QUESTION 20199 ---
During a re-fit or drydock, for how many hours must the fresh water system be charged with super-chlorinated freshwater at a concentration of 50 ppm?
--- ANSWER ---
12 hours.

--- QUESTION 0200 ---
What is the maximum time interval permitted between pressure tests carried out on all fresh water tanks?
--- ANSWER ---
An interval not greater than 5 years.

--- QUESTION 0201 ---
How frequently should fresh water distribution system filters be cleaned and changed under MSN 1845?
--- ANSWER ---
Monthly, or according to manufacturers' instructions.

--- QUESTION 0202 ---
Before draining calorifiers during periodic inspection, to what temperature should they be raised, and for how long, to ensure the destruction of colonized bacteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Raised to 70°C for at least one hour.

--- QUESTION 0203 ---
How frequently should shower heads, particularly in accommodation areas out of use for extended periods, be cleaned in a 50 ppm chlorine solution?
--- ANSWER ---
3 monthly.

--- QUESTION 0204 ---
What are the maintenance requirements for fresh water hoses every 6 months (or more frequently if required) before emptying and stowing?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be flushed and filled with a 50 ppm chlorine solution and allowed to stand for at least an hour.

--- QUESTION 0205 ---
What three distinct components form the basis of a Fresh Water Safety Plan (FWSP) format?
--- ANSWER ---

System assessment and hazard analysis; 2. Management plan and control measures; 3. Monitoring and corrective action system.

--- QUESTION 0206 ---
What standard color is generally used to clearly mark fresh water hoses?
--- ANSWER ---
Blue.

--- QUESTION 0207 ---
What physical precaution must be taken with fresh water hoses immediately after use and before stowing them in a locker clear of the deck?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be drained and capped at both ends.

--- QUESTION 0208 ---
How frequently must a ship's Master carry out inspections of the accommodation areas, and who must accompany them?
--- ANSWER ---
At least once a week, accompanied by a member of the crew.

--- QUESTION 0209 ---
How frequently must a ship's Master inspect the supplies of food and drinking water on board, and who must accompany them?
--- ANSWER ---
At least once a week, accompanied by a member of the catering staff.

--- QUESTION 0210 ---
Where must the records of weekly accommodation, food, and drinking water inspections be officially entered?
--- ANSWER ---
In the Official Log Book (OLB).

--- QUESTION 0211 ---
According to the list of ten main reasons for food poisoning, hot food should not be stored below what specific temperature?
--- ANSWER ---
63°C.

--- QUESTION 0212 ---
What is the recommended target storage temperature for a ship's dry provision store?
--- ANSWER ---
Around 10°C.

--- QUESTION 0213 ---
What are the correct operational temperature requirements for cold stores and freezers respectively?
--- ANSWER ---
Cold stores must be 5°C or colder, and freezers must be -18°C or colder.

--- QUESTION 0214 ---
In order to reduce potential fire hazards in the ship's laundry, which specific component of the dryers must be kept clean?
--- ANSWER ---
The lint filters.

--- QUESTION 0215 ---
What physical accessibility design requirement must be met for beds in the ship's hospital?
--- ANSWER ---
At least one bed should be accessible from both sides.

--- QUESTION 0216 ---
Who carries the primary regulatory responsibility for compiling, updating, and exhibiting the Muster List in conspicuous shipboard locations?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master.

--- QUESTION 0217 ---
In accordance with MGN 71 and SOLAS III/8, what specific operational capacity must the Muster List define for key individuals who may become disabled?
--- ANSWER ---
It must specify substitutes for key persons.

--- QUESTION 0218 ---
Which flag-state document must be posted in a conspicuous location alongside the Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance (DMLC) certificate during an alleyway inspection?
--- ANSWER ---
A valid Maritime Labour Convention (MLC) certificate.

--- QUESTION 0219 ---
When a seafarer signs on board, what two official documents must the Master provide to them in accordance with shipboard management procedures?
--- ANSWER ---
A copy of their job description (as per the SMS) and a copy of the Onboard Complaints Procedure.

--- QUESTION 0220 ---
Which specific officer on board a seagoing ship is explicitly required to hold a Certificate of Competency and a Food and Hygiene certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
The Chief Cook.

--- QUESTION 0221 ---
What initial form must be completed and dispatched to the Registry of Shipping and Seamen following a death on board?
--- ANSWER ---
Form MSF 4605.

--- QUESTION 0222 ---
If a deceased crew member was part of the Safe Manning Document, what must the vessel request prior to its departure from port?
--- ANSWER ---
A replacement for that crew member.

--- QUESTION 0223 ---
What is the core mission of a Port State Control (PSC) Memorandum of Understanding (MOU)?
--- ANSWER ---
The mission is to eliminate the operation of sub-standard ships through a harmonized system of Port State Control, ensuring they meet international safety, security, and environmental standards, and that crew members have adequate living and working conditions.

--- QUESTION 0224 ---
Which international maritime conventions provide regulations and the legal framework for Port State Control procedures?
--- ANSWER ---
The legal framework is derived from UNCLOS (United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea). Control procedures are provided by conventions including SOLAS (Chapter XI-1), MARPOL, STCW, AFS, MLC (Title 5), LL, COLREG, and BWM.

--- QUESTION 0225 ---
In the United Kingdom, which specific regulations and notice provide for the inspection of foreign seagoing ships and their crews?
--- ANSWER ---
The Merchant Shipping (Port State Control) Regulations 2011 and Merchant Shipping Notice MSN 1832 Amendment 2.

--- QUESTION 0226 ---
What definition describes a "substandard ship" within the context of Port State Control?
--- ANSWER ---
A vessel that, through its physical condition, its operation, or the activities of its crew, fails to meet basic standards of seaworthiness and thereby poses a threat to life and/or the environment.

--- QUESTION 0227 ---
Name at least five regional Port State Control Memorandums of Understanding (MOUs) or authorities mentioned in the text.
--- ANSWER ---
Any five of the following: Paris MOU, Tokyo MOU, Latin America MOU, Black Sea MOU, Indian Ocean MOU, Caribbean MOU, Mediterranean MOU, Abuja MOU, Riyadh MOU, or the USCG (United States Coast Guard).

--- QUESTION 0228 ---
What three operational parameters are determined by a ship's Ship Risk Profile under the Paris MOU New Inspection Regime?
--- ANSWER ---
The Ship Risk Profile determines the ship's priority for inspection, the interval between its inspections, and the scope of the inspection.

--- QUESTION 0229 ---
What are the three risk classifications that a vessel can be assigned under the Ship Risk Profile?
--- ANSWER ---
High Risk, Standard Risk, or Low Risk.

--- QUESTION 0230 ---
What six factors are considered when determining a vessel's Ship Risk Profile?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Type of ship
2. Flag
3. ISM Company Performance
4. Age
5. Historic parameters from the last 36 months (number of inspections and detentions)
6. Classification Society

--- QUESTION 0231 ---
What is the name of the centralized information system and database developed by EMSA to store and update Port State Control inspection data between member states?
--- ANSWER ---
THETIS.

--- QUESTION 0232 ---
What is the inspection interval for a High Risk Ship under the Paris MOU periodic inspection criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Between 5 and 6 months after the last inspection in the Paris MOU region.

--- QUESTION 0233 ---
What is the inspection interval for a Standard Risk Ship under the Paris MOU periodic inspection criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Between 10 and 12 months after the last inspection in the Paris MOU region.

--- QUESTION 0234 ---
What is the inspection interval for a Low Risk Ship under the Paris MOU periodic inspection criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Between 24 and 36 months after the last inspection in the Paris MOU region.

--- QUESTION 0235 ---
What are the three main types of Port State Control inspections?
--- ANSWER ---
Initial Inspection, More Detailed Inspection, and Expanded Inspection.

--- QUESTION 0236 ---
What items are checked during an Initial Inspection by a Port State Control Officer?
--- ANSWER ---
The officer checks certificates and documents, inspects the overall condition and hygiene of the ship, verifies compliance with international rules, and checks if any deficiencies from previous inspections were rectified within the stipulated timeframe.

--- QUESTION 0237 ---
Under what circumstances is a "More Detailed Inspection" conducted?
--- ANSWER ---
It is carried out when there are "clear grounds" for believing that the condition of the ship, its equipment, or its crew does not meet relevant international requirements.

--- QUESTION 0238 ---
What constitutes "clear grounds" for a Port State Control Officer to warrant a More Detailed Inspection?
--- ANSWER ---
Clear grounds exist when an officer finds evidence which, in their professional judgment, warrants a closer look. The absence of valid certificates automatically constitutes clear grounds.

--- QUESTION 0239 ---
Which specific vessel categories are subject to an Expanded Inspection?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Ships with a High Risk Profile.
2. Any Tankers, Bulk Carriers, and Passenger ships over 12 years of age.
3. Ships with overriding or unexpected factors.
4. Any ship inspected for the purpose of lifting a refusal of access notice.

--- QUESTION 0240 ---
How much advance notice must be sent by a ship subject to an Expanded Inspection prior to arrival?
--- ANSWER ---
72 hours notice.

--- QUESTION 0241 ---
According to Paris MOU data from 2011, what were the three distinct reasons that led to a total of 20 ships being banned (refusal of access) from the region?
--- ANSWER ---
Multiple detentions (17 ships), failure to call at an indicated repair yard (2 ships), and jumping detention (1 ship).

--- QUESTION 0242 ---
Name at least eight structural, operational, or safety areas checked during an Expanded Inspection.
--- ANSWER ---
Any eight of the following:

1. Documentation
2. Structural condition
3. Water/Weathertight condition
4. Emergency systems
5. Radio communication
6. Cargo operations
7. Fire safety
8. Alarms
9. Living and working conditions
10. Navigation equipment
11. Life saving appliances
12. Propulsion and auxiliary machinery
13. Dangerous Goods
14. Pollution prevention

--- QUESTION 0243 ---
Define a "Temporary Dispensation" as specified under regulation 4.1.4.
--- ANSWER ---
A temporary substitution of a particular fitting, material, appliance, or apparatus required by regulation where no specific exemption may be made, provided the condition of the ship and equipment continues to "correspond substantially" with statutory certificate requirements.

--- QUESTION 0244 ---
What is a "General Exemption" under regulatory definitions?
--- ANSWER ---
An exemption from one or more regulatory requirements granted in respect of a class or group of ships to which the particular regulation applies, which may include generic conditions or limitations.

--- QUESTION 0245 ---
What is the maximum period of validity permitted for a regulatory exemption?
--- ANSWER ---
The maximum period of validity must not exceed 5 years, and it must match the expiry date of the associated certificate (exemptions cannot be granted indefinitely).

--- QUESTION 0246 ---
Under STCW Regulation I/6, exemptions to serve in which two crew capacities are strictly prohibited?
--- ANSWER ---
Master and Chief Engineer.

--- QUESTION 0247 ---
Why might the practical benefits of an exemption from minimum crew levels be negligible under UK regulations?
--- ANSWER ---
While regulation 18 of the Merchant Shipping (Safe Manning, Hours of Work and Watchkeeping) Regulations 1997 allows safe manning exemptions, no exemptions are permitted by the Merchant Shipping (Hours of Work) Regulations 2002.

--- QUESTION 0248 ---
What local solution can be approved by the STCB if a vessel is short-handed and Port State Control refuses to accept manning below the Minimum Safe Manning Document?
--- ANSWER ---
The temporary employment of a locally available crew member qualified to the necessary STCW standard, whose certificate may not yet be eligible for a Certificate of Equivalent Competency (CEC).

--- QUESTION 0249 ---
What official document is issued by the MCA to acknowledge that a ship may continue operating with temporarily unrectified defects or damaged equipment?
--- ANSWER ---
A Temporary Dispensation Letter (Form MSF 1686).

--- QUESTION 0250 ---
Does possession of a flag State Temporary Dispensation Letter protect a ship from enforcement action by a foreign Port State Control Officer?
--- ANSWER ---
No. The Port State Control Officer is not bound to accept flag State documentation, and permitting the ship to operate remains a matter for the judgment and discretion of the individual PSCO.

--- QUESTION 0251 ---
Under what circumstances will a Temporary Dispensation Letter normally NOT be required for an equipment defect?
--- ANSWER ---
When a defect is noted while at sea and it is intended to be rectified prior to departure from the next port, or when the vessel is in a port where no repair facilities or spares are available.

--- QUESTION 0252 ---
According to Regulation 6, where must a ship subject to an expanded inspection remain until the inspection is fully completed?
--- ANSWER ---
The ship must remain in the port or anchorage.

--- QUESTION 0253 ---
What core criterion triggers the immediate detention of a vessel or the stoppage of an operation during an inspection?
--- ANSWER ---
When an inspection reveals deficiencies which, in the professional judgment of the inspector, are clearly hazardous to safety, health, or the environment.

--- QUESTION 0254 ---
How long must a copy of the Port State Control inspection report be retained on board a ship?
--- ANSWER ---
For two years.

--- QUESTION 0255 ---
Whom must the MCA immediately inform in writing when a ship is detained?
--- ANSWER ---
The flag State administration and the Recognized Organizations (classification societies) responsible for the issue of the ship's classification or statutory certificates.

--- QUESTION 0256 ---
If a detainable deficiency cannot be rectified in the port of inspection, what action may the MCA take?
--- ANSWER ---
The MCA may allow the ship to proceed to the nearest appropriate repair yard, subject to conditions ensuring safety and environment protection. Structural deficiencies may require thickness measurements before sailing.

--- QUESTION 0257 ---
What options are available to inspectors if a ship is detained solely because it lacks a functioning voyage data recorder (VDR)?
--- ANSWER ---
The ship may be allowed to proceed to the nearest appropriate port where the VDR can be readily rectified, or the shipowner may be required to rectify the defect within 30 days.

--- QUESTION 0258 ---
What steps are taken by the MCA to protect crew members when a complaint regarding ship safety is investigated?
--- ANSWER ---
The confidentiality of crew members during interviews is strictly ensured, and the identity of the complainant is never revealed to the shipmaster or shipowner.

--- QUESTION 0259 ---
What are the statutory reporting obligations of a UK pilot who learns of apparent anomalies while engaged on a vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
The pilot must immediately inform the authorizing port authority or the MCA if they observe anomalies that may prejudice safe navigation or pose a threat of harm to the marine environment.

--- QUESTION 0260 ---
What standard information should be transferred electronically in a pilot's anomaly report?
--- ANSWER ---
Ship name, IMO number, call sign, flag, last port of call, port of destination, and a description of the apparent anomalies found on board.

--- QUESTION 0261 ---
What is the difference between Priority I (Overriding factors) and Priority II (Unexpected factors) regarding additional inspections?
--- ANSWER ---
Priority I overriding factors are sufficiently serious to automatically trigger an additional inspection. Priority II unexpected factors indicate a serious threat, but the decision to undertake an additional inspection is left to the professional judgment of the Authority.

--- QUESTION 0262 ---
Name two overriding factors that automatically trigger a Priority I additional inspection.
--- ANSWER ---

1. Ships involved in a collision, grounding, or stranding on their way to port.
2. Ships accused of an alleged violation of provisions on the discharge of harmful substances or effluents.
3. Ships suspended or withdrawn from their Class for safety reasons since the last PSC inspection.

--- QUESTION 0263 ---
What is a Concentrated Inspection Campaign (CIC), and when does it take place?
--- ANSWER ---
A CIC is a campaign focusing on specific areas with a higher risk of non-compliance or new convention requirements. It takes place yearly over a 3-month period from September 1st to November 30th and is combined with regular inspections.

--- QUESTION 0264 ---
Match the specific focus areas of the Paris/Tokyo MOU Concentrated Inspection Campaigns (CICs) for the years 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025, and 2026.
--- ANSWER ---

* 2021: Stability in General
* 2022: STCW (Standards of Training, Certification and Watchkeeping)
* 2023: Fire Safety
* 2024: MLC (Crew Wages and Seafarer Employment Agreement)
* 2025: Ballast Water Management
* 2026: Cargo Securing

--- QUESTION 0265 ---
During the 2025 CIC on Ballast Water Management, what specific records, certificates, and operational elements are verified?
--- ANSWER ---
A valid International Ballast Water Management Certificate (IBWMC), an approved and up-to-date Ballast Water Management Plan (BWMP), crew familiarization with duties, Ballast Water Management System (BWMS) operational status and approval, Ballast Water Record Book (BWRB) logs, and sediment management.

--- QUESTION 0266 ---
Under the 2024 CIC on Crew Wages and SEAs, what is the significance of a "No" answer to questions on the checklist marked with an asterisk?
--- ANSWER ---
A "No" answer on an asterisked question constitutes ground for considering the detention of the ship.

--- QUESTION 0267 ---
Under the 2023 CIC on Fire Safety, what action must a Port State Control Officer take if any of the questions 1 to 10 are answered with a "No"?
--- ANSWER ---
A "No" answer must be accompanied by a relevant deficiency noted on the official Report of Inspection.

--- QUESTION 0268 ---
What are four of the specific technical line items checked during the 2023 Fire Safety Concentrated Inspection Campaign?
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following:

1. Maintenance of emergency escape routes.
2. Operation of fire doors.
3. Periodic testing of fixed fire detection and alarm systems.
4. Functionality of ventilation closing appliances.
5. Operability of machinery space power ventilation controls from two grouped positions.
6. Capability of fire pumps to deliver at least two required jets of water.
7. Operation of remote controls outside machinery spaces for stopping ventilation and oil transfer equipment.
8. Single-purpose use of the fixed gas fire extinguishing medium room.
9. Operability of fire main line valves.
10. Satisfactory execution of a witnessed fire drill.

--- QUESTION 0269 ---
If a Port State Control inspector in Rotterdam asks to see your Ship Security Plan, including items identified as confidential, what action should you take according to the ISPS Code?
--- ANSWER ---
You should provide the inspector with information showing certificate validity, current and past security levels, and special measures taken, but you must not disclose the details of the confidential ship security plan.

--- QUESTION 0270 ---
According to ISPS Code Regulation 9, what specific information regarding past ports must be available to show a duly authorized officer?
--- ANSWER ---
The security levels at previous ports, specifically the Declaration of Security for the last 10 ports.

--- QUESTION 0271 ---
What are the first four steps a company must take to obtain an International Ship Security Certificate (ISSC)?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Arrange for a Ship Security Assessment (SSA) internally or via a Recognized Security Organization (RSO).
2. Create the Ship Security Plan (SSP) based on the SSA findings.
3. Submit the SSP to the MCA for approval and send it onboard for implementation.
4. Install the Ship Security Alert System (SSAS) onboard and send a functional test report to the MCA.

--- QUESTION 0272 ---
Within what timeframe must a Company Security Officer ensure a vessel can pass an initial ISPS audit after establishing arrangements for drills and internal audits during the ISSC process?
--- ANSWER ---
Within the next 6 months.

--- QUESTION 0273 ---
What is the maximum period of validity for an Interim International Ship Security Certificate (ISSC) issued by the MCA?
--- ANSWER ---
Maximum of 6 months, with no extension permitted.

--- QUESTION 0274 ---
What is the typical period of validity for a full International Ship Security Certificate (ISSC) following an initial audit?
--- ANSWER ---
Valid for a maximum of 5 years.

--- QUESTION 0275 ---
When must an Intermediate ISPS verification/audit be conducted relative to the certificate's expiry date?
--- ANSWER ---
Between two and three years before the certificate expiry date.

--- QUESTION 0276 ---
Under what circumstances would an "Additional" ISPS verification or audit be triggered?
--- ANSWER ---
When a significant non-compliance with the ISPS Code is suspected or to close out an existing non-conformity.

--- QUESTION 0277 ---
What are the six procedural areas checked and verified during an MCA ISPS audit?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Access to the ship by ship's personnel, passengers, visitors, etc.
2. Restricted areas on the ship.
3. Handling of cargo.
4. Delivery of ship's stores.
5. Handling unaccompanied baggage.
6. Monitoring the security of the ship.

--- QUESTION 0278 ---
Name at least five documents or records that should be prepared prior to an ISPS audit to prove compliance.
--- ANSWER ---
Any five of the following:

1. Previous audit/verification reports showing closed-out non-conformities.
2. Results of internal security audits and reviews.
3. Evidence of control measures protecting the SSP from unauthorized access.
4. Records of security breaches.
5. Evidence of responding to changes in security levels.
6. Security details for the last 10 ports.
7. Maintenance, testing, and calibration records for security equipment (including SSAS and LRIT).
8. Evidence of training drills and exercises.

--- QUESTION 0279 ---
What is the minimum frequency required for conducting shipboard security drills, and what is the exception regarding crew changes?
--- ANSWER ---
At least once every three months. However, if more than 25 percent of the crew changes at one time, a drill must be conducted within one week of the change.

--- QUESTION 0280 ---
What is the required frequency for a full Security Exercise, and what are its maximum intervals?
--- ANSWER ---
At least once each calendar year, with no more than 18 months between the exercises.

--- QUESTION 0281 ---
What core elements should a comprehensive maritime Security Exercise test?
--- ANSWER ---
Communications, coordination, resource availability, and response between the various stakeholders.

--- QUESTION 0282 ---
List at least five specific duties assigned to the Ship Security Officer (SSO).
--- ANSWER ---
Any five of the following:

1. Undertake regular security inspections of the ship.
2. Maintain and supervise the implementation of the SSP.
3. Coordinate security aspects of cargo handling and ship's stores.
4. Propose modifications to the SSP.
5. Report deficiencies/non-conformities to the CSO and implement corrective actions.
6. Enhance security awareness and vigilance on board.
7. Ensure adequate training is provided to personnel.
8. Report all security incidents.
9. Coordinate implementation of the SSP with the CSO and PFSO.
10. Ensure security equipment is operated, tested, calibrated, and maintained.

--- QUESTION 0283 ---
How do the elements of Cyber Security integrate into the standard ISPS framework structure?
--- ANSWER ---
The Ship Security Assessment (SSA) incorporates a Cyber Security Assessment (CSA), which then informs the Cyber Security Policies, Processes, and Procedures integrated within the overall Ship Security Plan (SSP) or an independent Cyber Security Plan (CSP).

--- QUESTION 0284 ---
Define ISPS Security Levels 1, 2, and 3.
--- ANSWER ---

* **Security Level 1 (Normal):** The level at which ships and port facilities normally operate.
* **Security Level 2 (Heightened):** The level applying for as long as there is a heightened risk of a security incident.
* **Security Level 3 (Exceptional):** The level applying for the period of time when there is a probable or imminent risk of a security incident.

--- QUESTION 0285 ---
Which authority is responsible for setting the security levels for UK-flagged vessels?
--- ANSWER ---
The Department for Transport.

--- QUESTION 0286 ---
What are five scenarios under which a ship should consider executing a Declaration of Security (DoS) with a port facility?
--- ANSWER ---

1. The ship is operating at a higher security level than the port facility or another ship it is interfacing with.
2. There is a specific agreement between Contracting Governments covering certain international voyages or specific ships.
3. There has been a security threat or security incident involving the ship or port facility.
4. The ship is at a port not required to have and implement an approved port facility security plan.
5. The ship is conducting ship-to-ship activities with another ship not required to have an approved plan.

--- QUESTION 0287 ---
What is the baseline minimum retention period for a completed Declaration of Security (DoS) onboard a vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum of three years, provided it covers the vessel's last ten port calls. It must be retained longer if those last ten calls extend beyond the three-year limit.

--- QUESTION 0288 ---
What planning and reporting steps should be taken when preparing to transit a high-risk piracy area?
--- ANSWER ---
Carry out a formal risk assessment, ensure the crew is trained and vigilant (increasing lookouts), assess vessel speed at critical points, test communication paths, register with the European Naval Force, and report to the UK Maritime Trade Organisation (UKMTO) Dubai.

--- QUESTION 0289 ---
Under what specific circumstances is the Master authorized to cease broadcasting the Long Range Identification and Tracking (LRIT) signal?
--- ANSWER ---
When the ship is undergoing repairs, modifications, or conversions in a dry-dock, or if the vessel is officially laid up for a long period.

--- QUESTION 0290 ---
If the LRIT system is deactivated on authority of the Master, what documentation must be completed?
--- ANSWER ---
An official entry must be made in the Official Log Book (OLB) detailing the specific dates and times the equipment was switched off.

--- QUESTION 0291 ---
How frequently does the LRIT system automatically transmit identification data to an LRIT Data Centre?
--- ANSWER ---
At 6-hour intervals, operating automatically without human intervention.

--- QUESTION 0292 ---
What operational steps should be taken during a port call to prevent stowaways from entering the vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
Secure all access points, ensure the gangway is continuously attended and monitored (including using CCTV), obtain a certified list of stevedores from the port agent prior to arrival, verify all expected visitors with the Master, conduct regular rounds checking mooring lines and small boats, and perform a thorough stowaway search before departure.

--- QUESTION 0293 ---
If a stowaway is discovered onboard after departing a port, what are the initial actions the Master must take?
--- ANSWER ---
