Contact the Designated Person Ashore (DPA) and the P&I Club immediately, gather as much identification information as possible (identity, nationality, citizenship, port of embarkation), and arrange to disembark the individual at the first port of call.

--- QUESTION 0294 ---
What is the primary operational purpose of the Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR) and where must it be kept?
--- ANSWER ---
The CSR provides an onboard history of the vessel. It must be kept continuously onboard the ship and remain available for inspection at all times.

--- QUESTION 0295 ---
Name at least four key details recorded within a vessel's Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR).
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: Flag State, Ship's Identification Number (IMO Number), Name & Port of Registry, Registered Owners, Name of Company, or Name of Classification Society.

--- QUESTION 0296 ---
What are the three mandatory forms associated with managing a Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR)?
--- ANSWER ---

* **Form 1:** Continuous Synopsis Record
* **Form 2:** Amendments to Continuous Synopsis Record
* **Form 3:** Index of Amendments to the Continuous Synopsis Record

--- QUESTION 0297 ---
If a page of the Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR) is discovered to be missing, damaged, or lost, what administrative step must be taken?
--- ANSWER ---
Contact the Registry of Shipping and Seamen (RSS), who will provide signed and stamped duplicates.

--- QUESTION 0298 ---
If a handover reveals that the Ship Security Alert System (SSAS) has not been tested for 18 months, what immediate corrective measures must be initiated?
--- ANSWER ---
Log it as a non-conformity, inform the Designated Person Ashore (DPA) and Flag State administration, agree on a formal corrective action plan, test the SSAS at the earliest opportunity, and review onboard procedures to prevent recurrence.

--- QUESTION 0299 ---
When the Ship Security Alert System (SSAS) is activated, how does it respond regarding alarms onboard and signaling ashore?
--- ANSWER ---
It transmits a secure ship-to-shore alert containing the ship's identity and location directly to a designated competent authority/Company. It does *not* send the alert to other ships, does *not* raise any audible or visual alarm onboard, and continues transmitting until manually deactivated or reset.

--- QUESTION 0300 ---
From what mandatory locations onboard must the Ship Security Alert System (SSAS) be capable of activation?
--- ANSWER ---
From the navigation bridge and from at least one other separate location onboard.

--- QUESTION 0301 ---
According to MSF 5623 A, what type of document is a Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR) and how long does it remain with a ship?
--- ANSWER ---
The CSR is a form of log book that stays with the ship for its whole life.

--- QUESTION 0302 ---
Name three specific organizational or operational changes regarding a ship that a Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR) is designed to log.
--- ANSWER ---
Any three of the following: changes of owner, flag, name, Class, or ISM.

--- QUESTION 0303 ---
Under what circumstances must a copy of the whole CSR history be kept onboard the ship?
--- ANSWER ---
The ship must keep its copy of the whole CSR onboard even when it transfers to another flag state, is sold to another owner, is taken over by another bareboat charterer, or another company assumes operational responsibility.

--- QUESTION 0304 ---
For which three specific categories of vessels must a Continuous Synopsis Record (CSR) be maintained?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Passenger ships on international voyages
2. Cargo ships of 500 gt and above on international voyages
3. Class A passenger ships operating domestic services within UK waters

--- QUESTION 0305 ---
Who carries the ultimate responsibility for the proper upkeep of the CSR onboard UK ships?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master of the ship.

--- QUESTION 0306 ---
Why is it absolutely critical that the details within the onboard CSR are always accurate and up to date?
--- ANSWER ---
Because Port State Control will check the CSR as a matter of routine during inspections.

--- QUESTION 0307 ---
What actions are strictly prohibited regarding original CSR documents to ensure their physical integrity?
--- ANSWER ---
Original CSR documents must not be modified, deleted, erased, or defaced.

--- QUESTION 0308 ---
What is the specific purpose of Form 1 within the Continuous Synopsis Record framework?
--- ANSWER ---
It is the latest up-to-date record of the ship's details, and it is used to apply for the ship's first CSR document.

--- QUESTION 0309 ---
Which CSR form serves as the official application to amend the details contained within Form 1?
--- ANSWER ---
Form 2 (Amendments to the Continuous Synopsis Record).

--- QUESTION 0310 ---
What is the purpose of Form 3, and to what does it specifically apply?
--- ANSWER ---
Form 3 is the Index of Amendments, which records applications made to amend the latest version of Form 1. It applies only to the current Form 1, not to the entire CSR history.

--- QUESTION 0311 ---
Which specific entity issues Continuous Synopsis Records for UK registered ships, and in what language are they issued?
--- ANSWER ---
The Registry of Shipping and Seamen (RSS), in the English language.

--- QUESTION 0312 ---
Where is the Registry of Shipping and Seamen (RSS) physically located?
--- ANSWER ---
Anchor Court, Keen Road, Cardiff, CF24 5JW.

--- QUESTION 0313 ---
What form should a new build ship use when registering with the UK Ship Register to acquire its very first CSR?
--- ANSWER ---
Form 1 (Application for Continuous Synopsis Record).

--- QUESTION 0314 ---
When a ship is flagging into the UK Ship Register, which boxes must be completed on Form 2 to send to its previous Flag Administration, and why?
--- ANSWER ---
Boxes 1, 15, and 16 must be completed so that the previous Flag Administration can issue their final closure CSR document.

--- QUESTION 0315 ---
What documentation does RSS request from a ship flagging into the UK to mitigate common delays in receiving official records from the losing Flag Administration?
--- ANSWER ---
A full copy of the ship's CSR file (while the originals are kept onboard).

--- QUESTION 0316 ---
What sequence of actions must a ship's crew take on board if data entries in the current Form 1 require amendment?
--- ANSWER ---
They must fill out the changes on Form 2 and Form 3, attach the original forms to the latest Form 1 on the ship, and send a copy of Form 2 to the Registry of Shipping and Seamen (RSS).

--- QUESTION 0317 ---
How does RSS identify the new Form 1 document it issues after processing a valid amendment?
--- ANSWER ---
It issues the new Form 1 with the next consecutive document number.

--- QUESTION 0318 ---
Why might RSS intentionally leave a gap in the consecutive numbering sequence when issuing a new Form 1?
--- ANSWER ---
To allow the losing Flag Administration's closure CSR to be inserted into the sequence.

--- QUESTION 0319 ---
What process must be followed if you find that required amendments were not previously notified to RSS after receiving a new CSR?
--- ANSWER ---
You must begin the process again by completing a brand new Form 2 and Form 3.

--- QUESTION 0320 ---
What is the risk of having lost or damaged CSR documents onboard, and what immediate action should be taken?
--- ANSWER ---
It can cause potential delays to the ship's voyage. You should contact RSS as quickly as possible so they can provide signed and stamped duplicates (for a fee).

--- QUESTION 0321 ---
Which two forms must be completed and submitted to RSS when a vessel is officially leaving the UK Ship Register?
--- ANSWER ---
Form 2 (with boxes 1, 15, and 16 completed) and the Application to Remove a British Ship from the Register (MSF 4744).

--- QUESTION 0322 ---
What two actions will RSS perform as soon as possible after a ship's transfer date out of the UK flag is completed?
--- ANSWER ---
RSS will send the ship the closing UK CSR and forward a copy of the ship's complete CSR file to the new Flag Administration.

--- QUESTION 0323 ---
Under MARPOL Annex I, what is the maximum allowed oil content of the effluent for a ship of 400 gross tonnage and above discharging machinery space bilges outside a special area?
--- ANSWER ---
15 parts per million (ppm).

--- QUESTION 0324 ---
What are the five mandatory operational conditions that a ship of 400 gross tonnage and above must meet under MARPOL Annex I to discharge oily mixtures from machinery spaces outside special areas?
--- ANSWER ---

1. The ship is proceeding en route.
2. The oily mixture is processed through oil filtering equipment meeting Annex I Regulation 14 requirements.
3. The oil content of the effluent without dilution does not exceed 15 ppm.
4. The oily mixture does not originate from cargo pump-room bilges on oil tankers.
5. The oily mixture is not mixed with oil cargo residues (in the case of oil tankers).

--- QUESTION 0325 ---
What additional equipment capabilities are mandatory for oil filtering equipment when a ship of 400 gross tonnage and above is discharging machinery space bilges within a special area?
--- ANSWER ---
The oil filtering equipment must be provided with an alarm for when the effluent exceeds 15 ppm and an automatic stopping arrangement for the discharge.

--- QUESTION 0326 ---
What is the discharge requirement regarding oil or oily mixtures for any ship in the Antarctic area under MARPOL Annex I?
--- ANSWER ---
Any discharge into the sea of oil or oily mixtures is entirely prohibited.

--- QUESTION 0327 ---
Under MARPOL Annex I, what is the minimum distance from the nearest land required for an oil tanker to discharge oil or oily mixtures from its cargo area outside a special area?
--- ANSWER ---
More than 50 nautical miles.

--- QUESTION 0328 ---
What is the maximum instantaneous rate of discharge of oil content allowed from the cargo area of an oil tanker operating outside a special area?
--- ANSWER ---
30 liters per nautical mile.

--- QUESTION 0329 ---
For an oil tanker delivered after 31 December 1979, the total quantity of oil discharged into the sea from the cargo area must not exceed what fraction of the particular cargo of which the residue formed a part?
--- ANSWER ---
1/30,000 of the total quantity.

--- QUESTION 0330 ---
What automated system must be operational on an oil tanker to perform discharges from the cargo area outside a special area under MARPOL Annex I?
--- ANSWER ---
An Oil Discharge Monitoring and Control System (ODME).

--- QUESTION 0331 ---
What is the discharge criteria for oil or oily mixtures from the cargo area of an oil tanker when operating within a designated special area?
--- ANSWER ---
No discharge is permitted.

--- QUESTION 0332 ---
According to MARPOL Annex I, which parallel constitutes the boundary between the Mediterranean Sea and the Black Sea?
--- ANSWER ---
The 41° N parallel.

--- QUESTION 0333 ---
Which meridian bounds the Mediterranean Sea area to the west at the Straits of Gibraltar?
--- ANSWER ---
The meridian of 005°36' W.

--- QUESTION 0334 ---
The Baltic Sea area is bounded to the west by the parallel of the Skaw in the Skagerrak at what specific latitude?
--- ANSWER ---
57°44.8' N.

--- QUESTION 0335 ---
The Antarctic area special area under MARPOL is defined as the sea area south of what latitude?
--- ANSWER ---
Latitude 60° S.

--- QUESTION 0336 ---
If a tanker's Oil Discharge Monitoring and Control System (ODME) suffers a catastrophic failure en route, what is the immediate required logbook action?
--- ANSWER ---
The failure must be noted immediately in the Oil Record Book Part II.

--- QUESTION 0337 ---
Subject to allowance by the port State authority, how many ballast voyages may a tanker with a defective ODME undertake before proceeding to a repair port?
--- ANSWER ---
One ballast voyage.

--- QUESTION 0338 ---
What are the four categories assigned to Noxious Liquid Substances (NLS) carried in bulk under MARPOL Annex II based on their environmental hazard?
--- ANSWER ---
Category X (Major hazard), Category Y (Hazard), Category Z (Minor hazard), and Other Substances (considered to present no harm).

--- QUESTION 0339 ---
Under MARPOL Annex II, what specialized operational manual must every ship carrying Noxious Liquid Substances in bulk have on board?
--- ANSWER ---
A Procedures and Arrangements Manual.

--- QUESTION 0340 ---
What specific operational speed must a self-propelled ship maintain while discharging Noxious Liquid Substances (NLS) residues into the sea under MARPOL Annex II?
--- ANSWER ---
A speed of at least 7 knots.

--- QUESTION 0341 ---
What is the minimum speed requirement for a non-self-propelled ship during the discharge of Noxious Liquid Substances at sea?
--- ANSWER ---
At least 4 knots.

--- QUESTION 0342 ---
Where must the physical discharge of Noxious Liquid Substances residues take place relative to the ship's hull structure?
--- ANSWER ---
Below the waterline through the underwater discharge outlet(s).

--- QUESTION 0343 ---
What are the minimum distance from land and minimum water depth requirements for discharging Noxious Liquid Substances residues under MARPOL Annex II?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 12 nautical miles from the nearest land and in a depth of water not less than 25 meters.

--- QUESTION 0344 ---
Which document is the designated stowage plan for dangerous goods cargo carried in packaged form, and where must its primary copy be kept on board?
--- ANSWER ---
The Dangerous Goods Manifest, which must be kept on the vessel's bridge.

--- QUESTION 0345 ---
What certificate must the loading company provide to the carrier when shipping dangerous goods by sea in containers to confirm compliance with the IMDG Code?
--- ANSWER ---
A Container Packing Certificate.

--- QUESTION 0346 ---
Which certificate must a ship present to prove its sewage system meets the requirements of MARPOL Annex IV?
--- ANSWER ---
An International Sewage Pollution Prevention Certificate.

--- QUESTION 0347 ---
What is the correct date format mandated for entries made in the Oil Record Book Part I?
--- ANSWER ---
dd-MMM-yyyy (e.g., 14-APR-2024).

--- QUESTION 0348 ---
How frequently must the quantities of oil residues (sludge) retained on board be recorded in the Oil Record Book Part I?
--- ANSWER ---
Weekly (once a week, even if the voyage lasts more than one week).

--- QUESTION 0349 ---
What three options are compliant methods for disposing of used cooking oil under MARPOL Annex V?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Store in marked containers and land ashore.
2. Incinerate (if not mixed with other oils and not in waste/sludge tanks).

--- QUESTION 0350 ---
What is the minimum distance from the nearest land required to discharge comminuted or ground food waste outside special areas under MARPOL Annex V?
--- ANSWER ---
More than 3 nautical miles (while en route and as far as practicable).

--- QUESTION 0351 ---
Under MARPOL Annex V, what is the discharge rule for all "other garbage" including plastics, synthetic ropes, incinerator ashes, and packing materials?
--- ANSWER ---
Discharge is strictly prohibited everywhere (outside special areas, within special areas, and on offshore platforms).

--- QUESTION 0352 ---
Name six of the designated special areas established under MARPOL Annex V for garbage control.
--- ANSWER ---
Any six of the following: The Mediterranean Sea, the Baltic Sea Area, the Black Sea area, the Red Sea Area, the Gulfs area, the North Sea, the Wider Caribbean Region, or the Antarctic Area.

--- QUESTION 0353 ---
What two primary items/certificates comprise the legal evidence that a ship's structure, equipment, and systems comply with MARPOL Annex II regulations for NLS?
--- ANSWER ---
The Noxious Liquid Substances Certificate (or Certificate of Fitness for gas and chemical carriers) and the Procedures and Arrangements Manual.

--- QUESTION 0354 ---
What are the standard forms used for the "Record of Construction and Equipment" that supplement the International Oil Pollution Prevention (IOPP) Certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
Form A (for ships other than oil tankers) and Form B (for oil tankers).

--- QUESTION 0355 ---
What certification and documentation are required specifically for a vessel engaging in Ship-to-Ship (STS) bunkering operations?
--- ANSWER ---
International Oil Pollution Prevention Certificate (IOPPC), Ship Bunker Convention Certification, Civil liabilities insurance, Ship-to-ship safety checklist, and International Ship and Port Facility Security (ISPS) / Document of Security (DOS).

--- QUESTION 0356 ---
According to MARPOL Annex V, what is the minimum gross tonnage threshold for a ship to carry a Garbage Management Plan and a Garbage Record Book?
--- ANSWER ---
100 gross tonnage (GT).

--- QUESTION 0357 ---
What is the minimum certification requirement or number of carried persons that mandates a ship to maintain a Garbage Management Plan and a Garbage Record Book?
--- ANSWER ---
Any ship certified to carry 15 or more persons.

--- QUESTION 0358 ---
Under MARPOL Annex V, what are the three specific written procedures that a Garbage Management Plan must provide for the crew to follow?
--- ANSWER ---
Procedures for minimizing, collecting, storing, processing, and disposing of garbage.

--- QUESTION 0359 ---
For how long must a Garbage Record Book be preserved on board after the date of the last entry?
--- ANSWER ---
For a period of at least two years.

--- QUESTION 0360 ---
What is the minimum length overall for a ship to be required to display placards notifying the crew and passengers of garbage discharge requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
12 meters or more in length overall.

--- QUESTION 0361 ---
What key details must be recorded in the Ballast Water Record Book after completing a ballast water exchange?
--- ANSWER ---
The date, time, and location of loading, treatment, and discharge, as well as the method used.

--- QUESTION 0362 ---
Who must sign the entries in the Ballast Water Record Book?
--- ANSWER ---
The officer in charge of the operation must sign the individual entries, and the Master must sign each completed page.

--- QUESTION 0363 ---
What are the primary operational parameters and documentation items included in SEEMP Part II for IMO DCS reporting?
--- ANSWER ---
The methodology for data collection, fuel consumption logs, distance run, hours underway, and the verified annual report leading to a Statement of Compliance.

--- QUESTION 0364 ---
What are the maximum allowable oil content discharge limits for machinery space bilges in the Mediterranean Sea special area?
--- ANSWER ---
The discharge must meet less than 15 ppm using an Oily Water Separator equipped with an automatic stop device.

--- QUESTION 0365 ---
What are the rules regarding the discharge of cargo tank washings within a MARPOL special area like the Mediterranean Sea?
--- ANSWER ---
No discharge of cargo tank washings is allowed within a special area.

--- QUESTION 0366 ---
What sewage discharge restrictions apply to passenger ships operating within the Baltic Sea special area?
--- ANSWER ---
Discharge is prohibited unless using an approved, operating sewage treatment plant that meets established effluent quality standards.

--- QUESTION 0367 ---
How does EEDI differ from EEOI in operational terms?
--- ANSWER ---
EEDI (Energy Efficiency Design Index) is a fixed design index for new ships determined once built, whereas EEOI (Energy Efficiency Operational Indicator) is an operational index calculated from actual fuel consumption, cargo carried, and distance traveled over time.

--- QUESTION 0368 ---
What specific records and documentation must be maintained on board to show compliance with SEEMP Part III (CII) requirements during an inspection?
--- ANSWER ---
The SEEMP Part III document, CII calculation records, the annual operational carbon intensity rating, evidence of continuous monitoring, and a corrective action plan if the ship falls below the required grade.

--- QUESTION 0369 ---
To satisfy both EU MRV and UK MRV reporting regimes, what key structural documentation and monitoring compliance steps must be implemented?
--- ANSWER ---
Separate monitoring plans must be maintained, an accredited verifier must be assigned for each jurisdiction, annual emissions reports must be submitted, and valid Documents of Compliance (DOC) valid for 18 months must be held for both systems.

--- QUESTION 0370 ---
What key items, documents, and procedures must be included in a ship's SOPEP to satisfy MARPOL emergency preparedness requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
Contact lists, procedures for reporting/spill response, an oil spill equipment inventory, operational action checklists, ship area diagrams, and coastal authorities' reporting forms.

--- QUESTION 0371 ---
What is the regulatory threshold in gross tonnage for ships required to submit annual reports under the IMO Data Collection System (DCS)?
--- ANSWER ---
Ships of 5000 Gross Tonnage (GT) and above.

--- QUESTION 0372 ---
Under the IMO Data Collection System (DCS), what three metrics must ships report annually to their Administration?
--- ANSWER ---
Fuel consumption, distance traveled, and hours underway.

--- QUESTION 0373 ---
What is the validity period of the Statement of Compliance issued after successful verification of the IMO DCS data?
--- ANSWER ---
It is valid for the calendar year in which it has been issued and for the first 5 months of the subsequent calendar year.

--- QUESTION 0374 ---
How is grey water regulated under the international MARPOL conventions?
--- ANSWER ---
MARPOL does not regulate grey water; requirements must be checked via Flag State, Port State, Particular Sensitive Sea Area (PSSA) local laws, and company environmental policies.

--- QUESTION 0375 ---
What is the geographical and size scope of the EU MRV regulations regarding commercial shipping emissions?
--- ANSWER ---
They apply to all commercial ships of more than 5000 GT, irrespective of Flag, undertaking voyages into, out of, or between EU ports.

--- QUESTION 0376 ---
What is the validity period of a Document of Compliance (DOC) issued under EU MRV regulations?
--- ANSWER ---
It is valid for a period of 18 months after the end of the reporting period.

--- QUESTION 0377 ---
What is the general global limit for Sulphur content in fuel oil used or carried for use on board ships outside of Emission Control Areas (ECAs)?
--- ANSWER ---
Not exceeding 0.50% m/m.

--- QUESTION 0378 ---
What is the maximum allowable Sulphur content in fuel oil when a vessel is operating within an Emission Control Area (ECA)?
--- ANSWER ---
Not exceeding 0.10% m/m.

--- QUESTION 0379 ---
For how long must the Bunker Delivery Note (BDN) be retained on board a ship?
--- ANSWER ---
For a period of three years after the fuel oil has been delivered on board.

--- QUESTION 0380 ---
What is the minimum retention period for the representative fuel bunker samples collected during delivery?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be retained under the ship's control until the fuel is substantially consumed, but in any case for not less than 12 months from the time of delivery.

--- QUESTION 0381 ---
What information must be logged in the Fuel Changeover Record Book or logbook when switching to low-Sulphur fuel for ECA operations?
--- ANSWER ---
The volume of low-Sulphur fuel oils in each tank, alongside the precise date, time, and position of the ship when the operation occurred.

--- QUESTION 0382 ---
What is the gross tonnage threshold above which all ships must keep a ship-specific Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan (SEEMP) on board?
--- ANSWER ---
All ships above 400 GT.

--- QUESTION 0383 ---
List four operational or technical options outlined in SEEMP Part I to optimize a ship's energy performance over time.
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: Improved voyage planning, weather routing, just-in-time arrival, speed optimization, trim and ballast optimization, hull maintenance, or waste heat recovery.

--- QUESTION 0384 ---
What is the standard validity period of an International Air Pollution Prevention Certificate (IAPPC), and what specific items does it cover?
--- ANSWER ---
It is valid for 5 years and covers compliance regarding NOx, SOx, VOCs, Ozone-depleting substances, and shipboard incineration.

--- QUESTION 0385 ---
What is the regulatory purpose of the Engine International Air Pollution Prevention Certificate (EIAPPC)?
--- ANSWER ---
It details components and settings of an engine that influence nitrogen oxide (NOx) emissions, and is required for each engine over 130 kW.

--- QUESTION 0386 ---
What are the immediate steps a vessel must take if an exhaust gas scrubber breaks down while operating inside an Emission Control Area (ECA)?
--- ANSWER ---
Switch to compliant fuel oil, record the event in the logbook, enter the non-compliance in the log, notify the relevant maritime authorities if required, and arrange for repairs.

--- QUESTION 0387 ---
How does the freeboard of a vessel directly relate to its reserve buoyancy?
--- ANSWER ---
The freeboard of a vessel determines its reserve buoyancy. A large freeboard provides a large reserve buoyancy, allowing the vessel to withstand a significant amount of compartment flooding and still remain afloat. Conversely, a small freeboard provides a small reserve buoyancy, making the vessel vulnerable to sinking after a comparatively small amount of flooding.

--- QUESTION 0388 ---
How does the height of a vessel's freeboard affect the angle at which its deck edge becomes immersed when heeled?
--- ANSWER ---
A vessel with a large freeboard will experience deck edge immersion at a relatively large angle of heel, whereas a vessel with a small freeboard will experience deck edge immersion at a relatively small angle of heel.

--- QUESTION 0389 ---
What are the negative operational consequences for a vessel with a small freeboard operating in heavy weather?
--- ANSWER ---
A vessel with a small freeboard will ship more seas on deck in heavy weather. This endangers onboard personnel, deck cargo, and deck fittings such as hatchways.

--- QUESTION 0390 ---
What are the four core structural and safety requirements that must be met as part of the Conditions of Assignment before a freeboard is assigned and a load line certificate is issued?
--- ANSWER ---
The four requirements are:

1. Enough structural strength should be possessed.
2. Enough reserve buoyancy should be possessed.
3. Safety and protection of the crew must be provided.
4. Entry of water through the hull must be prevented.

--- QUESTION 0391 ---
What is considered the main underlying principle of the assignment of a load line?
--- ANSWER ---
The main principle is to maintain the appropriate ratio between a vessel's reserve buoyancy and its underwater volume.

--- QUESTION 0392 ---
By ensuring that the appropriate seasonal load line mark is not submerged at sea in salt water, what specific relative density (RD) value is being assumed?
--- ANSWER ---
An RD of 1.025.

--- QUESTION 0393 ---
According to the text, what specific physical features and dimensional parameters affect a vessel's actual reserve buoyancy?
--- ANSWER ---
The factors include:

* Actual freeboard for the given condition
* Superstructures (above the freeboard deck)
* Sheer profile
* Length for a given freeboard
* Depth for a given freeboard

--- QUESTION 0394 ---
In the process of assigning freeboard, what are the two main ship type classifications used to determine the initial tabular freeboard?
--- ANSWER ---
Type A and Type B.

--- QUESTION 0395 ---
List the design factors and corrections applied to the basic freeboard calculation to arrive at the final assigned freeboard.
--- ANSWER ---
The factors and corrections include:

* Block Coefficient
* Depth Correction
* Superstructure
* Sheer
* Bow Height

--- QUESTION 0396 ---
What are the standard application criteria that differentiate a UK Load Line Certificate from an International Load Line Certificate?
--- ANSWER ---

* **UK Load Line Certificates:** Issued to ships of less than 24 meters in length or less than 150 Gross Tonnage (GT).
* **International Load Line Certificates:** Issued to new ships of not less than 24 meters in length or existing ships of not less than 150 gross tons, regardless of whether or not they trade internationally.

--- QUESTION 0397 ---
Under what circumstances is a Load Line Exemption Certificate utilized, and which specific entity has the sole authority to issue it?
--- ANSWER ---
It is used for ships that do not trade internationally or to which the standard convention does not apply. It can only be issued by the MCA (Maritime and Coastguard Agency).

--- QUESTION 0398 ---
What is the primary regional scope and regulatory objective of the Stockholm Agreement?
--- ANSWER ---
The Stockholm Agreement is a regional agreement concerning the safety of Ro-Ro passenger ships operating on regular scheduled voyages between, to, or from ports in Northwest Europe and the Baltic Sea. Its objective is to enhance damage survivability by implementing higher stability standards than those required by the SOLAS 90 convention.

--- QUESTION 0399 ---
What specific technical hazard does the Stockholm Agreement address regarding Ro-Ro ferries, and what is its key vehicle deck requirement?
--- ANSWER ---
It addresses the safety effects of water accumulation on the vehicle deck after damage. It requires ferries to be able to withstand a certain amount of water (500 mm) on their watertight vehicle deck, even if the residual freeboard drops below a certain level.

--- QUESTION 0400 ---
What is the maximum standard period of validity for an International Load Line Certificate, and what is required annually to maintain it?
--- ANSWER ---
The certificate is valid for a maximum of 5 years and is subject to mandatory Annual Surveys each year, with a Renewal Survey taking place in the 5th year.

--- QUESTION 0401 ---
According to the provisions printed on the reverse side of a load line certificate, under what specific logistical circumstances can a certificate's validity be extended?
--- ANSWER ---
An extension may be granted if the certificate expires when the ship is not in a port where a survey can take place. The extension allows the ship to proceed directly to a designated port of survey.

--- QUESTION 0402 ---
What is the legal onboard display requirement for a valid load line certificate and its associated documentation?
--- ANSWER ---
The valid load line certificate must be displayed behind glass in a frame fixed to a bulkhead in the wheelhouse, and the notification of draughts must be posted in a position reasonably protected from the weather.

--- QUESTION 0403 ---
What are the 5 distinct types of load lines that can legally be assigned to a ship?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Type A
2. Type B
3. Type B with reduced freeboard
4. Type B with increased freeboard
5. Type B with Timber freeboard

--- QUESTION 0404 ---
What are the four sequential operational phases outlined for preparing and executing a Loadline Survey?
--- ANSWER ---
Plan, Prepare, Conduct, and Review.

--- QUESTION 0405 ---
During the planning phase of a Loadline Survey, what specific berth conditions must the master identify?
--- ANSWER ---
The master must identify a suitable berth where no cargo operations, bunkering, or storing are planned.

--- QUESTION 0406 ---
During survey preparations, what distinct responsibilities are allocated to the Master versus the Chief Officer (C/O)?
--- ANSWER ---

* **Master:** Must ensure that the original conditions of assignment and all other relevant statutory certificates are organized and readily available.
* **Chief Officer:** Must prepare stability condition printouts, compile loadicator test conditions, update the Planned Maintenance System (PMS) to address outstanding items, and ensure that draft marks are clearly painted.

--- QUESTION 0407 ---
What specific physical checks should the Bosun perform when preparing the deck for a load line inspection?
--- ANSWER ---
The Bosun should check all hatch and door rubber seals, portholes, and side scuttles, and ensure the active operation of all ventilators and their respective closing arrangements.

--- QUESTION 0408 ---
What administrative option does a Master have during a closing meeting if a surveyor notes deficiencies or negative findings?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master is given the opportunity to downgrade any findings by preparing a formal Corrective Action Plan and presenting it directly to the surveyor.

--- QUESTION 0409 ---
List at least six specific physical areas or fittings on deck that are subject to inspection during the conduct of a Loadline Survey.
--- ANSWER ---
Any six of the following:

* Hatchways
* Machinery space openings
* Ventilators
* Superstructure deck openings
* Cargo ports
* Air pipes
* Scuppers
* Side scuttles
* Inlets and discharges
* Guard rails and freeing ports
* Load Line marks

--- QUESTION 0410 ---
What are the standard methods used to test the water-tight integrity of cargo hold hatch covers prior to or during a survey?
--- ANSWER ---

1. **Chalk Test:** Applying chalk to the seals to see if a continuous impression is made when the hold is secured.
2. **Hose Test:** Applying a fire hose along the seams and around the cover sides while an inspector looks for leaks from inside the hold.
3. **Ultra-sound Test:** Performing an ultrasonic leak detection test.

--- QUESTION 0411 ---
According to SOLAS Chapter II-1, Regulation 13-1, what is the core policy regarding making structural openings or penetrations in watertight subdivisions?
--- ANSWER ---
The number of openings in watertight subdivisions must be kept to an absolute minimum compatible with the design and proper operational working of the ship. Where penetrations are necessary (for piping, ventilation, electrical cables, etc.), arrangements must be made to maintain the complete watertight integrity of the structure.

--- QUESTION 0412 ---
If a physical modification like a bulkhead penetration is proposed, what three administrative steps must a ship's management team take before commencing work?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Identify the specific type of bulkhead using the General Arrangement (GA) plan.
2. Prepare a detailed scope of work.
3. Contact the Technical Superintendent and Flag State administration prior to commencing any physical work.

--- QUESTION 0413 ---
If the actual displacement derived from observed drafts differs significantly from the calculated displacement (the sum of lightweight, cargo, and known deadweight items), which value must the master accept as paramount?
--- ANSWER ---
The master must accept the displacement determined from the observed draughts.

--- QUESTION 0414 ---
When actual displacement is greater than calculated displacement, how must this discrepancy be handled under regulatory guidance (e.g., MSIS 03)?
--- ANSWER ---
The discrepancy must be treated as an additional increment of cargo weight. This weight must be assumed to be acting at the vertical centre of gravity (VCG) position for the total cargo weight being carried, or the ship as a whole, whichever position is greater.

--- QUESTION 0415 ---
How is a "significant difference" between calculated and actual displacement mathematically defined for a vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
A significant difference is defined as a discrepancy in displacement that is equal to or greater than twice the vessel's tonnes per centimetre (TPC) immersion value.

--- QUESTION 0416 ---
Under the current IMO Intact Stability Code 2008, when must an existing passenger ship be re-inclined to determine its lightship parameters?
--- ANSWER ---
The ship must be re-inclined whenever a deviation from the approved stability information is found or anticipated that exceeds 2% of the lightship displacement, or when a deviation of the longitudinal centre of gravity (LCG) exceeds 1% of the ship's length ($L$).

--- QUESTION 0417 ---
What are the precise standard line thicknesses and circle dimensions specified for an official statutory Load Line Mark?
--- ANSWER ---
All lines used within the mark have a uniform thickness of 35 mm. The load line circle has an outer diameter of 450 mm, with a horizontal line passing through its center that measures 450 mm in length.

--- QUESTION 0418 ---
What notation system is used on a Passenger Safety Certificate to record and distinguish multiple approved subdivision load lines?
--- ANSWER ---
The subdivision load lines are distinguished by the notation **P1** for the principal passenger service configuration, and **P2**, **P3**, etc., for any approved alternative service configurations.

--- QUESTION 0419 ---
Under what operational scenario will a passenger vessel be assigned a "C" mark (such as C1, C2, C3) load line?
--- ANSWER ---
A passenger vessel is assigned "C" marks if it has flexible, alternative spaces designed to accommodate either cargo or passengers, which alters the required subdivision draught for each distinct condition of service.

--- QUESTION 0420 ---
What critical procedural checks must a Master perform regarding certificates and markings when a vessel utilizes multiple assigned load lines?
--- ANSWER ---
1. Verify that only one load line certificate is in active use at any given time, while all other certificates are kept in a sealed envelope under the safe custody of the master.
2. Ensure that only the single set of marks associated with the certificate in use is physically painted in and visible; all alternative marks must be painted out to match the background color.
3. Physically measure the new summer freeboard on each side, recording the entry in the Multiple Load Line Assignment Booklet with the Master's endorsement, making an official entry in the ship's logbook, and informing the company office.

--- QUESTION 0421 ---
What is the geographical definition of the North Atlantic Winter Seasonal Zone as outlined in MSN 1752?
--- ANSWER ---
The zone lies within the meridian of longitude 50°W from the coast of Greenland to latitude 45°N, thence the parallel of latitude 45°N to longitude 15°W, thence the meridian of longitude 15°W to latitude 60°N, thence the parallel of latitude 60°N to the Greenwich Meridian, and thence this meridian northwards.

--- QUESTION 0422 ---
What are the exact calendar periods defined for the Winter and Summer seasons within the North Atlantic Winter Seasonal Zone?
--- ANSWER ---
* **WINTER:** 16th October to 15th April
* **SUMMER:** 16th April to 15th October

--- QUESTION 0423 ---
Define the precise technical distinction between "Weathertight" and "Watertight" as stated in SOLAS Chapter II-1.
--- ANSWER ---
* **Weathertight:** Means that in any sea conditions, water will not penetrate into the ship.
* **Watertight:** Means having scantlings and arrangements capable of preventing the passage of water in any direction under the head of water likely to occur in both intact and damaged conditions (at equilibrium, including intermediate stages of flooding).

--- QUESTION 0424 ---
Define the terms "Freeboard" and "Statutory Freeboard" as they relate to a vessel's design layout.
--- ANSWER ---
* **Freeboard:** The physical distance measured vertically from the waterline to the top of the freeboard deck.
* **Statutory Freeboard:** The specific distance measured vertically from the upper edge of the Summer Load Line mark to the upper edge of the deck line.

--- QUESTION 0425 ---
Which rules of the Collision Regulations are covered in the presentation context regarding Open Water Single Vessel Situations?
--- ANSWER ---
Rules 13, 14, 15, 16, 17, and 18.

--- QUESTION 0426 ---
What types of conditions or scenarios are explicitly specified for the single vessel situations?
--- ANSWER ---
Open Water Situations during both Day and Night.

--- QUESTION 0427 ---
According to MARPOL Annex 1 Regulation 27, what is the minimum required area under the righting lever curve (GZ Curve) up to a 30-degree angle of heel for oil tankers?
--- ANSWER ---
0.055 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0428 ---
According to MARPOL Annex 1 Regulation 27, what is the minimum required area under the righting lever curve between 30 degrees and 40 degrees angle of heel for oil tankers?
--- ANSWER ---
0.03 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0429 ---
What is the minimum required initial metacentric height (GM) in port for an oil tanker under MARPOL Annex 1 Regulation 27?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.15m

--- QUESTION 0430 ---
Under MARPOL Annex 1 Regulation 27 for oil tankers, at what minimum angle of heel must the maximum righting lever (GZ) occur?
--- ANSWER ---
An angle of heel not less than 25 degrees

--- QUESTION 0431 ---
According to the International Grain Code, the angle of heel due to the shift of grain shall not be greater than 12 degrees or which other angle, whichever is the lesser?
--- ANSWER ---
The angle at which the deck edge is immersed

--- QUESTION 0432 ---
For cargo ships carrying grain, the net or residual area between the heeling arm curve and the righting arm curve up to an angle of heel of 40 degrees or the angle of flooding (whichever is less) must not be less than what value?
--- ANSWER ---
0.075 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0433 ---
What is the minimum required initial metacentric height (GM) for cargo ships carrying grain after correction for free surface effects?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.30m

--- QUESTION 0434 ---
For cargo ships carrying timber deck cargoes, what is the minimum required area under the righting lever curve up to a 40-degree angle of heel (or lesser angle at which water could enter the hull)?
--- ANSWER ---
0.08 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0435 ---
What is the minimum metacentric height (GM) required at all times for cargo ships carrying timber deck cargoes under the alternative stability criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.10m

--- QUESTION 0436 ---
For containerships greater than 100m, what is the formula given to determine the total area under the righting lever curve (GZ curve) up to the angle of flooding?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.029 / C metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0437 ---
In the stability parameters for containerships greater than 100m, what does the variable 'D' represent in the provided formulas?
--- ANSWER ---
Molded draft of the ship in metres

--- QUESTION 0438 ---
For offshore supply vessels, if the maximum GZ occurs at a 15-degree heel, what is the required area under the righting lever curve up to 30 degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
0.07 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0439 ---
What is the required initial metacentric height (GM) for offshore supply vessels under the statical stability criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.15m

--- QUESTION 0440 ---
What is the primary effect of a beam wind and rolling on a vessel's stability?
--- ANSWER ---
It reduces the dynamic stability of the vessel as a result of the wind heeling lever.

--- QUESTION 0441 ---
According to the MCA's suggested method for container ships under severe wind and rolling, what is the relationship between the gust wind moment and the steady wind moment?
--- ANSWER ---
Gust wind moment = 1.5 x steady wind moment

--- QUESTION 0442 ---
What mathematical method is suggested to calculate the areas S1 and S2 on a container ship's severe wind and rolling stability curve?
--- ANSWER ---
Simpson's Rules

--- QUESTION 0443 ---
What are the two mandatory criteria chapters listed under Part A of the Intact Stability Code 2008?
--- ANSWER ---
Chapter 1 (General) and Chapter 2 (General criteria)

--- QUESTION 0444 ---
According to SOLAS Regulation 5-1, what curves or tables must be supplied to the master to provide rapid and simple guidance regarding intact and damage stability?
--- ANSWER ---
Curves or tables of minimum operational metacentric height (GM) or maximum allowable vertical centre of gravity (KG)

--- QUESTION 0445 ---
Define the term 'KG' as used in ship stability.
--- ANSWER ---
The height of the vessel's centre of gravity above the keel, representing the point through which gravitational force acts vertically downwards.

--- QUESTION 0446 ---
What formula is used to determine the Righting Lever (GZ) using Cross Curves of Stability (KN) and KG?
--- ANSWER ---
GZ = KN - (KG x Sine θ)

--- QUESTION 0447 ---
What is represented by the 'BM' or the Centre of Buoyancy?
--- ANSWER ---
The point at which the total forces of buoyancy act vertically upward, located at the geometrical centre of the underwater volume.

--- QUESTION 0448 ---
List at least five typical items found in the table of contents of a standard ship stability book.
--- ANSWER ---
Any five of the following: 1. General description of ship, 2. Instructions for use, 3. General arrangement plans, 4. Hydrostatic curves/tables, 5. Capacity plans/tables, 6. Tank sounding tables, 7. Loading restrictions, 8. Standard operating conditions, 9. Stability calculations, 10. Precautions against flooding, 11. Cross-flooding arrangements, 12. Guidance for safe operation.

--- QUESTION 0449 ---
What is the purpose of the cross curves (KN curves) provided in a ship's stability booklet?
--- ANSWER ---
To enable Statical Stability Curves (GZ curves) to be drawn for the ship in any sailing condition and displacement.

--- QUESTION 0450 ---
What type of stability software (Stability Computer) can calculate intact stability and check damage stability based on a limit curve?
--- ANSWER ---
Type 2

--- QUESTION 0451 ---
What type of stability software calculates damage stability associated with an actual loading condition and actual flooding cases using direct application of user or sensor defined damage to enable a safe return to port (SRtP)?
--- ANSWER ---
Type 4

--- QUESTION 0452 ---
When a vessel heels over unexpectedly due to an angle of loll, what is the immediate recommended action regarding the ship's heading?
--- ANSWER ---
Alter course to put the ship's head into the predominant waves to prevent it from rolling across to the opposite side.

--- QUESTION 0453 ---
Why is it dangerous to allow a lolled vessel to roll across to the opposite side?
--- ANSWER ---
The momentum involved in the action may result in a capsizing moment.

--- QUESTION 1454 ---
What are the recommended actions to lower the centre of gravity (reduce KG) when correcting an angle of loll?
--- ANSWER ---
Pumping out ballast from high-sided tanks or ballasting double-bottom tanks.

--- QUESTION 0455 ---
What physical condition causes an angle of loll to occur?
--- ANSWER ---
It occurs when the vertical centre of gravity moves above the metacentre (negative initial GM), causing an unstable upright condition.

--- QUESTION 0456 ---
Why are ships carrying timber deck cargo particularly susceptible to an angle of loll?
--- ANSWER ---
They may have a small initial GM, and if the cargo absorbs water during the voyage, the center of gravity (G) will rise.

--- QUESTION 0457 ---
What is the value of the righting lever (GZ) when a ship is at its angle of loll?
--- ANSWER ---
Zero

--- QUESTION 0458 ---
What is the defining difference between static stability and dynamic stability?
--- ANSWER ---
Statical stability is the ability of the ship to return upright when heeled (represented by the GZ lever), while dynamic stability is the total work required to heel the ship to a certain angle (represented by the area under the GZ curve).

--- QUESTION 0459 ---
Under the special criteria for passenger ships, what is the maximum permissible angle of heel due to passengers crowding to one side?
--- ANSWER ---
10 degrees

--- QUESTION 0460 ---
What is the assumed minimum weight for each passenger when performing passenger ship stability calculations?
--- ANSWER ---
75 kg

--- QUESTION 0461 ---
Under passenger ship criteria, what is the maximum permissible angle of heel when the vessel is turning?
--- ANSWER ---
10 degrees

--- QUESTION 0462 ---
Where is the center of gravity assumed to be for standing passengers in passenger stability criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
1.0 metre above the deck level

--- QUESTION 0463 ---
In damage stability, what safety index is used as a measure of a ship's safety in a damaged condition under the probabilistic method?
--- ANSWER ---
The attained subdivision index A.

--- QUESTION 0464 ---
To demonstrate compliance with damage stability rules under the probabilistic method, what is the required relationship between the attained subdivision index A and the required subdivision index R?
--- ANSWER ---
The attained subdivision index A must be greater than or equal to the required subdivision index R (A >= R).

--- QUESTION 0465 ---
According to general damage stability assumptions for side damage, what is the standard longitudinal extent of the damage?
--- ANSWER ---
1/3 L^(2/3) or 14.5 meters, whichever is less.

--- QUESTION 0466 ---
What is the standard vertical extent of assumed side damage in deterministic stability regulations?
--- ANSWER ---
From the baseline upwards without limit.

--- QUESTION 0467 ---
Under the IBC Code, what is the maximum standard angle of heel permitted in any stage of flooding due to unsymmetrical flooding, provided no deck immersion occurs?
--- ANSWER ---
30 degrees (the limit is 25 degrees, but it may be increased to 30 degrees if no deck immersion occurs).

--- QUESTION 0468 ---
According to the IBC Code and IGC Code, what is the minimum required range of positive stability beyond the position of equilibrium for the final righting-lever curve?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum range of 20 degrees.

--- QUESTION 0469 ---
Under the IBC Code, what is the minimum required maximum residual righting lever (GZ) value within its 20-degree range of remaining stability?
--- ANSWER ---
At least 0.1 meter.

--- QUESTION 0470 ---
Under Regulation 27 of the Load Line Convention, what is the maximum standard angle of heel permitted due to unsymmetrical flooding?
--- ANSWER ---
15 degrees (though an angle of up to 17 degrees may be accepted if no part of the deck is immersed).

--- QUESTION 0471 ---
What happens to the freeboard assignment for a Type A ship compared to a Type B ship?
--- ANSWER ---
The freeboard increases for a Type A ship and reduces for a Type B ship.

--- QUESTION 0472 ---
What is the minimum positive metacentric height (GM) required by the MCA for a Ship Type A after flooding?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 50 millimeters (0.05 meters).

--- QUESTION 0473 ---
According to MARPOL Annex 1, what is the damage location applicability for an oil tanker exceeding 225 meters in length?
--- ANSWER ---
Anywhere along the ship's length.

--- QUESTION 0474 ---
According to the Merchant Shipping Regulations 1997 No. 1509, where must the damage control plan and booklets be permanently exhibited or readily available?
--- ANSWER ---
On the navigating bridge.

--- QUESTION 0475 ---
What term describes the ingress of water to compartments that were originally assumed to be intact during a damage stability assessment?
--- ANSWER ---
Progressive flooding.

--- QUESTION 0476 ---
What is defined as the angle of heel at which the lower edge of openings in the hull, superstructures, or deckhouses that cannot be closed weathertight become immersed?
--- ANSWER ---
The down-flooding angle.

--- QUESTION 0477 ---
How is the Attained Subdivision Index A fundamentally calculated for a specific zone or occurrence?
--- ANSWER ---
By multiplying the probability of the occurrence (flooding of a zone) by the probability of surviving that occurrence.

--- QUESTION 0478 ---
Under the general criteria for dynamic stability, what must be the minimum area under the righting lever curve (GZ curve) up to a 30-degree angle of heel?
--- ANSWER ---
0.055 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0479 ---
Under the general criteria for dynamic stability, what must be the minimum area under the righting lever curve (GZ curve) up to a 40-degree angle of heel (or the lesser angle at which water could enter the hull)?
--- ANSWER ---
0.09 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0480 ---
According to the general criteria, what is the minimum required area under the GZ curve between the 30-degree and 40-degree angles of heel?
--- ANSWER ---
0.03 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0481 ---
According to the general criteria, the righting lever GZ must be at least how many metres at an angle of heel equal to or greater than 30 degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
0.2m

--- QUESTION 0482 ---
Under general stability criteria, the maximum righting lever GZ should ideally occur at an angle of heel not less than how many degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
25 degrees

--- QUESTION 0483 ---
What is the minimum required initial metacentric height (GM) under the general criteria for statical stability?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.15m

--- QUESTION 0484 ---
Under the severe wind and rolling criterion, the angle of heel under the action of a steady wind shall not exceed 16 degrees or what percentage of the angle of deck edge immersion, whichever is less?
--- ANSWER ---
80%

--- QUESTION 0485 ---
In the severe wind and rolling criterion, the area 'b' (representing the gust wind heeling effect) must be equal to or greater than which area?
--- ANSWER ---
Area 'a'

--- QUESTION 0486 ---
For passenger ships, what is the maximum allowable angle of heel on account of passengers crowding to one side?
--- ANSWER ---
10 degrees

--- QUESTION 0487 ---
When assessing passenger ship stability, what is the minimum assumed weight for each passenger?
--- ANSWER ---
75kg

--- QUESTION 0488 ---
For a passenger ship, the assumed center of gravity for passengers standing upright is located at what height above the deck level?
--- ANSWER ---
1m

--- QUESTION 0489 ---
For a passenger ship, the assumed center of gravity for seated passengers is located at what height above the seat?
--- ANSWER ---
3m

--- QUESTION 0490 ---
What is the maximum allowable angle of heel for passenger ships when turning?
--- ANSWER ---
10 degrees

--- QUESTION 0491 ---
Which regulation under MARPOL Annex 1 governs the stability criteria for oil tankers?
--- ANSWER ---
Regulation 27

--- QUESTION 0492 ---
What is the minimum initial metacentric height (GM) required for oil tankers while in port?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.15m

--- QUESTION 0493 ---
According to the International Grain Code, the angle of heel due to the shift of grain must not be greater than 12 degrees or the angle at which the deck edge is immersed (whichever is lesser) for ships constructed on or after which date?
--- ANSWER ---
1st January 1994

--- QUESTION 0494 ---
Under the International Grain Code, the net or residual area between the heeling arm curve and the righting arm curve up to an angle of heel of 40 degrees (or the angle of flooding, whichever is less) must not be less than how many metre-radians?
--- ANSWER ---
0.075 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0495 ---
What is the minimum required initial metacentric height (GM) for cargo ships carrying grain after correction for free surface effects?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.30m

--- QUESTION 0496 ---
Under the alternative stability criteria for cargo ships carrying timber deck cargoes, what is the minimum required area under the righting lever curve up to a 40-degree angle of heel?
--- ANSWER ---
0.08 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0497 ---
For cargo ships carrying timber deck cargoes, the maximum righting lever should occur at an angle of heel not less than how many degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
25 degrees

--- QUESTION 0498 ---
What is the minimum required metacentric height (GM) at all times for cargo ships carrying timber deck cargoes under the alternative criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.10m

--- QUESTION 0499 ---
For containerships greater than 100m, the dynamic stability up to a 30-degree angle of heel must be at least $0.009 / C$ metre-radians. What does the value 'C' depend on?
--- ANSWER ---
The value of 'C' is calculated using a formula based on ship dimensions such as LBP, mean draft, moulded depth, moulded breadth measured at different depths, KG(F), block coefficient, water-plane area coefficient, and hatch coaming dimensions.

--- QUESTION 0500 ---
Under the severe wind and rolling criterion, the gust wind moment is calculated as how many times the steady wind moment?
--- ANSWER ---
1.5 times

--- QUESTION 0501 ---
In the severe wind and rolling criterion, what specific value is given for the steady wind heeling pressure?
--- ANSWER ---
48.5 kg/m²

--- QUESTION 0502 ---
According to the severe wind and rolling criterion, how many degrees is the ship assumed to roll into the steady wind as a result of wave action?
--- ANSWER ---
15 degrees

--- QUESTION 0503 ---
Under the severe wind and rolling criterion, the angle of dynamic heel ($\theta_3$) must not exceed the angle at which what event takes place?
--- ANSWER ---
Progressive down-flooding

--- QUESTION 0504 ---
What mathematical rule is suggested by the MCA to calculate the area S1 when evaluating compliance with the severe wind and rolling criterion?
--- ANSWER ---
Simpson's Rules

--- QUESTION 0505 ---
If an offshore supply vessel's characteristics mean it cannot comply with standard criteria, what is the alternative minimum required area under the GZ curve up to a 15-degree angle of heel?
--- ANSWER ---
0.07 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0506 ---
For offshore supply vessels utilizing the alternative criteria, what is the minimum required area under the GZ curve up to a 30-degree angle of heel?
--- ANSWER ---
0.055 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0507 ---
For offshore supply vessels under alternative criteria, what is the minimum required area under the GZ curve between 30 degrees and 40 degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
0.003 metre-radians

--- QUESTION 0508 ---
For offshore supply vessels under alternative criteria, the righting lever GZ must be at least 0.2m at an angle equal to or greater than how many degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
30 degrees

--- QUESTION 0509 ---
For offshore supply vessels under alternative criteria, the maximum righting lever must occur at an angle of heel not less than how many degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
15 degrees

--- QUESTION 0510 ---
What is the minimum required initial metacentric height (GM) for offshore supply vessels under the alternative statical stability criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 0.15m

--- QUESTION 0511 ---
According to MARPOL Annex I Regulation 28, all oil tankers must be fitted with what type of system capable of verifying compliance with intact and damage stability requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
A stability instrument approved by the Administration.

--- QUESTION 0512 ---
By what final date were all oil tankers constructed before January 1, 2016, required to comply with the mandatory installation of a stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
January 1, 2021 (or at the first scheduled renewal survey on or after January 1, 2016, but no later than January 1, 2021).

--- QUESTION 0513 ---
What official document must the Administration issue for a stability instrument for the purposes of control under the regulations?
--- ANSWER ---
A document of approval.

--- QUESTION 0514 ---
In addition to oil tankers, what code makes the carriage of a approved stability instrument mandatory for all ships subject to it?
--- ANSWER ---
The IBC Code (International Bulk Chemical Code).

--- QUESTION 0515 ---
What are the three acceptable types of calculations performed by stability software depending upon a vessel's stability requirements?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Software calculating intact stability only.
2. Software calculating intact stability and checking damage stability on the basis of a limit curve or previously approved loading conditions.
3. Software calculating intact stability and damage stability by direct application of pre-programmed damage cases.

--- QUESTION 0516 ---
For which type of vessel is software that calculates intact stability and damage stability by direct application of pre-programmed damage cases specifically mentioned as applicable?
--- ANSWER ---
Some tankers.

--- QUESTION 0517 ---
Under what condition can an Administration accept direct calculation results that differ from the required minimum GM or maximum VCG stated in the approved stability booklet?
--- ANSWER ---
Under the condition that all relevant stability requirements will be complied with by the results of the direct calculations.

--- QUESTION 0518 ---
What defines a passive data entry system for a stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
It requires manual data entry.

--- QUESTION 0519 ---
What defines an active data entry system for a stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
It partly replaces manual data entry with sensors that read and enter the contents of tanks, etc.

--- QUESTION 0520 ---
Is an integrated system that controls or initiates actions based on sensor-supplied inputs covered within the scope of the stability instrument code?
--- ANSWER ---
No, except for the part that calculates the stability.

--- QUESTION 0521 ---
Name at least four loading parameters that a stability instrument must present for a given loading condition to assist the master.
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following:

1. Detailed deadweight data items (including center of gravity and free surfaces).
2. Trim and list.
3. Draught at the draught marks and perpendiculars.
4. Summary of loading condition displacement (including VCG, LCG, TCG, VCB, LCB, TCB, LCF, GM, and GML).
5. Table showing the righting lever versus heeling angle.
6. Down-flooding angle and corresponding down-flooding opening.
7. Compliance with stability criteria.

--- QUESTION 0522 ---
How must the stability instrument notify the user on screen and in a hard copy printout if any operational or stability limitations are not complied with?
--- ANSWER ---
A clear warning should be given.

--- QUESTION 0523 ---
In what manner must data be presented on screen and in a hard copy printout by a stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
In a clear, unambiguous manner.

--- QUESTION 0524 ---
What specific chronological information must be part of the screen display and hard copy printout of a saved calculation?
--- ANSWER ---
The date and time of the saved calculation.

--- QUESTION 0525 ---
What specific software identifier must each hard copy printout contain to verify the calculation program?
--- ANSWER ---
The identification of the calculation program, including the version number.

--- QUESTION 0526 ---
What rule applies to the units of measurement used within a loading calculation on a stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
Units of measurement should be clearly identified and used consistently.

--- QUESTION 0527 ---
Under what condition may a deviation from acceptable stability instrument tolerances be accepted by an Administration?
--- ANSWER ---
Only if the Administration considers that there is a satisfactory explanation for the difference and that there will be no adverse effect on the safety of the ship.

--- QUESTION 0528 ---
How should the accuracy of a stability instrument's results be determined?
--- ANSWER ---
By comparison with an independent program or the approved stability booklet using identical input data.

--- QUESTION 0529 ---
What is the acceptable tolerance for the printout of input data in programs that use only pre-programmed data from the approved stability booklet?
--- ANSWER ---
Zero tolerance.

--- QUESTION 0530 ---
For software using pre-programmed stability booklet data, what minor output data differences are considered acceptable?
--- ANSWER ---
Small differences associated with calculation rounding, abridged input data, or varying methods for handling trims and free surface moments (subject to review by the Administration).

--- QUESTION 0531 ---
Against what references should tolerances be established for programs using hull form models as their basis for stability calculations?
--- ANSWER ---
Against either data from the approved stability booklet or data obtained using the Administration's approval model.

--- QUESTION 0532 ---
Whose responsibility is it to ensure the correct working of the stability instrument after final or updated software has been installed?
--- ANSWER ---
The ship's master.

--- QUESTION 0533 ---
In whose presence must the initial or updated software installation test calculations be carried out?
--- ANSWER ---
In the presence of an Administration's surveyor.

--- QUESTION 0534 ---
Why are actual loading condition results not suitable for checking the correct working of a newly installed or updated stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
Because testing must be performed against approved test conditions to ensure specific, verifiable mathematical accuracy.

--- QUESTION 0535 ---
Where are the standard test conditions normally stored within a stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
They are permanently stored in the stability instrument.

--- QUESTION 0536 ---
During software installation testing, by how much must you modify the deadweight items to sufficiently change the draught or displacement to check logical trends?
--- ANSWER ---
By at least 10%.

--- QUESTION 0537 ---
What is the final step when testing a modified load condition during an installation test sequence?
--- ANSWER ---
Restore the initial test condition and verify that the relevant input and output data match the approved test condition exactly.

--- QUESTION 0538 ---
How often is the ship's master required to periodically check the accuracy of the stability instrument using at least one approved test condition?
--- ANSWER ---
At each annual survey.

--- QUESTION 0539 ---
What action must be taken if an Administration's representative is not present during an annual check of the stability instrument?
--- ANSWER ---
A copy of the test condition results obtained by the check must be retained on board as documentation for later verification.

--- QUESTION 0540 ---
When must the checking of all approved test loading conditions be performed directly in the presence of the Administration's representative?
--- ANSWER ---
At each renewal survey.

```python
text = """
--- QUESTION 0541 ---
What is the primary purpose of the Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
The purpose of the Inclining Experiment is to determine the lightship parameters of a vessel, specifically to determine the KG (vertical center of gravity) of the ship in the light displacement condition.

--- QUESTION 0542 ---
How is a list induced during an Inclining Experiment to determine the KG of a ship?
--- ANSWER ---
A list is achieved by shifting a known weight athwartships, which causes the ship's center of gravity (G) to move off the center-line to G1.

--- QUESTION 0543 ---
What is the formula used to calculate the shift in the center of gravity (GG1) during the Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
[IMAGE: page-3.jpg]
GG1 = (w * d) / W, where 'w' is the shifted weight, 'd' is the distance the weight is moved, and 'W' is the displacement of the vessel.

--- QUESTION 0544 ---
List the ship plans and data that must be available to the person in charge of the Inclining Experiment.
--- ANSWER ---
1. Lines plan
2. Hydrostatic curves or hydrostatic data
3. General arrangement plan of decks, holds, inner bottoms, etc.
4. Capacity plan showing capacities and vertical and longitudinal centers of gravity of cargo spaces, tanks, etc.
5. Tank sounding tables
6. Draught mark locations
7. Docking drawing with keel profile and draught mark corrections (if available)

--- QUESTION 0545 ---
When must an Inclining Experiment be performed on a cargo ship?
--- ANSWER ---
It must be performed on completion of build to determine its stability data (unless a test has been conducted on a sister ship) and when alterations are made to the ship that will affect its stability.

--- QUESTION 0546 ---
According to the Intact Stability Code section 8, how often should passenger ships undergo a lightweight survey to verify changes in lightship displacement and longitudinal center of gravity?
--- ANSWER ---
At intervals not exceeding five years.

--- QUESTION 0547 ---
How can free surface effects be minimized in tanks prior to an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
By emptying tanks completely or limiting the number of slack tanks (such as fresh water reserve, fuel/diesel oil, lube oil, sanitary tanks, and potable water tanks).

--- QUESTION 0548 ---
What precautions should be taken regarding pressed-up tanks before conducting an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be completely full with no voids, and personnel must be aware of entrapped air in the tank.

--- QUESTION 0549 ---
What are the requirements for mooring arrangements during an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
Consider the depth of water, wind, and current. The ship should be moored in a quiet area away from the wash of passing vessels, and it must be free to list to allow readings to be taken.

--- QUESTION 0550 ---
What certification and marking requirements apply to the test weights used in an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
Weights must be marked with an identification number and their weight, and weight certification should be presented.

--- QUESTION 0551 ---
What is the minimum recommended length/deflection requirement for the pendulum used in an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
The pendulum should be long enough to give a measured deflection to each side of the upright of at least 15 cm.

--- QUESTION 0552 ---
What are the primary considerations during the initial walk-through and survey step of conducting an Inclining Test?
--- ANSWER ---
1. Consider weather conditions
2. Ensure suspended items are secured on the vessel
3. Check all plans and equipment to be used

--- QUESTION 0553 ---
Why are freeboard and draught readings taken during the Inclining Test, and what water property must be determined at that time?
--- ANSWER ---
They are taken to determine the displacement of the ship at the time of the inclining test. The specific gravity of the water must also be determined.

--- QUESTION 0554 ---
What checks and actions must be performed immediately before inclining the vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
1. Check that the vessel is floating freely
2. Check the pendulum and record its length
3. Mark the initial position of the weights on the deck
4. Check communication
5. Check that all personnel are in place

--- QUESTION 0555 ---
Describe how a plumb line is utilized to measure deflection when conducting the Inclining Experiment.
--- ANSWER ---
[IMAGE: page-13.jpg]
A plumb line is suspended from a point 'O'. When the ship is upright, it hangs down and crosses a batten at point 'X'. When the ship is listed, the plumb line deflects to point 'Y' along the batten to measure the deflection.
"""
print("Total questions:", len(text.strip().split("--- QUESTION ")))



```

```text
Total questions: 16


```

```python
output = """--- QUESTION 0541 ---
What is the primary purpose of the Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
The purpose of the Inclining Experiment is to determine the lightship parameters of a vessel, specifically to determine the KG of the ship in the light displacement condition.

--- QUESTION 0542 ---
How is a list induced during an Inclining Experiment to determine the vertical center of gravity of a ship?
--- ANSWER ---
A list is achieved by shifting a weight athwartships, causing the center of gravity to move off the center-line to G1.

--- QUESTION 0543 ---
What formula calculates the shift in the center of gravity during the Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
[IMAGE: page-3.jpg]

GG1 = (w * d) / W, where w is the shifted weight, d is the distance the weight is moved, and W is the displacement of the vessel.

--- QUESTION 0544 ---
What ship plans and data must be available to the person in charge of the Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
The required plans and data include the lines plan, hydrostatic curves or data, general arrangement plan of decks/holds/inner bottoms, capacity plan showing capacities and vertical/longitudinal centers of gravity, tank sounding tables, draught mark locations, and docking drawing with keel profile and draught mark corrections if available.

--- QUESTION 0545 ---
When must an Inclining Experiment be performed on cargo ships?
--- ANSWER ---
On completion of build to determine its stability data, and whenever alterations are made to the ship which will affect its stability.

--- QUESTION 0546 ---
According to Section 8 of the Intact Stability Code, how often should a lightweight survey be carried out on passenger ships?
--- ANSWER ---
At intervals not exceeding five years to verify any changes in lightship displacement and longitudinal center of gravity.

--- QUESTION 0547 ---
How can free surface effects be minimized in tanks prior to an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
By emptying tanks completely and limiting the number of slack tanks.

--- QUESTION 0548 ---
What precautions must be taken regarding pressed-up tanks before an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be completely full with no voids, and personnel should be aware of entrapped air in the tank.

--- QUESTION 0549 ---
What factors regarding mooring arrangements should be considered prior to an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
Consider depth of water, wind, and current. The ship should be moored in a quiet area away from the wash of passing vessels and must be free to list to allow readings to be taken.

--- QUESTION 0550 ---
What are the marking and certification requirements for test weights used in an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
Weights must be marked with an identification number and its weight, and weight certification should be presented.

--- QUESTION 0551 ---
What is the minimum recommended length or deflection capability for the pendulum used in an Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
The pendulum should be long enough to give a measured deflection to each side of the upright of at least 15 cm.

--- QUESTION 0552 ---
What tasks are included in the initial walk-through and survey phase of conducting an Inclining Test?
--- ANSWER ---
Considering weather conditions, ensuring suspended items are secured on the vessel, and checking all plans and equipment to be used.

--- QUESTION 0553 ---
What is the purpose of taking freeboard and draught readings during the Inclining Test?
--- ANSWER ---
To determine the displacement of the ship at the time of the inclining test.

--- QUESTION 0554 ---
What checks and actions must be taken during the third phase when inclining the vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
Check that the vessel is floating freely, check the pendulum and record the length, mark the initial position of the weights on the deck, check communication, and check that all personnel are in place.

--- QUESTION 0555 ---
How is a plumb line and batten setup used to measure deflection when the ship is listed during the Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
[IMAGE: page-13.jpg]

A plumb line is suspended and attached to a point O. When the ship is upright, it hangs down and crosses a batten at point X. When the ship is listed, the plumb line is deflected to point Y along the batten.
"""
print(output)
```

What checks and actions must be taken during the third phase when inclining the vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
Check that the vessel is floating freely, check the pendulum and record the length, mark the initial position of the weights on the deck, check communication, and check that all personnel are in place.

--- QUESTION 0555 ---
How is a plumb line and batten setup used to measure deflection when the ship is listed during the Inclining Experiment?
--- ANSWER ---
[IMAGE: page-13.jpg]

A plumb line is suspended and attached to a point O. When the ship is upright, it hangs down and crosses a batten at point X. When the ship is listed, the plumb line is deflected to point Y along the batten.

--- QUESTION 0556 ---
Which IMO Resolution outlines the official guidelines for voyage planning?
--- ANSWER ---
IMO Resolution A.893(21)

--- QUESTION 0557 ---
What are the four essential stages of voyage planning that must be completed prior to a vessel's departure?
--- ANSWER ---
Appraisal, Planning, Execution, and Monitoring

--- QUESTION 0558 ---
Which mandatory international codes and conventions govern voyage planning alongside standard industry guidelines like the Bridge Procedures Guide (BPG)?
--- ANSWER ---
SOLAS Regulation V/34, the ISM Code, and STCW Chapter VIII

--- QUESTION 0559 ---
What primary policy statement details the Master's specific, long-term operational framework and structural principles for the bridge watchkeeping team?
--- ANSWER ---
Master's Standing Orders

--- QUESTION 0560 ---
What document contains specific, time-defined procedural instructions written daily by the Master for the Officer of the Watch (OOW)?
--- ANSWER ---
Master's Night Orders

--- QUESTION 0561 ---
Under the Intertanko Guide to Safe Navigation, when is a vessel exempt from performing explicit under-keel clearance (UKC) calculations?
--- ANSWER ---
In the open deep sea when the water depth is greater than or equal to twice the vessel's static draft.

--- QUESTION 0562 ---
What standard baseline percentage of a vessel's static draft is typically used to calculate required UKC in coastal or shallow water passages?
--- ANSWER ---
20% of the static draft

--- QUESTION 0563 ---
Name at least four distinct environmental and physical factors that must be evaluated when calculating a vessel's true Under-Keel Clearance (UKC).
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: squat effects, water density, height of swell/tide, nature and stability of the seabed, reduced depths over pipelines, vessel size and handling characteristics, draft observation reliability, increase in draft due to heeling/rolling, or accuracy of hydrographic data.

--- QUESTION 0564 ---
What three specific ECDIS items and safety parameters should be established during a comprehensive review of an uploaded route provided by a voyage optimization service?
--- ANSWER ---
CATZOC information, Cross Track Distance (XTD), and safety contour/depth parameters.

--- QUESTION 0565 ---
What are the four fundamental criteria mandated by SOLAS Regulation V/34 that an intended voyage plan route must satisfy?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Take into account relevant ships' routeing systems.
2. Ensure sufficient sea room for safe passage.
3. Anticipate all known navigational hazards and adverse weather.
4. Take into account marine environmental protection measures and minimize environmental damage.

--- QUESTION 0566 ---
What operational considerations should be addressed during a passage planning meeting regarding environmental protection constraints?
--- ANSWER ---
Emission control areas (ECAs) and ballast water management/exchange requirements.

--- QUESTION 0567 ---
Name at least four operational factors that directly dictate the bridge manning levels throughout different stages of a passage plan.
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: vessel's location, traffic density, weather conditions and visibility, shipboard operations, engine room watch condition, vessel's structural condition, security considerations, experience of the Officer of the Watch (OOW), and crew fatigue.

--- QUESTION 0568 ---
What bridge team interaction methods help junior officers clarify navigation choices and develop professional situational awareness without compromising hierarchy?
--- ANSWER ---
Encouraging bridge team members to seek clarification on actions to better understand decision-making, and practicing the technique of "thinking aloud" to openly discuss actions.

--- QUESTION 0569 ---
What primary nautical publications and materials provide mariners with regional limits of expected ice areas and icing conditions?
--- ANSWER ---
The Mariners Handbook, Ocean Routeing Charts, Admiralty Sailing Directions, and the Intact Stability Code.

--- QUESTION 0570 ---
What three distinct categories of ships are defined under the Polar Code based on their capability to handle first-year ice or open water conditions?
--- ANSWER ---
