How can a ship's crew verify that a specific wire rope is recorded in the vessel's lifting register?
--- ANSWER ---
The wire rope will have a tag, tally, or disc attached near the winch or on the end connection ferrule, it will be marked with a surveyor's or manufacturer's stamp, and its serial number and markings will match the certificate in the Lifting Register.

--- QUESTION 1520 ---
What reduction in diameter requires a wire rope to be discarded and replaced?
--- ANSWER ---
A wire rope must be discarded and replaced when it has lost 10% of its diameter.

--- QUESTION 1521 ---
According to the limits of weardown and corrosion table, what is the maximum permissible reduction at any point on structural members?
--- ANSWER ---
A maximum reduction of 10% based on the material thickness.

--- QUESTION 1522 ---
What are the wear limits for loose gear diameters and pins in holes before they must be condemned?
--- ANSWER ---
Loose gear is limited to a maximum reduction of 5% on any diameter, and a pin in a hole is limited to a maximum reduction of 2% on its diameter.

--- QUESTION 1523 ---
What percentage of broken or corroded wires in a given length dictates that a wire rope must be discarded under the Lloyd's Register criteria?
--- ANSWER ---
The wire rope must be discarded if 5% of the wires are broken or corroded within any length of 10 rope diameters.

--- QUESTION 1524 ---
What are the three core planning and management requirements an employer must ensure for every lifting operation?
--- ANSWER ---
Every lifting operation must be properly planned, appropriately supervised, and carried out in a safe manner.

--- QUESTION 1525 ---
Ship's lifting gear cannot be used unless it has been load tested within what preceding time frame?
--- ANSWER ---
It must have been tested within the preceding 5 years.

--- QUESTION 1526 ---
Following a test of lifting gear, within how many days must the official certificate be issued?
--- ANSWER ---
The certificate must be issued within 28 days of the test being conducted.

--- QUESTION 1527 ---
How long must lifting gear test certificates or reports be kept in a safe place on board the ship?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be kept on board for at least 2 years.

--- QUESTION 1528 ---
How frequently must lifting equipment that is exposed to conditions causing deterioration be inspected if it is used to lift persons?
--- ANSWER ---
It must be inspected by a competent person at least every 6 months.

--- QUESTION 1529 ---
How frequently must lifting equipment that is NOT used to lift persons be inspected under normal conditions?
--- ANSWER ---
It must be inspected by a competent person at least every 12 months.

--- QUESTION 1530 ---
If an inspection reveals a deficiency in lifting equipment involving an imminent risk of serious personal injury, who must be notified?
--- ANSWER ---
The deficiency must be reported to the MCA (or relevant enforcing authority, such as the UK Health and Safety Executive).

--- QUESTION 1531 ---
What certification steps are mandatory for new lifting appliances installed on or after January 1, 2026, before entering service?
--- ANSWER ---
They must undergo plan appraisal and material verification, inspection and testing during fabrication, verification of component certification (including loose gear), and load testing and thorough examination once installed on board.

--- QUESTION 1532 ---
At which survey will surveyors verify the compliance, marking, and certification of existing lifting appliances under the updated January 2026 SOLAS requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
At the first Cargo Ship Safety Construction Renewal Survey or Passenger Ship Safety Survey conducted after January 1, 2026.

--- QUESTION 1533 ---
What specific information must be permanently and clearly marked on all loose gear under the updated SOLAS requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
All loose gear must be permanently marked with its unique identification (serial number), its Safe Working Load (SWL), and any additional marks required for safe use.

--- QUESTION 1534 ---
How do the LOLER regulations interact with the PUWER regulations regarding lifting equipment?
--- ANSWER ---
LOLER applies over and above the general requirements of PUWER to deal with specific hazards and risks associated with lifting equipment and lifting operations.

--- QUESTION 1535 ---
What types of equipment are explicitly exempted from PUWER regulations because they are subject to other specific merchant shipping regulations?
--- ANSWER ---
Any safety equipment or apparatus provided in compliance with the International Convention for the Safety of Life at Sea (SOLAS) requirements.

--- QUESTION 1536 ---
Where safety of work equipment depends on installation conditions, when must a competent person inspect the equipment?
--- ANSWER ---
The equipment must be inspected after initial installation, or after reassembly at a new site or location, and before putting it into service for the first time.

--- QUESTION 1537 ---
According to the table of emergency drills and their frequency, how often must a fire drill be carried out on a non-passenger vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
Within 24 hours of leaving port if more than 25% of the crew have not taken part in the previous month.

--- QUESTION 1538 ---
For a Passenger Vessel Class I, when must fire drills and abandon ship drills be carried out?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be held prior to or immediately upon departure.

--- QUESTION 1539 ---
What is the mandated frequency for carrying out a man overboard drill?
--- ANSWER ---
At regular intervals.

--- QUESTION 1540 ---
How often must a fast rescue craft (FRC) be launched and tested if one is carried on board?
--- ANSWER ---
Monthly.

--- QUESTION 1541 ---
What is the required frequency for conducting dangerous (enclosed) space drills?
--- ANSWER ---
Two monthly (every two months).

--- QUESTION 1542 ---
How often must damage control drills be conducted on passenger ships of Class I, II, II(A) and EU classes A to D?
--- ANSWER ---
Three monthly (every three months).

--- QUESTION 1543 ---
Who is authorized to conduct weekly and monthly inspections of life-saving appliances?
--- ANSWER ---
An authorized service provider or shipboard personnel under the direction of a senior ship's officer.

--- QUESTION 1544 ---
Who must sign the reports and checklists for weekly and monthly life-saving appliance inspections?
--- ANSWER ---
The company's representative or the ship's master.

--- QUESTION 1545 ---
Who can conduct the Annual Thorough Inspection and Operational Test of life-saving appliances?
--- ANSWER ---
Certified personnel of either the manufacturer or an authorized service provider. They may also be a ship operator provided they are authorized.

--- QUESTION 1546 ---
What additional condition must be met during a Five-year Thorough Inspection of survival craft and rescue boats compared to an annual test?
--- ANSWER ---
It must be conducted with a load equal to 1.1 times the total weight of the survival craft or rescue boat and its full complement of persons and equipment.

--- QUESTION 1547 ---
List at least five items of a lifeboat, rescue boat, or fast rescue boat structure that must be thoroughly examined during the annual thorough examination (excluding the release gear).
--- ANSWER ---
Any five of the following: condition of the boat structure (including fixed and loose equipment), engine and propulsion system, sprinkler system, air supply system, maneuvering system, power supply system, bailing system, fender/skate arrangements, or rescue boat righting system.

--- QUESTION 1548 ---
What five items must be thoroughly examined on the release gear for lifeboats and rescue boats during the annual examination?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Operation of devices for activation of release gear
2. Excessive free play (tolerances)
3. Hydrostatic interlock system (where fitted)
4. Control and release cables
5. Hook fastening

--- QUESTION 1549 ---
During the annual operational test of a davit-launched lifeboat's on-load release system, how should the boat be positioned in the water?
--- ANSWER ---
Only partially in the water, such that the mass of the boat remains substantially supported by the falls and the hydrostatic interlock system (where fitted) is not triggered.

--- QUESTION 1550 ---
What three things must be confirmed before operating the on-load release gear during its operational test?
--- ANSWER ---

1. The lifeboat is as close as possible to the water surface.
2. The engine is started.
3. All crew are in their seats with their seatbelts fastened.

--- QUESTION 1551 ---
What danger does operating the on-load release handle present if there is insufficient confirmation of safety?
--- ANSWER ---
It may result in death or serious injury due to dropping the lifeboat into the water from a height.

--- QUESTION 1552 ---
What is the detailed 7-step resetting procedure for the on-load release gear of a lifeboat?
--- ANSWER ---

1. One person each positioned at the fore and aft hooks lifts up the hooks.
2. Swing down the reset levers simultaneously.
3. Confirm that the hooks are reset.
4. Insert the safety pin.
5. Fit the suspension links in the hooks.
6. Hoist the lifeboat just clear of the water.
7. Check and confirm the complete reset before resuming hoisting.

--- QUESTION 1553 ---
What operational status must the lifeboat be in to conduct an off-load release system operational test?
--- ANSWER ---
The lifeboat must be fully waterborne.

--- QUESTION 1554 ---
If the hooks of a lifeboat cannot be released by the off-load release operation even though the boat is waterborne, what alternative procedure can be utilized?
--- ANSWER ---
The on-load release procedure.

--- QUESTION 1555 ---
What is the first step in conducting the operational test of a free-fall lifeboat release function without actually launching the boat?
--- ANSWER ---
Engage the arrangements for the test without launching the lifeboat, as specified in the manufacturer's operating instructions.

--- QUESTION 1556 ---
During the operational test of a free-fall lifeboat release function, what must be done if a back-up release mechanism is applicable?
--- ANSWER ---
The test procedures must be repeated using the back-up release mechanism.

--- QUESTION 1557 ---
What two steps are required to test the release hook of a davit-launched liferaft automatic release function?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Manually release the hook with a load of 150 kg on the hook.
2. Automatically release the hook with a dummy weight on the hook when it is lowered to the ground.

--- QUESTION 1558 ---
What specific aspects must be checked when inspecting a davit or other launching structure?
--- ANSWER ---
Corrosion, misalignments, deformation, and excessive free play.

--- QUESTION 1559 ---
What specific damage should you look for when checking wires and sheaves of launching appliances?
--- ANSWER ---
Kinks and corrosion.

--- QUESTION 1560 ---
What components are inspected as part of the winch checks for launching appliances?
--- ANSWER ---
The brake assembly (holding brake and centrifugal brake), brake pads (replaced when necessary), winch foundation, and (if applicable) the remote control system and power supply system.

--- QUESTION 1561 ---
What does a "SOLAS MES" mean?
--- ANSWER ---
A Marine Evacuation System which complies with the requirements of SOLAS Chapter III and the Life-Saving Appliances (LSA) Code, and has Marine Equipment Directive (MED) approval.

--- QUESTION 1562 ---
How often must a SOLAS Marine Evacuation System (MES) be deployed for training/testing purposes?
--- ANSWER ---
Every 6 years.

--- QUESTION 1563 ---
Who must the company contact to ensure they are present to witness a SOLAS MES deployment?
--- ANSWER ---
The MCA (Maritime and Coastguard Agency) / RO (Recognized Organisation).

--- QUESTION 1564 ---
What defines a successful deployment of an MES unit?
--- ANSWER ---
A successful evacuation within the time frame required by SOLAS or the HSC Code, as applicable.

--- QUESTION 1565 ---
According to MGN 558, what constitutes an unsuccessful deployment of an MES system regarding manufacturer interaction during pre-deployment checks?
--- ANSWER ---
If unplanned actions were required by the equipment manufacturer or the accredited representative, without which deployment would not have occurred.

--- QUESTION 1566 ---
If full MES capacity cannot embark the rafts from the ship within the permitted SOLAS time frame, how is the deployment classified?
--- ANSWER ---
It is classified as an unsuccessful deployment.

--- QUESTION 1567 ---
According to COSWP Chapter 15, what two specific physical characteristics define an enclosed space?
--- ANSWER ---
An enclosed space has limited openings for entry and exit, and it has inadequate ventilation.

--- QUESTION 1568 ---
Is an enclosed space explicitly designed for continuous worker occupancy?
--- ANSWER ---
No, an enclosed space is not designed for continuous worker occupancy.

--- QUESTION 1569 ---
Why might a shipboard compartment become a dangerous enclosed space only intermittently?
--- ANSWER ---
It can become dangerous intermittently due to the specific type of cargo carried or the nature of the work being undertaken inside it, such as spray painting.

--- QUESTION 1570 ---
What updated terminology does MGN 659 use instead of "dangerous space" to help distinguish these areas from other hazards onboard?
--- ANSWER ---
Enclosed space

--- QUESTION 1571 ---
If there is any doubt about whether a shipboard space is enclosed, how should it be treated until tested?
--- ANSWER ---
It should be treated as an enclosed space.

--- QUESTION 1572 ---
Under MGN 659, what specific document should be created and kept under review during risk assessments to manage potential enclosed spaces?
--- ANSWER ---
An inventory or register of enclosed spaces.

--- QUESTION 1573 ---
What must the master of a ship ensure regarding all entrances to unattended enclosed spaces?
--- ANSWER ---
The master must ensure they are either kept closed or otherwise secured against entry, except when entry is necessary.

--- QUESTION 1574 ---
What three broad atmospheric conditions can make an enclosed space hazardous?
--- ANSWER ---

1. The atmosphere may be oxygen-deficient.
2. The space may be oxygen-enriched.
3. The atmosphere may be flammable and/or toxic.

--- QUESTION 1575 ---
How can the natural process of rusting cause an oxygen deficiency in a sealed steel tank?
--- ANSWER ---
Rusting consumes oxygen as it chemically combines with the steel structure of the tank.

--- QUESTION 1576 ---
Name three types of oxygen-absorbing cargoes that can cause oxygen depletion in a closed space.
--- ANSWER ---
Grain, oilseeds, scrap metal, and wood.

--- QUESTION 1577 ---
What hazardous gas may be produced in a cathodically protected cargo tank that is used for ballast?
--- ANSWER ---
Hydrogen

--- QUESTION 1578 ---
Name two types of fixed fire-extinguishing or preventing media that can intentionally displace oxygen in a vessel's tanks or inter-barrier spaces.
--- ANSWER ---
Carbon dioxide (CO2) and inert gas.

--- QUESTION 1579 ---
Which specific inert gas is commonly used to purge shipboard tanks, leading to an oxygen-deficient atmosphere?
--- ANSWER ---
Nitrogen

--- QUESTION 1580 ---
What are two primary technical causes of an oxygen-enriched atmosphere within an enclosed space?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Leaks from damaged or poorly maintained hoses, pipes, valves, or poor connections.
2. Using an excess of oxygen in welding or flame cutting processes.

--- QUESTION 1581 ---
According to MGN 659, what three publications or codes should be consulted to check if a cargo has the potential to decay or release toxic chemicals?
--- ANSWER ---
The IMSBC Code (for bulk carriers), Safety Data Sheets (for tankers), and the IMDG Code.

--- QUESTION 1582 ---
What seven general precautions or procedural steps must be taken before a potentially dangerous space is entered?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Assessment by a competent person.
2. Identification of potential hazards.
3. Preparation, venting, and securing of the space.
4. Testing of the atmosphere.
5. Implementation of a permit-to-work system.
6. Agreement on preparation and entry procedures.
7. Putting emergency procedures in place.

--- QUESTION 1583 ---
Apart from atmospheric hazards, what physical environmental factors should be considered before entering an enclosed space under MGN 659?
--- ANSWER ---
Low lighting, reduced visibility, trip hazards, low ceilings, and narrow walkways.

--- QUESTION 1584 ---
According to ISGOTT, what must be done to a tank with high hydrocarbon gas and inert gas content before air can be safely introduced for entry?
--- ANSWER ---
The tank must be purged with inert gas to reduce the hydrocarbon (HC) content below the Lower Flammable Limit (LFL).

--- QUESTION 1585 ---
What specific measurement tool is referenced for measuring hydrocarbon gas concentrations during the gas-freeing process?
--- ANSWER ---
A Tankscope

--- QUESTION 1586 ---
Why must you exercise extreme caution and potentially wear breathing apparatus when initially unbolting or opening a tank top or manhole access?
--- ANSWER ---
Opening the access can cause the atmosphere in the space where you are standing to change and become hazardous, and even putting your head near the opening can have fatal consequences.

--- QUESTION 1587 ---
What is the minimum steady percentage of oxygen by volume required to permit entry into an enclosed space without breathing apparatus?
--- ANSWER ---
20.8% (or a steady reading of 20% by volume depending on the specific standard applied; 20.8% is the ideal normal atmospheric level).

--- QUESTION 1588 ---
Prior to enclosed space entry, what is the maximum acceptable percentage of the Lower Flammable Limit (LFL) on a combustible gas indicator?
--- ANSWER ---
Not more than 1% of the Lower Flammable Limit (LFL).

--- QUESTION 1589 ---
Atmospheric entry conditions state that toxic vapours and gases must not exceed what percentage of their occupational exposure limit (OEL) or workplace exposure limit (WEL)?
--- ANSWER ---
Not more than 50% of the occupational or workplace exposure limit.

--- QUESTION 1590 ---
What are the Short-Term Exposure Limit (STEL) and the time-weighted maximum exposure limit for Carbon Monoxide (CO) according to COSWP?
--- ANSWER ---
The STEL is 100 ppm (maximum 15 minutes) and the time-weighted average is 20 ppm (maximum 8 hours).

--- QUESTION 1591 ---
What are the Short-Term Exposure Limit (STEL) and the time-weighted maximum exposure limit for Hydrogen Sulphide (H2S) according to COSWP?
--- ANSWER ---
The STEL is 10 ppm (maximum 15 minutes) and the time-weighted average is 5 ppm (maximum 8 hours).

--- QUESTION 1592 ---
List four specific design or functional capabilities that standard shipboard atmosphere testing equipment must feature.
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: Capable of remote sampling, performs a self-test, shows which gas is being measured, readable in all lighting, alarms at danger levels, operates in all temperatures, protected from dust/water, minimum 10-hour battery life, and intrinsically safe.

--- QUESTION 1593 ---
Can a standard personal atmosphere monitor be used to perform pre-entry testing of an enclosed space?
--- ANSWER ---
No, personal monitoring equipment is designed for personal use only unless it has a certified and approved additional capability to conduct remote readings, such as a pumped capability.

--- QUESTION 1594 ---
Under what two circumstances should a Self-Contained Breathing Apparatus (SCBA) be used to enter an unsafe or untested enclosed space?
--- ANSWER ---
Only in cases of an emergency (such as rescue) or for the express purpose of essential atmosphere testing.

--- QUESTION 1595 ---
At what locations or layouts within an enclosed space should atmospheric testing be conducted to account for gases of varying densities?
--- ANSWER ---
Testing must be carried out at different height levels.

--- QUESTION 1596 ---
If remote testing of a double bottom tank is impossible prior to entry, what must be assumed about the atmosphere?
--- ANSWER ---
It must be assumed that the atmosphere is hazardous until proven otherwise, requiring full emergency entry protocols.

--- QUESTION 1597 ---
If deep-sea vessels cannot access shore-based facilities frequently, where must the calibration equipment for portable atmosphere testing devices be kept?
--- ANSWER ---
The calibration equipment must be carried on board the ship.

--- QUESTION 1598 ---
What is the regulatory definition of a "Competent Person" in the context of enclosed space entry?
--- ANSWER ---
A person with sufficient theoretical knowledge and practical experience to make an informed assessment of the likelihood of a dangerous atmosphere being present or subsequently arising in the space.

--- QUESTION 1599 ---
What is the regulatory definition of a "Responsible Person" regarding enclosed spaces?
--- ANSWER ---
A person authorized to permit entry into an enclosed space who has sufficient knowledge of the procedures to be established and complied with on board to ensure the space is safe for entry.

--- QUESTION 1600 ---
What are the primary responsibilities of an enclosed space "Attendant"?
--- ANSWER ---
The attendant maintains a watch over those entering the space, maintains continuous communications with those inside, and initiates emergency procedures if an incident occurs.

--- QUESTION 1601 ---
How often must an emergency drill for rescue from an enclosed space be carried out on all SOLAS ships?
--- ANSWER ---
Every two months.

--- QUESTION 1602 ---
What five components must be included as a minimum requirement in an enclosed space rescue drill?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Checking and use of personal protective equipment required for entry.
2. Checking and use of communication equipment and procedures.
3. Checking and use of instruments for measuring the atmosphere.
4. Checking and use of rescue equipment and procedures.
5. Instructions in first aid and resuscitation techniques.

--- QUESTION 1603 ---
Is it acceptable to conduct an enclosed space rescue drill inside an actual, live enclosed space onboard?
--- ANSWER ---
No, rescue drills should not be conducted in a real enclosed space onboard.

--- QUESTION 1604 ---
What emergency equipment must be physically positioned at the entrance to an enclosed space during an entry execution?
--- ANSWER ---
Breathing apparatus, lifelines, rescue harnesses, torches or a lamp, and a means of hoisting an incapacitated person.

--- QUESTION 1605 ---
What critical document must be completed, signed, and posted at the entrance before anyone enters an enclosed space?
--- ANSWER ---
The Permit to Work (PTW).

--- QUESTION 1606 ---
What mandatory brief meeting should occur immediately before entry to discuss duties, safety parameters, and the scope of work?
--- ANSWER ---
A tool-box talk.

--- QUESTION 1607 ---
What action should be taken by the team inside an enclosed space if unforeseen difficulties or new hazards develop?
--- ANSWER ---
They must stop the job and immediately evacuate the space.

--- QUESTION 1608 ---
What specific guidance document publishes approved workplace exposure limits (WELs) in ppm and mg/m3 for legal reference under UK COSHH regulations?
--- ANSWER ---
The Health and Safety Executive (HSE) Guidance Note EH40 (EH40/2005).

--- QUESTION 1609 ---
What does the acronym WWD stand for in voyage charter parties?
--- ANSWER ---
Weather Working Day

--- QUESTION 1610 ---
Under voyage charter party terms, how is laytime affected by weather when the term "WWD" (Weather Working Day) applies?
--- ANSWER ---
Time counts only when weather conditions do not stop cargo operations (e.g., rain preventing the loading of a grain cargo).

--- QUESTION 1611 ---
What does the shipping acronym SHEX stand for regarding laytime calculations?
--- ANSWER ---
Sundays and Holidays Excluded

--- QUESTION 1612 ---
What does the shipping acronym FHINC stand for regarding laytime calculations?
--- ANSWER ---
Fridays and Holidays Included

--- QUESTION 1613 ---
What does the acronym WIBON stand for on a charter party?
--- ANSWER ---
Whether In Berth Or Not

--- QUESTION 1614 ---
In laytime terms, what does the abbreviation "uu" mean when added to an exclusion like SHEX?
--- ANSWER ---
Unless Used (meaning the time will count toward laytime if cargo operations are actually carried out during the excluded days).

--- QUESTION 1615 ---
What is the penalty money paid by a charterer to a shipowner called if the charterer fails to complete loading or discharging within the allowed laytime?
--- ANSWER ---
Demurrage

--- QUESTION 1616 ---
How is demurrage typically calculated and billed?
--- ANSWER ---
On a US Dollar (USD) per day basis or pro-rata for part of a day.

--- QUESTION 1617 ---
What is the money payable by a shipowner to a charterer called if loading or discharging operations are completed in less than the allowed laytime?
--- ANSWER ---
Despatch (or Dispatch) money

--- QUESTION 1618 ---
What is the standard monetary rate for despatch money relative to demurrage?
--- ANSWER ---
It is usually half the amount of the demurrage rate.

--- QUESTION 1619 ---
What legal document is tendered by the Master when a vessel has arrived and is in all respects ready to load or discharge cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
Notice of Readiness (NOR)

--- QUESTION 1620 ---
Does the submission of a Notice of Readiness (NOR) represent the exact start of laytime?
--- ANSWER ---
No, it is not the start of laytime per se, but it specifies when the laytime will officially commence.

--- QUESTION 1621 ---
What acronym combines the allowed laydays and the ultimate cancelling day of a charter party?
--- ANSWER ---
Laycan

--- QUESTION 1622 ---
What critical maritime document records all operational events during a vessel's stay in port from arrival to final departure, and is used to determine demurrage or despatch?
--- ANSWER ---
Statement of Facts (SOF)

--- QUESTION 1623 ---
Which international maritime organization recommends the standard layout for a Statement of Facts?
--- ANSWER ---
BIMCO (The Baltic and International Maritime Council)

--- QUESTION 1624 ---
Who must sign the Statement of Facts at the port?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master or Chief Mate, and the Charterer's representative (who may be from the terminal or an agent).

--- QUESTION 1625 ---
What document is issued by the ship as immediate evidence of the quantity and condition of cargo when it is received on board?
--- ANSWER ---
Mate's Receipt

--- QUESTION 1626 ---
How is a Mate's Receipt utilized in relation to the Bill of Lading?
--- ANSWER ---
It is referred to when checking the accuracy of the cargo description in the Bills of Lading before they are signed. It is returned to the ship in exchange for the signed Bill of Lading.

--- QUESTION 1627 ---
What action should be taken on the Mate's Receipt if steel coils are loaded and appear rusty?
--- ANSWER ---
A comment/remark concerning the rusty condition must be noted on the receipt.

--- QUESTION 1628 ---
What are the three primary functions of a Bill of Lading?
--- ANSWER ---

1. It is evidence of the Contract of Carriage.
2. It is a document of title.
3. It is a receipt for the goods loaded on board.

--- QUESTION 1629 ---
What BIMCO standard form is widely used as a generic Bill of Lading for shipments contractually linked to charter parties?
--- ANSWER ---
CONGENBILL

--- QUESTION 1630 ---
Name at least six standard pieces of information you would expect to find on a completed Bill of Lading.
--- ANSWER ---
Any six of the following: Name and details of the Shipper, Name and details of the Consignee, Name of the vessel, Bill of Lading Number, Port of Loading, Port of Discharge, Shipper's description of goods, Apparent condition of the goods, Gross weight of the cargo, Amount of freight paid/payable, Number of original Bills of Lading, Date and Place of issuance, Signature of the Master.

--- QUESTION 1631 ---
Why is it vital for a Master to accurately describe the apparent order and condition of cargo on a Bill of Lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Inaccurate descriptions can expose the carrier to commercial claims, result in the loss of P&I cover, lose the right to limit liability, lose the right of indemnity from the charterer, or lead to criminal prosecution.

--- QUESTION 1632 ---
Is a Master under a legal obligation to sign a Bill of Lading presented to them that inaccurately describes the cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
No, the Master is under no obligation to sign bills of lading that inaccurately describe the cargo.

--- QUESTION 1633 ---
What landmark 1851 legal case originally ruled that a carrier was not liable to receivers if a master signed a B/L for goods that were never actually shipped on board?
--- ANSWER ---
Grant v Norway (1851)

--- QUESTION 1634 ---
Which piece of legislation overturned the decision in Grant v Norway (1851) to make a signed B/L conclusive evidence of shipment against the carrier in the hands of a lawful holder?
--- ANSWER ---
COGSA 92 (Carriage of Goods by Sea Act 1992)

--- QUESTION 1635 ---
What is a Sea Waybill, and how does its legal function differ from a Bill of Lading?
--- ANSWER ---
A Sea Waybill acts as a cargo receipt and evidence of the contract of carriage, but unlike a Bill of Lading, it is not a Document of Title. Goods are delivered to the named consignee upon proof of identity without requiring presentation of the physical document.

--- QUESTION 1636 ---
How many "Original" copies of a Bill of Lading are traditionally issued?
--- ANSWER ---
Three

--- QUESTION 1637 ---
Does the Master normally retain an original or a copy of the Bill of Lading, and what must be marked on it?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master retains a copy, which must be clearly marked or stamped "Copy, Non-negotiable".

--- QUESTION 1638 ---
What operational steps should a Master take if cargo damage is detected prior to loading?
--- ANSWER ---
Take steps to prevent the damaged cargo from being loaded, inform the Shipper, clause the bill of lading (unless replacement goods are provided), issue a Letter of Protest, collect/preserve evidence, and seek assistance from the local P&I correspondent.

--- QUESTION 1639 ---
Can a Master formally reject damaged cargo solely on their own authority without company consultation?
--- ANSWER ---
No, formal rejection should not be given without the Company's approval, unless the charter party explicitly dictates that non-conforming cargo must be rejected.

--- QUESTION 1640 ---
What notation is typically included on Container Bills of Lading because a Master cannot verify the internal contents or condition of a container?
--- ANSWER ---
"Shippers load stow and count" and "said to contain".

--- QUESTION 1641 ---
What does "clausing" a Bill of Lading mean, and where should this remark be placed?
--- ANSWER ---
Clausing means writing a factual remark representing the Master's assessment of the true condition or specifications of the goods. It must be written or typed directly on the face of the Bill of Lading.

--- QUESTION 1642 ---
What action must a Master take at the discharge port if three different people present original Bills of Lading simultaneously?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master may take reasonable time to investigate, must immediately contact the Company, Charterer, and local P&I club correspondent, and verify the legal holder by tracing the endorsements on the back of the B/L.

--- QUESTION 1643 ---
What is a Letter of Indemnity (LOI) in shipping?
--- ANSWER ---
A promise issued by one party to indemnify the recipient against defined financial or legal liabilities that may arise out of a specific request.

--- QUESTION 1644 ---
Why is it legally dangerous to accept a Letter of Indemnity from a shipper in exchange for issuing an unclaused ("clean") Bill of Lading for damaged cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
Such LOIs are not legally binding, offer the company no real protection if the shipper breaks their promise, and may implicate the Master in a fraud investigation.

--- QUESTION 1645 ---
What is the difference between a Letter of Protest and Noting Protest?
--- ANSWER ---
A Letter of Protest is a written communication recording operational dissatisfaction or holding another party responsible for a specific issue. Noting Protest is a formal declaration under oath by the Master before a notary stating that circumstances beyond control may have caused cargo or ship damage.

--- QUESTION 1646 ---
Why should a Master reserve the right to "extend" a protest at the time of noting protest?
--- ANSWER ---
Because it is impossible to know the full extent of hidden ship or cargo damage immediately, and an Extended Note of Protest allows additions once the full facts come to light.

--- QUESTION 1647 ---
Under standard charter party types, how are operating and voyage costs allocated under a Bareboat Charter?
--- ANSWER ---
Both operating costs and voyage costs are fully paid by the Charterer (including insurance, though the Owner may maintain separate default insurance).

--- QUESTION 1648 ---
Under standard charter party types, how are operating and voyage costs allocated under a Time Charter?
--- ANSWER ---
Operating costs are paid by the Shipowner, and voyage costs are paid by the Charterer.

--- QUESTION 1649 ---
Under standard charter party types, how are operating and voyage costs allocated under a Voyage Charter?
--- ANSWER ---
Both operating costs and voyage costs are paid by the Shipowner (though the charterer normally covers loading and discharging costs under FIOS terms).

--- QUESTION 1650 ---
Under a standard Time Charter, which party bears the risk of time/fuel losses caused by bad weather encountered while at sea?
--- ANSWER ---
The Charterer

--- QUESTION 1651 ---
Under a standard Time Charter, which party bears the risk of time lost due to a cargo stoppage caused by a failure of the ship?
--- ANSWER ---
The Owner

--- QUESTION 1652 ---
What does it mean if a Time Charter Party contains terms requiring the Master to "sign Bills of Lading as presented"?
--- ANSWER ---
It means the Master must sign the presented bills even if they are clean when they technically should be claused. The charter party will include an indemnity clause protecting the Owner against liabilities resulting from this.

--- QUESTION 1653 ---
What standard international conventions are typically incorporated via the General Paramount Clause in a CONGENBILL to regulate outbound shipments?
--- ANSWER ---
The Hague Rules (1924) or the Hague-Visby Rules (1968), along with the SDR Protocol (1979).

--- QUESTION 1654 ---
Which maritime set of rules is typically specified on a standard Bill of Lading to govern how General Average is adjusted and settled?
--- ANSWER ---
The York-Antwerp Rules 1994 (typically settled in London unless otherwise agreed).

--- QUESTION 1655 ---
Which BIMCO specialized charter party form is designed for ocean towage on a daily hire basis?
--- ANSWER ---
TOWHIRE 2021

--- QUESTION 1656 ---
Which BIMCO specialized charter party form is designed for ocean towage on a lump sum basis?
--- ANSWER ---
TOWCON 2021

--- QUESTION 1657 ---
What term is used when the laytime allowed for loading and discharging is counted together as a single operation?
--- ANSWER ---
Reversible laytime

--- QUESTION 1658 ---
Under a Voyage Charter Party, who bears the risk for weather delays that occur while the ship is at sea?
--- ANSWER ---
The shipowner

--- QUESTION 1659 ---
Under a Voyage Charter Party, who bears the risk for a fuel price increase?
--- ANSWER ---
The shipowner

--- QUESTION 1660 ---
What is the specific definition of laytime in a voyage charter party?
--- ANSWER ---
The amount of time allowed for the loading and unloading of cargo.

--- QUESTION 1661 ---
What does the term "laydays" refer to in a voyage charter party?
--- ANSWER ---
The period of specified days during which the shipowner must present the vessel to the charterer at the agreed place for loading.

--- QUESTION 1662 ---
What is the "Cancelling Date" in the context of a Voyage Charter Party?
--- ANSWER ---
The specific date by which the ship must have arrived at the load port and tendered its Notice of Readiness.

--- QUESTION 1663 ---
What legal form of damages does demurrage represent when a charterer fails to complete loading or discharging within the allowed time?
--- ANSWER ---
Liquidated damages

--- QUESTION 1664 ---
What is the meaning and practical effect of the maritime phrase "Once on demurrage always on demurrage"?
--- ANSWER ---
It means that once the allowed laytime has expired and the ship goes on demurrage, any standard exceptions to the loading/discharging time no longer apply.

--- QUESTION 1665 ---
What is dispatch (despatch) and how is its monetary rate typically calculated relative to demurrage?
--- ANSWER ---
Dispatch is an amount paid by the shipowner to the charterer for completing loading and/or discharging in less than the allowed laytime. Its rate is usually half the amount of the demurrage rate.

--- QUESTION 1666 ---
Which built-in receiver methodology provides an indication of GNSS fix integrity to the user, sometimes utilizing a red, amber, and green traffic light system?
--- ANSWER ---
Receiver Autonomous Integrity Monitor (RAIM)

--- QUESTION 1667 ---
According to MGN 379 Amendment 1, what is the minimum number of satellites normally required per fix for RAIM to correctly determine fix accuracy and identify position line anomalies?
--- ANSWER ---
At least 5 satellites

--- QUESTION 1668 ---
What warning light color is displayed by RAIM during periods of poor satellite geometry (higher DOP) to indicate that the fix accuracy is diluted based on the cut of position lines?
--- ANSWER ---
Amber

--- QUESTION 1669 ---
Which code contains the procedures for conducting an assessment of acceptability for loading solid bulk cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
Section 4 of the IMSBC Code (International Maritime Solid Bulk Cargoes Code)

--- QUESTION 1670 ---
When carrying a solid bulk cargo by sea, what is the official name designation by which it must be identified in the transport documentation?
--- ANSWER ---
Bulk Cargo Shipping Name (BCSN)

--- QUESTION 1671 ---
A dangerous cargo shift caused by liquefaction may occur when the moisture content of Group A or Group A and B solid bulk cargoes exceeds what specific limit?
--- ANSWER ---
The Transportable Moisture Limit (TML)

--- QUESTION 1672 ---
If a solid bulk cargo is not listed in the IMSBC Code, who must provide the master with a certificate stating the carriage requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
The competent authority

--- QUESTION 1673 ---
What specific document must a vessel hold to legally carry cargoes classified as dangerous goods in solid bulk form?
--- ANSWER ---
A Document of Compliance for the Carriage of Dangerous Goods

--- QUESTION 1674 ---
What evidence must be retained on board for 3 years to demonstrate compliance with MARPOL Annex VI fuel sulfur content requirements to a Port State Control inspector?
--- ANSWER ---
Bunker Delivery Note (BDN)

--- QUESTION 1675 ---
Under MARPOL Annex VI fuel sulfur regulation compliance, what is the required retention period for representative fuel samples on board?
--- ANSWER ---
12 months

--- QUESTION 1676 ---
Every ship of what gross tonnage and above is required to carry a Garbage Management Plan and a Garbage Record Book under MARPOL Annex V?
--- ANSWER ---
100 Gross Tonnage (GT) and above

--- QUESTION 1677 ---
According to MARPOL Annex V, what is the minimum number of certified persons carried on board that triggers the requirement for a Garbage Management Plan and a Garbage Record Book?
--- ANSWER ---
15 or more persons

--- QUESTION 1678 ---
For how long must a vessel's Garbage Record Book be preserved from the date of the last entry?
--- ANSWER ---
At least two years

--- QUESTION 1679 ---
Every ship of what minimum overall length is required to display placards notifying the crew and passengers of MARPOL Annex V garbage discharge requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
12 meters or more in length overall

--- QUESTION 1680 ---
During a loadline survey, what document lists the original Conditions of Assignment and should be checked against the back of the loadline certificate?
--- ANSWER ---
The Annex

--- QUESTION 1681 ---
What is the primary physical inspection purpose of a loadline survey with respect to structures below and above the freeboard deck?
--- ANSWER ---
To check the watertight integrity below the freeboard deck and the weathertight integrity above it.

--- QUESTION 1682 ---
If a vessel heels over unexpectedly and no external listing moments (such as cargo shift) are found, what dangerous stability condition should be assumed?
--- ANSWER ---
An angle of loll

--- QUESTION 1683 ---
What immediate navigational action should be taken if a vessel experiences an unexpected angle of loll, in order to prevent it from rolling violently to the opposite side?
--- ANSWER ---
Alter course to put the ship's head into the predominant waves.

--- QUESTION 1684 ---
According to the Intact Stability Code Section 3.6.7, what are two critical loading restriction values or limits that must be included in the ship's stability booklet?
--- ANSWER ---
Maximum KG or minimum GM

--- QUESTION 1685 ---
What error occurs in an echo sounder due to the separation distance between a vessel's dual transducers where the pulse follows an angled actual path rather than a vertical depth?
--- ANSWER ---
Pythagoras error

--- QUESTION 1686 ---
How do increases in water salinity and water temperature affect the velocity of the sound pulse emitted by an echo sounder?
--- ANSWER ---
They increase the speed of sound in water, which causes the recorded depth to appear shallower.

--- QUESTION 1687 ---
What alternative operating mode is used on ECDIS when an Electronic Navigational Chart (ENC) is unavailable for a leg of a voyage, requiring the vessel to carry an up-to-date portfolio of paper charts?
--- ANSWER ---
Raster Chart Display System (RCDS) mode

--- QUESTION 1688 ---
What operational status is an Safety Management Certificate (SMC) subjected to if a vessel has been in cold lay-up for a period exceeding 3 months?
--- ANSWER ---
The Safety Management Certificate (SMC) is invalidated (requires an interim audit).

--- QUESTION 1689 ---
If a vessel is in hot lay-up for a period between 3 to 12 months, what is the required administrative action regarding its Safety Management Certificate (SMC)?
--- ANSWER ---
The Safety Management Certificate (SMC) must be revalidated, and an additional audit must be performed.

--- QUESTION 1690 ---
What specific document must both the incoming and outgoing Masters sign to formally log the change of command under flag requirements?
--- ANSWER ---
The Official Log Book (OLB)

--- QUESTION 1691 ---
Why does GNSS (particularly GPS) accuracy degrade when a vessel is navigating in high latitudes greater than 55 degrees?
--- ANSWER ---
Due to the lower altitude of the satellite orbits relative to the observer at high latitudes.

--- QUESTION 1692 ---
When planning a mid-winter passage from the UK to Boston in February, west of which meridian and north of which parallel should a mariner specifically monitor ice warnings for icebergs?
--- ANSWER ---
West of 40°W and north of 40°N

--- QUESTION 1693 ---
What type of steady, strong winds dominate the weather route during a passage from Singapore to Mumbai in the month of August?
--- ANSWER ---
Southwest (SW) monsoon winds

--- QUESTION 1694 ---
According to the IMCA Guidelines for Lifting Operations, what must a lifting plan define regarding the physical gear before it is put into use for the lift?
--- ANSWER ---
Pre-use checks to be carried out

--- QUESTION 1695 ---
Which two codes or frameworks guide the environmental and operational calculations required when conducting a risk assessment for a heavy lift operation?
--- ANSWER ---
The Intact-Stability Code and the vessel's Safety Management System (SMS)

--- QUESTION 1696 ---
According to the York-Antwerp Rules, what maritime law principle states that all stakeholders in a sea venture proportionately share losses resulting from a voluntary sacrifice of part of the ship or cargo to save the whole in an emergency?
--- ANSWER ---
General Average

--- QUESTION 1697 ---
Within how many seconds must an emergency generator be capable of starting automatically?
--- ANSWER ---
Within 45 seconds

--- QUESTION 1698 ---
Where must the emergency generator be located on a vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
Above the uppermost continuous deck and readily accessible from the open deck.

--- QUESTION 1699 ---
What is the minimum required duration for emergency generator power provision on cargo ships and passenger ships respectively?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum of 18 hours on cargo ships and a minimum of 36 hours on passenger ships.

--- QUESTION 1700 ---
According to SOLAS Chapter II-2 Regulation 13, how many means of escape are required from each machinery space of category A for ships of more than 1000 gross tonnage (gt)?
--- ANSWER ---
Two means of escape

--- QUESTION 1701 ---
Emergency Escape Breathing Devices (EEBDs) located in machinery spaces must provide a minimum air supply of how many minutes?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum of 10 minutes

--- QUESTION 1702 ---
What are the three immediate initial actions on the bridge to be taken by the Chief Officer in the event of a grounding?
--- ANSWER ---
Immediately stop the engine, sound the general alarm, and make a PA announcement / call the Master.

--- QUESTION 1703 ---
In the event of a fire, what three primary safety and environmental priorities must the Master establish?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Safety of the crew
2. Safety of the ship
3. Protection of the environment

--- QUESTION 1704 ---
During an Engine Room fire emergency, what two immediate actions must the Engine Room Party take under the leadership of the Chief Engineer?
--- ANSWER ---
Stop the ventilation and cut off fuel supplies.

--- QUESTION 1705 ---
Which document must the Chief Officer consult to check damage stability and evaluate flooding model options using the stability computer after a grounding?
--- ANSWER ---
The damage stability booklet and plans.

--- QUESTION 1706 ---
List at least five essential documents or plans that should be prepared prior to a vessel entering dry dock.
--- ANSWER ---
Any five of the following: Docking plan, Repair list, Dock Work Schedule, General arrangement plan, Fire fighting plan, Shell expansion plan, Rigging plan, Stability information, Manhole, plugs and anodes plan, or Statutory and classification society certificates.

--- QUESTION 1707 ---
According to COSWP Chapter 34.4, what is the minimum number of boarding access/egress points that must be agreed upon between the vessel and the shipyard before arrival in a dry dock?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum of two means of access/egress between the vessel and the quayside.

--- QUESTION 1708 ---
What structural items must be replaced and verified as a critical check prior to flooding a dry dock?
--- ANSWER ---
The hull plugs (plugs replaced with evidence of replacement) and sea chest covers.

--- QUESTION 1709 ---
What are the four primary factors or categories identified in recovery planning considerations when rescuing a large number of people from the water?
--- ANSWER ---
Scale, Priority, Resources, and People.

--- QUESTION 1710 ---
When organizing a recovery plan for rescuing persons from the water, what priority should be given to people in the water compared to those in survival craft?
--- ANSWER ---
People in the water must take priority over those in survival craft.

--- QUESTION 1711 ---
According to the IMO Guide to Recovery Techniques, how should a ship adjust its heading relative to the wind when preparing for a recovery operation in rough seas?
--- ANSWER ---
Keep sufficiently off the wind to reduce the ship's roll and pitch and to create a lee.

--- QUESTION 1712 ---
If differential movement between a drifting ship and survivors is too violent for direct recovery, what intermediate option can be veered down to transfer survivors safely?
--- ANSWER ---
A liferaft (acting as an intermediate platform or fender).

--- QUESTION 1713 ---
What ship-specific data concerning the physical properties of the vessel must be included in the Emergency Towing Booklet (ETB) to comply with SOLAS?
--- ANSWER ---
Ship's name, call sign, IMO number, anchor details, cable and chain details, height of mooring deck(s) above base, draft range, and displacement range.

--- QUESTION 1714 ---
What are the three required locations on board where copies of the Emergency Towing Booklet (ETB) must be kept?
--- ANSWER ---
The Bridge, Forecastle space, and the Ship's office or Cargo Control room.

--- QUESTION 1715 ---
What core legal and financial principle forms the foundation of a Lloyd's Open Form (LOF) salvage agreement?
--- ANSWER ---
The principle of "no cure, no pay."

--- QUESTION 1716 ---
What two atmospheric air masses interact over the North Atlantic to cause frequent depressions, particularly during winter months?
--- ANSWER ---
Cold polar air (polar maritime) meeting warmer tropical air (tropical maritime).

--- QUESTION 1717 ---
What type of weather systems dominate the southern Indian Ocean during a transit from Perth to Cape Town in July?
--- ANSWER ---
Southern hemisphere trade winds and winter systems.

--- QUESTION 1718 ---
What specific statutory insurance coverage is required under the Maritime Labour Convention (MLC) regarding crew welfare in the event of abandonment or being held as hostages?
--- ANSWER ---
MLC Repatriation and Abandonment insurance (including provisions that crew must be paid if held as hostages).

--- QUESTION 1719 ---
What are the four distinct evolutionary stages in the formation and lifespan of a polar front depression?
--- ANSWER ---

1. Origin and infancy
2. Maturity
3. Occlusion
4. Death

--- QUESTION 1720 ---
What acronym represents the oil pollution prevention emergency response readiness mechanism that must be initiated as part of the Chief Officer's back-up party actions during a grounding?
--- ANSWER ---
SOPEP (Shipboard Oil Pollution Emergency Plan)

--- QUESTION 1721 ---
According to the provided text, what elements characterize a Safety Culture?
--- ANSWER ---
A Safety Culture is characterized by clearly defined expectations, good communications, clear leadership, good planning, risk awareness, accountability, and effective knowledge management.

--- QUESTION 1722 ---
Name three regular maintenance tasks that should be performed on a ship's lifting equipment.
--- ANSWER ---
Regular maintenance tasks include greasing bearings and moving parts, inspecting ropes, wires, and chains for wear, corrosion, or damage, replacing shackles, links, and rings when deformation is evident, examining structures for corrosion, cracks, and wear, inspecting hollow structures for trapped water, and carrying out functional tests of controls, brakes, stops, and safety devices.

--- QUESTION 1723 ---
According to MGN 332, what action should be taken regarding hollow structures like masts and gantries during routine maintenance?
--- ANSWER ---
They should be inspected for trapped water, drained, treated, and resealed as necessary.

--- QUESTION 1724 ---
Who is permitted to carry out routine inspections of a vessel's lifting gear?
--- ANSWER ---
A "competent person," who could be a member of the ship's crew with the appropriate knowledge and experience.

--- QUESTION 1725 ---
Who must provide the competent person required to carry out annual inspections and 5-yearly tests of lifting equipment?
--- ANSWER ---
A company specializing in such testing.

--- QUESTION 1726 ---
During the pre-testing checks for a 5-yearly lifeboat dynamic load test, who is designated as the Officer in Charge (OIC) for the safety of the operation?
--- ANSWER ---
The Chief Officer (C/O).

--- QUESTION 1727 ---
What stability precaution must be verified prior to loading a lifeboat for a dynamic load test?
--- ANSWER ---
Ensure there are no free surface effects which may cause a rise in the centre of gravity when loading the boat.

--- QUESTION 1728 ---
To what specific weight must a lifeboat be loaded during its system test?
--- ANSWER ---
A load of 1.1 times the weight of the fully loaded boat with persons, equipment, and stores.

--- QUESTION 1729 ---
What can be used to provide the load inside a lifeboat during testing, and what device monitors it?
--- ANSWER ---
Water bags can be used to provide the load, and a load cell monitors the weight.

--- QUESTION 1730 ---
What role does the attending surveyor play once the lifeboat test procedure is agreed upon and the boat is loaded?
--- ANSWER ---
The surveyor will now only observe.

--- QUESTION 1731 ---
Describe the procedure for a lifeboat winch brake dynamic test.
--- ANSWER ---
Lift the brake to fully enable the boat to reach maximum lowering speed, then apply the brake suddenly and hold the boat 1 meter above the water.

--- QUESTION 1732 ---
According to LSA Code requirements, a launching appliance winch brake must be capable of withstanding a static test with a proof load of not less than how many times the maximum working load?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 1.5 times the maximum working load.

--- QUESTION 1733 ---
What is the formula provided in the LSA Code to determine the minimum lowering speed (S) in metres per second for a fully loaded survival craft?
--- ANSWER ---
S = 0.4 + 0.02 * H (where H is the height in metres from the davit head to the waterline at the lightest sea-going condition).

--- QUESTION 1734 ---
When executing an On-Load Release test, where should the weight of the lifeboat be positioned?
--- ANSWER ---
The weight of the boat must still be on the falls, but the boat should be touching the water surface.

--- QUESTION 1735 ---
What safety protocol must be followed if the minimum operating crew enters a lifeboat to override the release mechanism during an On-Load Release test?
--- ANSWER ---
They must wear appropriate PPE, including safety helmets and life jackets, and must be sitting down when the boat is released.

--- QUESTION 1736 ---
What is the primary requirement regarding the position of the boat before operating the Off-Load Release gear?
--- ANSWER ---
Ensure the boat is fully waterborne.

--- QUESTION 1737 ---
Write out the algebraic formula used to find the total deviation of a magnetic compass using its coefficients.
--- ANSWER ---
Total Deviation = A + B sin Co + C cos Co + D sin 2Co + E cos 2Co

--- QUESTION 1738 ---
In what unit of measurement are the magnetic compass coefficients expressed?
--- ANSWER ---
They are measured in degrees (they are not forces).

--- QUESTION 1739 ---
Which ship safety certificate includes the "Details of navigational systems and equipment" (Form E) showing the actual provision of the standard magnetic compass?
--- ANSWER ---
Cargo Ship Safety Equipment Certificate.

--- QUESTION 1740 ---
If a vessel is loading steel coils that appear rusty, what document must reflect this condition before the Bills of Lading are signed?
--- ANSWER ---
The Mate's receipt.

--- QUESTION 1741 ---
What is a Letter of Protest?
--- ANSWER ---
A written communication recording dissatisfaction on the part of one party concerning any operational matter over which the recipient or other party has control, holding them responsible for any consequences.

--- QUESTION 1742 ---
What is Noting Protest?
--- ANSWER ---
A declaration under oath by the Master of the ship that circumstances beyond his control have or may have given rise to loss and/or damage to his ship or its cargo, or have caused him to take action which may render owners liable to legal action.

--- QUESTION 1743 ---
Why should a Master reserve the right to extend a protest at the time of noting protest?
--- ANSWER ---
Because it is impossible to ascertain the full extent of a loss or damage at the time of noting protest; an extended protest is made when relevant facts come to light.

--- QUESTION 1744 ---
Name four meteorological or oceanographic warning signs of an approaching Tropical Revolving Storm (TRS) that an OOW should monitor.
--- ANSWER ---
Signs include a barometer reading 3 mb below the mean daily/diurnal reading, a significant change in the direction and/or strength of the Trade Winds, a long low swell, spiralling cirrus cloud (followed by As, then Cb bands), squally heavy rain, an oppressive atmosphere, and ugly lurid skies.

--- QUESTION 1745 ---
What action should a vessel take if it is in the dangerous semi-circle of a TRS in the Northern Hemisphere?
--- ANSWER ---
Put the wind on the starboard bow and alter course to starboard as the wind veers.

--- QUESTION 1746 ---
What action should a vessel take if it is in the navigable semi-circle of a TRS in the Southern Hemisphere?
--- ANSWER ---
Put the wind on the starboard quarter and alter course to port as the wind backs.

--- QUESTION 1747 ---
Under the Merchant Shipping and Fishing Vessels (Health and Safety at Work) (Work at Height) Regulations 2010, what must be included in the planning of work at height?
--- ANSWER ---
The planning must include the selection of work equipment and planning for emergencies and rescue situations.

--- QUESTION 1748 ---
What is the fundamental organizational difference between a Safety Officer and a Safety Representative on board a vessel?
--- ANSWER ---
A Safety Officer holds an appointed role, whereas a Safety Representative holds an elected position.

--- QUESTION 1749 ---
On which seagoing ships is the Company required to appoint a safety officer?
--- ANSWER ---
On every seagoing ship on which five or more seafarers are employed.

--- QUESTION 1750 ---
Where must the Master record the appointment of a safety officer?
--- ANSWER ---
In the official logbook.

--- QUESTION 1751 ---
Name three core duties of an appointed shipboard Safety Officer.
--- ANSWER ---
Duties include ensuring safe work practices are followed per COSWP and the company's SMS; ensuring each joining crew member receives adequate familiarization; developing and maintaining high safety consciousness; ensuring safety inspections are carried out; advising on risk assessments; investigating onboard incidents; encouraging near-miss reporting; and maintaining records of accidents and dangerous occurrences.

--- QUESTION 1752 ---
What is the standard maximum interval, in months, allowed between consecutive inspections of the outside of a passenger ship's bottom?
--- ANSWER ---
36 months

--- QUESTION 1753 ---
For passenger ships that go to sea, how many inspections of the ship's bottom must take place out of the water within any five-year period?
--- ANSWER ---
Two inspections

--- QUESTION 1754 ---
What is the normal maximum age limit for a ship to be eligible for an In-Water Survey (IWS)?
--- ANSWER ---
15 years

--- QUESTION 1755 ---
Which two specific categories of vessels subject to the Enhanced Survey Programme (ESP) will not be considered for an In-Water Survey (IWS) if they are over 15 years of age?
--- ANSWER ---
Oil Tankers and Bulk Carriers

--- QUESTION 1756 ---
Under MGN 672, what does the abbreviation EDD stand for?
--- ANSWER ---
Extended Dry-Docking

--- QUESTION 1757 ---
Extended Dry-Docking (EDD) allows qualifying UK vessels to dry dock once in what specific time period, instead of twice in a five-year period?
--- ANSWER ---
A seven-and-a-half-year period (90 months)

--- QUESTION 1758 ---
For ships already in service, up to what age from the date of build can Extended Dry-Docking (EDD) be implemented?
--- ANSWER ---
Up to 10 years of age

--- QUESTION 1759 ---
Name three internationally trading vessel types of 500 GT or more that are eligible for consideration for Extended Dry-Docking (EDD).
--- ANSWER ---
Any three of the following: Dedicated container carriers, LNG Tankers, LPG carriers, Ro-Ro cargo ships, Refrigerated cargo ships, Dedicated wood chip carriers, Dedicated cement carriers, Livestock carriers, Deck cargo ships, General dry cargo ships of double-side skin construction, or MODUs.

--- QUESTION 1760 ---
According to MGN 672, why are passenger ships and Ro-Ro passenger ships excluded from Extended Dry-Docking (EDD) consideration?
--- ANSWER ---
All passenger ships, including Ro-Ro passenger ships, are explicitly listed as criteria that will not be considered for Extended Dry-Docking.

--- QUESTION 1761 ---
Vessels fitted with which type of propulsion system are ineligible for Extended Dry-Docking (EDD) consideration under MGN 672?
--- ANSWER ---
Propulsion thrusters

--- QUESTION 1762 ---
When a shipowner applies to the MCA for an In-Water Survey (IWS), at least how many weeks before the survey must the formal application be submitted?
--- ANSWER ---
Not less than 4 weeks

--- QUESTION 1763 ---
What specific confirmation must be included in the signed statement from the Master when applying for an In-Water Survey (IWS)?
--- ANSWER ---
Confirmation that the vessel has not knowingly suffered any grounding or contact below the waterline since the last inspection (or details of such contact confirming there is no known damage).

--- QUESTION 1764 ---
If an In-Water Survey (IWS) application is accepted, the owner must provide an undertaking to dry dock the vessel within what timeframe if it is deemed necessary following the survey?
--- ANSWER ---
Within 3 months (or sooner if considered necessary)

--- QUESTION 1765 ---
According to Chapter 34.5 of the Code of Safe Working Practices for Merchant Seafarers (COSWP), what must be documented at departure before a vessel undergoes sea trials?
--- ANSWER ---
The stability condition of the vessel

--- QUESTION 1766 ---
What safety actions must be performed for all persons on board before a vessel departs for sea trials?
--- ANSWER ---
A full safety muster, providing necessary safety instructions, and a demonstration of the emergency signals on board.

--- QUESTION 1767 ---
Who is responsible for conducting a risk assessment and implementing necessary control measures prior to a vessel departing for sea trials?
--- ANSWER ---
Shipyard representatives

--- QUESTION 1768 ---
Name four items that are typically included in a standard list of plans and information required for docking.
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: Docking plan, Repair list, Dry Dock Work Schedule, General arrangement plan, Fire fighting plan, Shell expansion plan, Rigging plan, Stability information, Manhole, plugs and anodes plan, or Statutory/classification society certificates.

--- QUESTION 1769 ---
List four specific structural or equipment positions that are identified on a vessel's docking plan.
--- ANSWER ---
Any four of the following: Position of bulkheads, Position of main structural members, Rise of floor, Position of plugs, Position of transducers, or Position of blocks.

--- QUESTION 1761 ---
With what type of condition is a ship typically expected to enter the dry dock?
--- ANSWER ---
A small trim by the stern

--- QUESTION 1771 ---
What notation is associated with the requirement for low-level alarms on the stern tube lubricating oil gravity tank?
--- ANSWER ---
SML notation

--- QUESTION 1772 ---
For tailshaft condition monitoring, what is the maximum interval permitted between lubricating oil analyses?
--- ANSWER ---
6 months

--- QUESTION 1773 ---
What minimum parameters must be included in a tailshaft lubricating oil analysis report?
--- ANSWER ---
Water content, chloride content, content of metal particles, and oil ageing (oxidation).

--- QUESTION 1774 ---
For shafts with keyed propellers, what specific areas must be examined by an efficient crack detection method at the 5-yearly period?
--- ANSWER ---
The after end of the cylindrical part of the shaft and one-third of the length of the taper from the big end.

--- QUESTION 1775 ---
How frequently should the hydraulic oil analysis report be verified for shafts with controllable pitch propellers mounted on a solid flange coupling?
--- ANSWER ---
Every 5 years (with the sample drawn not more than three months prior).

--- QUESTION 1776 ---
What specific measurement is recorded via poker gauge readings during a docking survey?
--- ANSWER ---
Stern bush clearance

--- QUESTION 1777 ---
Unless considered necessary by the surveyor, what is the maximum frequency that sea connections and overboard discharge valves/cocks need to be opened up?
--- ANSWER ---
Once in a special survey period

--- QUESTION 1778 ---
From which Special Survey number onwards is the gauging of sea chest plating mandatory?
--- ANSWER ---
Special Survey No. IV onwards

--- QUESTION 1779 ---
During a special survey, what additional action must be performed on the anchors and chain cables after they are ranged?
--- ANSWER ---
They must be examined and the chain cables must be gauged.

--- QUESTION 1780 ---
On hopper dredgers, which specific bottom structural items and accessories must be examined if the docking survey is part of a special survey?
--- ANSWER ---
Hopper bottom doors and accessories such as hinges, actuating rods, and hydraulic systems.

--- QUESTION 1781 ---
What action involves writing a remark on a bill of lading to represent the Master's factual findings based on their best assessment of the goods?
--- ANSWER ---
Clausing

--- QUESTION 1782 ---
If a bill of lading is inaccurate and a replacement cannot be provided due to time constraints, what must be done to the bill of lading before it is signed?
--- ANSWER ---
It must be claused.

--- QUESTION 1783 ---
What should be done to all originals and copies of an inaccurate bill of lading if an accurate replacement bill of lading is provided?
--- ANSWER ---
They should be destroyed.

--- QUESTION 1784 ---
Why should a Master resist pressure from a charterer or shipper to accept a letter of indemnity in exchange for issuing a clean bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Due to the considerable inherent risks involved in accepting letters of indemnity.

--- QUESTION 1785 ---
What are two examples of general printed clauses that are unlikely to protect a carrier from inaccurate descriptions of cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
"Shipper's load and count" and "particulars furnished by shipper"

--- QUESTION 1786 ---
Why is it advisable to always clause a bill of lading in the Master's own handwriting or in typed text?
--- ANSWER ---
Some jurisdictions do not recognize printed, standard form qualifications.

--- QUESTION 1787 ---
Where on a bill of lading should clausing ideally be made?
--- ANSWER ---
On the face of the bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1788 ---
If a Master must place clausing remarks on the reverse side of a bill of lading, what must be placed on the face of the document?
--- ANSWER ---
An appropriate reference (e.g., "see reverse of this bill of lading for Master's clausing").

--- QUESTION 1789 ---
According to wide legal acceptance, what is the hierarchy of authority between typed words, printed words, and handwritten words on a bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Handwritten words override typed words, which in turn override printed words.

--- QUESTION 1790 ---
What alternative document can a Master use for an attachment if an original bill of lading form is unavailable to handle extensive steel cargo clausing?
--- ANSWER ---
The carrier's headed stationery.

--- QUESTION 1791 ---
Who should the Master notify immediately if there is a disagreement regarding the clausing of a bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
The Company

--- QUESTION 1792 ---
If a Master lacks the time to check a bill of lading's accuracy or properly clause it, what alternative action is advised?
--- ANSWER ---
Leave the signature to an authorized agent with full and proper instructions.

--- QUESTION 1793 ---
Why is clausing a bill of lading with "order and condition unknown" considered unacceptable when cargo or packaging appears damaged?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master is under a legal obligation to inspect the order and condition of the goods.

--- QUESTION 1794 ---
When writing a cargo discrepancy remark, why should broad words like "some", "many", "a number of", or "several" be avoided?
--- ANSWER ---
They may make the clausing legally meaningless.

--- QUESTION 1795 ---
Under what specific international regulatory framework must a Master specify any cargo inaccuracy, grounds of suspicion, or lack of reasonable checking means?
--- ANSWER ---
The Hamburg Rules

--- QUESTION 1796 ---
If the shipper's figures exceed the ship's figures by an amount beyond normal variations and the shipper refuses to adjust them, what figures should the Master use or clause into the bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
The ship's figures.

--- QUESTION 1797 ---
What is the typical accuracy percentage range assigned to a properly executed draft survey?
--- ANSWER ---
0.5 per cent

--- QUESTION 1798 ---
For oil tankers, if the terminal/shipper figure exceeds the ship's figure by more than what general percentage should the bill of lading be claused?
--- ANSWER ---
0.25 per cent

--- QUESTION 1799 ---
What operational ratio compares a ship's volume figures against the shipper's volume figures over previous voyages to establish a customized normal difference?
--- ANSWER ---
Vessel Experience Factor (VEF)

--- QUESTION 1800 ---
If a Master determines that the actual quantity loaded exceeds the shipper's figure on the presented bill of lading, how should the bill be claused?
--- ANSWER ---
"Number quantity and weight unknown"

--- QUESTION 1801 ---
If a cargo covered by a bill of lading is loaded over a period of several days, what specific date is the correct date of shipment to insert?
--- ANSWER ---
The date of completion of loading.

--- QUESTION 1802 ---
Where specifically should a Master sign a bill of lading if there is no designated signature box?
--- ANSWER ---
At the bottom of the bill of lading face.

--- QUESTION 1803 ---
Why should a Master never sign or stamp a bill of lading next to the shipper's description of the cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
It may be legally interpreted as an acceptance of that description.

--- QUESTION 1804 ---
What must a Master do immediately if they feel they have signed a bill of lading under duress or threat of any kind?
--- ANSWER ---
Inform the Company as soon as it is safe to do so and make a detailed report.

--- QUESTION 1805 ---
What commercial procedure involves a Master issuing a signed but otherwise blank bill of lading form, predominantly in the tanker trade?
--- ANSWER ---
Early Departure Procedure (EDP)

--- QUESTION 1806 ---
What potential penalty faces a carrier if a Master carries cargo on deck without proper trade custom, statutory obligation, or shipper agreement?
--- ANSWER ---
The carrier may breach the contract, lose contractual exceptions, lose the right to limit liability, and lose P&I cover.

--- QUESTION 1807 ---
What commercial risk is a Master exposed to if they deliver cargo against an original bill of lading that was retained on board instead of through ordinary banking channels?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master may be deemed to have acted in bad faith if competing claims arise, holding the carrier fully liable for wrongful delivery.

--- QUESTION 1808 ---
What action regarding bulk oil parcels loaded on different dates or locations requires explicit Company approval before execution by the Master?
--- ANSWER ---
Commingling or blending cargo on board.

--- QUESTION 1809 ---
What must occur with the first single set of bills of lading before replacement "split bills of lading" can be safely issued?
--- ANSWER ---
All original bills of lading of the first set must be surrendered.

--- QUESTION 1810 ---
What must equals the amount in the original bill of lading when split bills of lading are issued?
--- ANSWER ---
The total cargo stated in the split bills of lading.

--- QUESTION 1811 ---
To whom should delivery be made once replacement bills of lading have been issued?
--- ANSWER ---
Only to the receiver named in the replacement bills of lading and for the amount stated in that particular bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1812 ---
Why might delivery orders be requested by cargo interests instead of split bills of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Because cargo interests do not require bills of lading that allow them to trade the cargo during the carriage.

--- QUESTION 1813 ---
What complication arises if cargo interests request a bulk cargo to be discharged at more than one port?
--- ANSWER ---
Complications arise if all the original bills of lading cannot be surrendered at the first discharge port for clausing or replaced with delivery orders or split bills of lading.

--- QUESTION 1814 ---
What is the risk if a bulk cargo is discharged at a first port without surrendering the original bills of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
An original bill of lading may be presented at the second discharge port for delivery of the full cargo.

--- QUESTION 1815 ---
What serious consequence can a carrier face if they discharge cargo at a port other than the destination named in the bill of lading without proper authorization?
--- ANSWER ---
The carrier could be deemed to have committed a deviation, which is a breach of the contract of carriage.

--- QUESTION 1816 ---
Why are letters of indemnity offered in exchange for a clean, ante-dated, or post-dated bill of lading generally unenforceable in most jurisdictions?
--- ANSWER ---
Courts view them as facilitating a fraud, as they involve a deliberate act of inserting information known to be incorrect into the bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1817 ---
Why will P&I cover be unavailable from Gard for claims arising from an ante-dated or post-dated bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Gard's Rules explicitly exclude cover for claims arising from the issue of an ante- or post-dated bill of lading, or one known to contain an incorrect description of the cargo, its quantity, or condition.

--- QUESTION 1818 ---
Under what circumstances may a court enforce a letter of indemnity regarding cargo condition or description?
--- ANSWER ---
If there is genuine doubt whether or not the cargo is damaged, or where the dispute as to clausing is so trivial that the means required to establish the true position are out of proportion to the likely outcome.

--- QUESTION 1819 ---
What requirement must be met for a letter of indemnity for delivering cargo without an original bill of lading to be acceptable, unless the Company is satisfied with the entity's solvency?
--- ANSWER ---
The letter of undertaking must be properly worded and counter-signed by a first-class bank.

--- QUESTION 1820 ---
What actions should a Master immediately take if offered a letter of indemnity to issue an incorrect bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
The Master should refuse the offer (despite threats or pressure) and immediately inform the Company to obtain instructions.

--- QUESTION 1821 ---
Under Article I of the Hague-Visby Rules, what documents must cover a contract of carriage for it to apply under these rules?
--- ANSWER ---
A bill of lading or any similar document of title.

--- QUESTION 1822 ---
According to the Hague-Visby Rules, what two items are explicitly excluded from the definition of "Goods"?
--- ANSWER ---
Live animals and cargo which by the contract of carriage is stated as being carried on deck and is so carried.

--- QUESTION 1823 ---
What period of time does the "Carriage of goods" cover under the Hague-Visby Rules?
--- ANSWER ---
The period from the time when the goods are loaded onto the ship until the time they are discharged from the ship.

--- QUESTION 1824 ---
Under Article III paragraph 3 of the Hague-Visby Rules, what three items must a bill of lading show on demand of the shipper?
--- ANSWER ---
(a) The leading marks necessary for identification, (b) either the number of packages/pieces or the quantity/weight, and (c) the apparent order and condition of the goods.

--- QUESTION 1825 ---
When is a carrier or master not bound to state marks, number, quantity, or weight in a Hague-Visby bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
When they have reasonable grounds for suspecting that the information does not accurately represent the goods received, or when they have had no reasonable means of checking.

--- QUESTION 1826 ---
Under the Hague-Visby Rules, when is proof to the contrary against a bill of lading inadmissible?
--- ANSWER ---
When the bill of lading has been transferred to a third party acting in good faith.

--- QUESTION 1827 ---
Under Article X of the Hague-Visby Rules, what must be true about the ports for the rules to apply to a bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
The carriage of goods must be between ports in two different States.

--- QUESTION 1828 ---
How do the Hamburg Rules define the "Consignee"?
--- ANSWER ---
The person entitled to take delivery of the goods.

--- QUESTION 1829 ---
How do the Hamburg Rules differ from the Hague-Visby Rules regarding the inclusion of live animals as "Goods"?
--- ANSWER ---
The Hamburg Rules explicitly include live animals in the definition of "Goods".

--- QUESTION 1830 ---
Under the Hamburg Rules, what forms of signature are permissible on a bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Handwriting, printed in facsimile, perforated, stamped, in symbols, or made by any other mechanical or electronic means (if not inconsistent with the law of the country of issuance).

--- QUESTION 1831 ---
According to Article 16 of the Hamburg Rules, what must a carrier do if they suspect that the shipper's description of the goods is inaccurate or if they have no means to check it?
--- ANSWER ---
The carrier must insert a reservation in the bill of lading specifying the inaccuracies, grounds of suspicion, or the absence of reasonable means of checking.

--- QUESTION 1832 ---
Under the Hamburg Rules, if a carrier fails to note the apparent condition of the goods on the bill of lading, what is the legal presumption?
--- ANSWER ---
The carrier is deemed to have noted that the goods were in apparent good condition.

--- QUESTION 1833 ---
What is the primary purpose of a steel pre-shipment cargo survey?
--- ANSWER ---
To establish the apparent condition of the steel cargo immediately before it is loaded onto the vessel.

--- QUESTION 1834 ---
What elements are examined during a pre-shipment vessel survey for a steel cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
The vessel's holds (for suitability) and the watertight integrity of the hatches, ventilators, sounding pipes, and accesses.

--- QUESTION 1835 ---
What are two primary functions of using dunnage when loading cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
To distribute the load from the cargo, prevent friction damage, prevent distortion of the cargo in the stow, and assist in cargo securing.

--- QUESTION 1836 ---
What brittle coating covers raw steel when it leaves the producer, which easily displaces to allow rusting to begin?
--- ANSWER ---
Mill scale.

--- QUESTION 1837 ---
Why is it vital to identify rust that forms in a salt-laden environment compared to a fresh-water environment?
--- ANSWER ---
Rust from a salt-laden environment can cause rapid deterioration and pitting of the steel surface, which considerably reduces its commercial value.

--- QUESTION 1838 ---
What chemical is commonly used by surveyors to test rust for salt content, and what visual reaction indicates a positive result?
--- ANSWER ---
Silver nitrate, which turns milky when exposed to chlorides.

--- QUESTION 1839 ---
How does the industry define "Pitted" rust on steel cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
Penetration of the surface with minor indentations which cannot be removed by wire brushing.

--- QUESTION 1840 ---
How is "Scale" rust described in steel cargo terminology?
--- ANSWER ---
Thick flaking rust.

--- QUESTION 1841 ---
If a product is galvanized, what specific visual changes should a surveyor comment on?
--- ANSWER ---
Whether the zinc coating is dull or affected by white oxidation.

--- QUESTION 1842 ---
