What documentation should not be claused when mechanical cargo damage is caused by stevedores during loading, since it does not strictly represent pre-shipment damage?
--- ANSWER ---
The bill of lading

--- QUESTION 1843 ---
What common term is used to describe mechanical cargo damage where the edges of the material have become warped or distorted?
--- ANSWER ---
Edges buckled

--- QUESTION 1844 ---
What specific phrasing is usually used in a Mate's receipt and bill of lading to describe the condition of the cargo at the time of loading if it is undamaged?
--- ANSWER ---
"In apparent good order and condition"

--- QUESTION 1845 ---
Why should general terms such as "some", "a few", and "a number of" be avoided when clausing bills of lading for damaged cargo?
--- ANSWER ---
Remarks must be as accurate as possible, and the affected cargo should be explicitly identified.

--- QUESTION 1846 ---
Who relies on the description of the cargo in the bill of lading if the cargo is sold while en route during the voyage?
--- ANSWER ---
The new owner of the cargo

--- QUESTION 1847 ---
What is the main duty of an independent surveyor attending at the discharge port during an outturn survey?
--- ANSWER ---
To examine the cargo upon discharge to check for damage.

--- QUESTION 1848 ---
According to the text, who is the author of the Gard Guidance on Bills of Lading excerpts provided from London Offshore Consultants?
--- ANSWER ---
Captain Peter Roberts

--- QUESTION 1849 ---
What recommended standard form version of a Letter of Indemnity is used when cargo is delivered at a port other than that stated in the bill of lading against the production of at least one original bill of lading, signed by the Requestor alone?
--- ANSWER ---
INT GROUP B

--- QUESTION 1850 ---
What recommended standard form version of a Letter of Indemnity is used for the delivery of cargo without production of the original bill of lading when a bank joins in the agreement?
--- ANSWER ---
INT GROUP AA

--- QUESTION 1851 ---
When a bank joins in a Letter of Indemnity, what is the recommended minimum fixed monetary limit it will generally insist upon?
--- ANSWER ---
A minimum of 200% of the sound market value of the cargo at the time of delivery.

--- QUESTION 1852 ---
Under standard forms INT GROUP A and AA, when does the liability of the Requestor terminate?
--- ANSWER ---
Upon the delivery of all original bills of lading to the shipowner.

--- QUESTION 1853 ---
What is the standard initial period of liability for a bank under an INT GROUP AA Letter of Indemnity?
--- ANSWER ---
Six years

--- QUESTION 1854 ---
For what duration can a bank's liability under an INT GROUP AA agreement be automatically renewed at the request of the shipowner?
--- ANSWER ---
Further periods of two years from time to time.

--- QUESTION 1855 ---
If a bank is unwilling to extend the Termination Date of its Letter of Indemnity, how must it discharge its liability?
--- ANSWER ---
By paying the shipowner the maximum sum payable under its indemnity (or such lesser sum as required).

--- QUESTION 1856 ---
What specific type of registry interference is mentioned that a Requestor is obliged to prevent or remove, such as to allow the sale of the ship?
--- ANSWER ---
A caveat entered on the ship's registry.

--- QUESTION 1857 ---
When a bank joins a Letter of Indemnity, what action will it generally refuse to do directly, opting instead to provide monetary funds so the shipowner can arrange it?
--- ANSWER ---
Provide bail or other security.

--- QUESTION 1858 ---
Under the special provisions for tankers, delivery of a bulk liquid or gas cargo to what three locations is deemed to be delivery to the requested party?
--- ANSWER ---
A terminal or facility, another ship, or a lighter or barge.

--- QUESTION 1859 ---
Which specific body's law governs and construes the standard form Letters of Indemnity shown in the text?
--- ANSWER ---
English law

--- QUESTION 1860 ---
Under standard Letter of Indemnity terms, how is the liability of multiple persons signing the indemnity described?
--- ANSWER ---
Joint and several

--- QUESTION 1861 ---
According to the Preface, what are the three historical core functions or roles evidenced by a bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
The carrier's receipt of the goods, the terms of the contract of carriage, and the right to possession of the goods.

--- QUESTION 1862 ---
Who is the Gard claims manager based in London who led the project and wrote the guidance?
--- ANSWER ---
Mark Russell

--- QUESTION 1863 ---
What website is provided by Gard where this publication is kept up to date?
--- ANSWER ---
www.gard.no

--- QUESTION 1864 ---
According to the text, what does "title" mean in the context of bills of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Title means the right to possession of the goods from the carrier, rather than the right to ownership.

--- QUESTION 1865 ---
What contract usually determines the actual ownership of the goods, as opposed to the right to possession from the carrier?
--- ANSWER ---
The sales contract.

--- QUESTION 1866 ---
Under the standard Letters of Indemnity (such as INT GROUP B), which country's law governs the contract and which court holds jurisdiction?
--- ANSWER ---
The indemnity is governed by English law, and the parties submit to the jurisdiction of the High Court of Justice of England.

--- QUESTION 1867 ---
What is the nature of the liability for individuals who sign the standard Letter of Indemnity under INT GROUP B?
--- ANSWER ---
The liability is joint and several.

--- QUESTION 1868 ---
When a bank joins a Letter of Indemnity under form INT GROUP BB, what is its liability strictly restricted to?
--- ANSWER ---
It is restricted to the payment of specified sums of money demanded and does not extend to providing bail or other security.

--- QUESTION 1869 ---
Unless extended or subject to ongoing legal proceedings, what is the standard termination date for a bank's liability under the INT GROUP BB indemnity?
--- ANSWER ---
Six years from the date of the Indemnity.

--- QUESTION 1870 ---
Under the bank's agreement in INT GROUP BB, for how long can the indemnity's termination date be extended at a single request?
--- ANSWER ---
For a period of two calendar years at a time.

--- QUESTION 1871 ---
If a bank is unwilling to extend the Termination Date when requested under INT GROUP BB, how must it discharge its liability?
--- ANSWER ---
By paying the maximum sum payable under the indemnity (or such lesser sum as required).

--- QUESTION 1872 ---
What is the primary difference between the INT GROUP B and INT GROUP C standard form Letters of Indemnity?
--- ANSWER ---
INT GROUP B requires the production of at least one original bill of lading for the substituted delivery, whereas INT GROUP C allows delivery without production of the original bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1873 ---
Under INT GROUP C, if the cargo is delivered to a bulk liquid or gas terminal, what is this delivery legally deemed to be?
--- ANSWER ---
It is deemed to be delivery to the party to whom the requestor asked delivery to be made.

--- QUESTION 1874 ---
If a ship Master is in doubt about any aspect of a bill of lading, who is the primary entity they are advised to contact for assistance?
--- ANSWER ---
The Company (assistance can also be sought from the local P&I correspondent).

--- QUESTION 1875 ---
What specific words are inserted in an order bill of lading to distinguish it from a document that names only a specific consignee?
--- ANSWER ---
The words "to order (or assigns)".

--- QUESTION 1876 ---
Why might a Master suspect fraud if original shipped bills of lading are found to exist when a received for shipment bill of lading is presented for delivery?
--- ANSWER ---
Because original received for shipment bills of lading would normally be stamped or surrendered and replaced once the goods are on board.

--- QUESTION 1877 ---
What are the most common negotiable bills of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Order bills of lading.

--- QUESTION 1878 ---
What wording is typically inserted in an order bill of lading instead of naming a consignee?
--- ANSWER ---
"Order" or "order (or assigns)."

--- QUESTION 1879 ---
Who may transfer an order bill of lading to another person?
--- ANSWER ---
The shipper or the named consignee.

--- QUESTION 1880 ---
How is an order bill of lading transferred?
--- ANSWER ---
By endorsing the bill of lading and handing it to another person.

--- QUESTION 1881 ---
What is an endorsement in blank?
--- ANSWER ---
The shipper or consignee signs their name on the bill of lading without naming a new holder, allowing it to be transferred to whoever receives it.

--- QUESTION 1882 ---
What is an endorsement in full?
--- ANSWER ---
An endorsement that names the person to whom the bill of lading is transferred in addition to the endorser's signature.

--- QUESTION 1883 ---
Who can receive a bill of lading endorsed in full?
--- ANSWER ---
Only the named endorsee.

--- QUESTION 1884 ---
What wording must be added if a named endorsee is to transfer the bill of lading further?
--- ANSWER ---
"To order" or "to order (or assigns)."

--- QUESTION 1885 ---
Can bearer bills of lading be negotiated?
--- ANSWER ---
Yes. They are negotiable documents.

--- QUESTION 1886 ---
How is a bearer bill of lading transferred?
--- ANSWER ---
Simply by handing it from one person to another, without endorsement.

--- QUESTION 1887 ---
What is typically shown instead of a named consignee on a bearer bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
The word "Bearer," or no named consignee.

--- QUESTION 1888 ---
How can an order bill of lading become a bearer bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
By being endorsed in blank by the person named in the order bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1889 ---
Why can negotiable bills of lading be traded several times during a voyage?
--- ANSWER ---
Because ownership of the goods can be transferred by transferring the bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1890 ---
Against what document should delivery normally be made when using a negotiable bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
An original bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1891 ---
What problem may arise if the original bill of lading cannot be presented?
--- ANSWER ---
Delivery of the cargo may be delayed because the bill has been lost or is delayed.

--- QUESTION 1892 ---
Which documents can help avoid problems caused by a missing original bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
Waybills and straight bills of lading, provided delivery against presentation is not expressly required.

--- QUESTION 1893 ---
Why are waybills and straight bills of lading not negotiable?
--- ANSWER ---
Because they identify both the shipper and the consignee.

--- QUESTION 1894 ---
When is the non-negotiable nature of a waybill or straight bill of lading generally not a problem?
--- ANSWER ---
When the cargo is traded between only one buyer and one seller.

--- QUESTION 1895 ---
How are non-negotiable bills of lading usually identified?
--- ANSWER ---
They are usually stamped or marked as non-negotiable.

--- QUESTION 1896 ---
Besides acting as a document of title, what other important function does a bill of lading perform?
--- ANSWER ---
It serves as evidence of the contract of carriage.

--- QUESTION 1897 ---
Does a bill of lading act as evidence of the contract of carriage whether it is negotiable or non-negotiable?
--- ANSWER ---
Yes.

--- QUESTION 1898 ---
Who may act as the carrier under a contract of carriage?
--- ANSWER ---
The owner, charterer, or freight forwarder.

--- QUESTION 1899 ---
Who normally enters into the contract of carriage with the shipper?
--- ANSWER ---
The carrier.

--- QUESTION 1900 ---
Who is the Master normally considered to be employed by?
--- ANSWER ---
The shipowner.

--- QUESTION 1901 ---
What does the Master's signature on a bill of lading usually constitute?
--- ANSWER ---
A contract with the shipowner.

--- QUESTION 1902 ---
What should the Master assume regarding bills of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
That they will either sign the bills of lading themselves or authorize another person to sign on their behalf.

--- QUESTION 1903 ---
Where are the terms of carriage usually found?
--- ANSWER ---
On the reverse side of the bill of lading.

--- QUESTION 1904 ---
Why is it important to properly incorporate the charterparty into the bill of lading?
--- ANSWER ---
To ensure the relevant charterparty terms become part of the contract of carriage.

--- QUESTION 1905 ---
What function does a bill of lading perform regarding loaded goods?
--- ANSWER ---
It serves as a receipt for the goods loaded.
